# Actual projection normals and complete-child gluing

JOINT UNVERIFIED. Worker max2, invocation78 after actual ended baseline77.
Project gd-exact-profile-20260926. Guidance91f76640c669d4da.
One allocated invocation, controller cap1, completed ceiling78/attempted79.
No mathematical programs, numerical or recurrence replay, formal calls,
fact_submit, consults, installations or subagents. Original target OPEN.
This is one joint mathematical candidate, not a collection of accepted facts.

## Verdict and exact scope

The requested universal U_d block inequality is NOT proved or refuted.
In particular the low-d, arbitrary-start H2 stress test is NOT closed.
There is no full triangle upper bound and no full-function W upper bound.

This round develops ONE mechanism, retaining projection-normal reserves.
It derives an exact identity with all physical and scaled auxiliary points
in one body, and displays the precise H2 central debt. It pays that debt
uniformly for all bodies and starts when d>=2/epsilon-2(A+B), and pays
all depths and every d>=1 on the entire initial unit ball of the centered
triangle, including starts outside its quadratic core. It also pays the
previous actual strict-switch H3 family using a new terminal-norm bound.

A tempting strengthened child law, the terminal shifted-norm bound, would
suffice for the desired U law. It has an explicit two-child gluing identity.
But this stronger law is FALSE for general compact bodies on the ACTUAL
original H3, and indeed at every parent depth at least3: one static
asymmetric interval and one legal complete two-cap trajectory refute it.
The same entire interval family satisfies the U law with ratio below3/4.
Thus this counterexample rejects the strengthening, not U itself.
It must not be advertised as a counterexample for the centered triangle.

No second directional storage is introduced. Failure of the stronger norm
law has not supplied a decisive failure of the requested U law itself.
The remaining task is an actual low-d H2 debt estimate, followed by a
uniform same-m all-depth estimate retaining the nonlocal normal coupling.
A signed telescope or a sufficient-condition reduction is not that estimate.

## 1. Definitions, normalization, and accepted scope

Fix ONE integer m>=1536. Set

 epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon, lambda=1+sqrt(2),
 c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,
 H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,b_j,H_j), b_j=1+a*c^j,
 S_j=sum H_j, n_j=2^j-1, D_m=1+2S_m.

All children inherit this same m and these same parameters. For the literal
H2 write A=1+a, B=1+beta, so H2=(A,B,A), S2=2A+B. The symbol A in this
H2 abbreviation is a physical step; below Cchild_j=1+S_j denotes a child mass.
It is NOT the original benchmark primitive capacity sequence.

For a nonempty compact convex subset D of a finite-dimensional Euclidean
space containing0, define

 P_D(x)=the Euclidean projection of x onto D,
 F_D(x)=max_(g in D)(<x,g>-||g||^2/2),
 U_d(x)=max_(g in D)(2<x,g>-d||g||^2), d>0,
 T_h(x)=x-h P_D(x).

For a finite nonnegative word h, T_hword is its actual chronological
composition. All uses of U with different d use this SAME body D.
The centered-triangle specialization is

 v1=(1,0), v2=(-1/2,sqrt(3)/2), v3=(-1/2,-sqrt(3)/2),
 D_triangle=conv{v1,v2,v3}.

The original W_n(h) is the supremum of
2(f(x_n)-f_star)/(L||x_0-x_star||^2) over all finite-dimensional convex
L-smooth functions with a minimizer, under x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i grad f(x_i)/L.
The original normalization is W=2R. All physical leaves and the actual
terminal are retained here. Even a universal upper bound within the centered
triangle family would not by itself be an upper bound for that full W.

Accepted931b9653ce0f7201 supplies B_(2^m-1)/D_m<47/50 and the CONDITIONAL
799/800 reduction if the full-function bound17/(16D_m) is established.
It does not supply that bound. Accepted1252b8823bb5a290 fixes the ordered
interpolation residual and the actual unqueried-terminal convention.
Only those stated accepted scopes are used; no unaccepted general-body
representation theorem or previous unverified proof is a premise below.
All new assertions are derived together in this document.

## 2. Projection facts, legal objectives, and true minimizers

For g=P_D(x), squared-distance minimization along each segment in D gives

 <x-g,z-g><=0 for every z in D.                         (2.1)

Conversely, expanding ||x-z||^2-||x-g||^2 proves sufficiency of (2.1).
Adding the inequalities at x and y gives
||P_D(x)-P_D(y)||^2<=<P_D(x)-P_D(y),x-y>, proving1-Lipschitzness.
Completing the square identifies g as the unique maximizer in F_D.
The two maximizing inequalities bound
F_D(x+t)-F_D(x)-<g,t> between0 and ||t||^2. Thus F_D is differentiable,
convex, finite, and1-smooth, with gradient P_D.

Its complete minimum set is the polar cone

 K_D={z:<z,g><=0 for every g in D},                     (2.2)

and its minimum is0. Membership in K_D makes every candidate in F_D
nonpositive, and g=0 attains0. If <z,g>>0 for some g in D, then t*g in D
for0<t<=1, and sufficiently small positive t gives positive objective.
This proves the converse. The closed convex set K_D contains0, so a closest
initial minimizer exists. We do not replace that distance by the origin norm.

For every d>0,

 U_d(x)=||x||^2/d-dist(x,dD)^2/d,
 U_d(x)<=||x-z_star||^2/d for EVERY z_star in K_D.      (2.3)

The first identity is completion of the square. For the second,
<z_star,g><=0 implies
2<x,g>-d||g||^2<=2<x-z_star,g>-d||g||^2
=||x-z_star||^2/d-d||g-(x-z_star)/d||^2.
Take the maximum. Consequently a proved U block law at j=m,d=1 would
have the true closest-distance consequence in this projection class.
This does not require a norm bound about the origin to be the true radius.

For actual queries and terminal put gi=P_D(xi), fi=F_D(xi). Then every
ordered original interpolation residual is exactly

 fi-fj+<gi,xj-xi>+||gi-gj||^2/2
   =<xj-gj,gi-gj><=0.                                (2.4)

The same formula holds when either sample is any minimizer, with gradient0
and value0. Actual Euclidean vectors supply a PSD Gram. Scaling the objective
by L and using steps h/L preserves every point and the W normalization.

## 3. Exact one-step identity with BOTH normal reserves retained

Consider one actual step xplus=x-h*g, g=P_D(x), with h>0 and d>=1.
Put

 Dmass=d+2h, p=P_D(x/Dmass), q=P_D(xplus/d),
 theta=h/(Dmass-1), z=theta*g+(1-theta)*q,
 e=q-g,
 Nphys=<x-g,g-q>,
 Naux=<x-Dmass*p,p-z>.

Both normal quantities are nonnegative by (2.1). The mixture z is in D;
indeed0<theta<=1/2. Define

 Bcoef(d,h)=h*((2-h)*(d+2h)-2)/(d+2h-1)^2.

There is the EXACT identity

 U_(d+2h)(x)-U_d(xplus)
   = Bcoef(d,h)||q-g||^2 +2theta*Nphys
     +Dmass||p-z||^2+2Naux.                          (3.1)

To prove it, evaluate 2<x,z>-Dmass||z||^2 and subtract the final maximum
2<x-hg,q>-d||q||^2. With x=g+(x-g), the coefficient of <g,e> cancels
because theta*(Dmass-1)=h. The remainder is
-2theta<x-g,e>+[d-Dmass*(1-theta)^2]||e||^2.
The square coefficient is Bcoef(d,h). Finally the difference between the
initial maximum at p and its value at z is
Dmass||p-z||^2+2<x-Dmass*p,p-z>. This proves (3.1), including boundaries.

For0<h<=3/2, Bcoef>=0 because d+2h>=1+2h and
(2-h)(1+2h)-2=h(3-2h)>=0. More generally h<2 is paid when
(d+2h)(2-h)>=2. At h>2 the coefficient is negative for every d>=1.
The physical normal and the auxiliary normal are distinct. The auxiliary
square is also essential data; it is not another projection-normal inequality.

For a complete word of N steps let

 di=d+2 sum_(t=i)^(N-1)h_t,
 xi+1=xi-hi*gi, gi=P_D(xi), pi=P_D(xi/di),
 thetai=hi/(di-1), zi=thetai*gi+(1-thetai)*p_(i+1).

Then the exact whole-word gap is the sum, over EVERY physical i, of

 hi*((2-hi)*di-2)/(di-1)^2 *||p_(i+1)-gi||^2
 +2thetai<xi-gi,gi-p_(i+1)>
 +di||pi-zi||^2+2<xi-di*pi,pi-zi>.                   (3.2)

All pi, gi and zi belong to the same D. The normals at arbitrary other
physical or auxiliary points remain available through (2.1).
Equation (3.2) is an exact interface, not proof that the signed sum is
nonnegative at every depth.

## 4. The literal H2 stress test and its outstanding debt

Take x1=x0-A*g0, x2=x1-B*g1, x3=x2-A*g2. Define

 d0=d+4A+2B, d1=d+2A+2B, d2=d+2A, d3=d,
 gi=P_D(xi) for i=0,1,2; pi=P_D(xi/di) for i=0,1,2,3.

These are the THREE actual queried projections and FOUR scaled auxiliary
projections. No one is substituted by an independent maximizer from another
run or another body. The final actual projection P_D(x3) is also legal and
will be used in Section5, but p3 need not equal it when d>1.

Both leaf coefficients in (3.2) are nonnegative, since A<sqrt(2)<3/2.
The middle coefficient is

 Cmid=B*(epsilon*d1-2)/(d1-1)^2.                       (4.1)

It follows that, for every compact convex D containing0 and EVERY x0,

 d>=max(1,2/epsilon-2(A+B))
   => U_d(T_H2 x0)<=U_(d+2S2)(x0).                   (4.2)

This is a fully paid complete-block statement, with an explicit domain in d.
It does NOT handle the small trailing masses most relevant to terminal blocks.

When epsilon*d1<2 the exact unpaid central debt is

 Debt=B*(2-epsilon*d1)/(d1-1)^2 *||p2-g1||^2.         (4.3)

The required payment is the sum of the two positive leaf squares and ALL
three physical normals, three auxiliary mixture squares, and three auxiliary
normals in (3.2). Their actual nonlocal correlations may improve that payment.
No lower bound on this sum sufficient for EVERY x0 and every remaining
1<=d<2/epsilon-2(A+B) is proved here. In particular, the presence of these
nonnegative terms does not permit assuming they exceed (4.3).

The single long-step core obstruction is also exact: sufficiently small
nonzero x with x and (1-h)x in D_triangle has
U_d(T_h x)=(h-1)^2||x||^2/d>||x||^2/(d+2h) for h>2.
Section8 pays all such small inputs over each COMPLETE actual block.

## 5. A sufficient terminal shifted-norm certificate

For an arbitrary nonnegative complete word with mass S, input x, endpoint y,
and actual gradients gi, write y=x-sum_i hi*gi. For d>=1 put
q=P_D(y/d) and

 zbar=[sum_i hi*gi+(d+S)q]/(d+2S) in D.              (5.1)

All coefficients are nonnegative and sum to1. Evaluating the initial U at
this one feasible point and completing the square yields

 U_(d+2S)(x)-U_d(y)
   >= [||x||^2-||y+S*q||^2]/(d+2S).                 (5.2)

For verification of the algebra, x-(d+2S)zbar=y-(d+S)q; expand both squares
and subtract2<y,q>-d||q||^2. The numerator is exactly
||x||^2-||y||^2-2S<y,q>-S^2||q||^2.
The omitted difference is the nonnegative initial maximization remainder.

For a fixed y the two quantities ||P_D(y/d)||^2 and <y,P_D(y/d)> are
nonincreasing in d>0. Indeed if d2>d1 and qi=P_D(y/di), maximizing at the
opposite qi gives

d1*(||q1||^2-||q2||^2)
 <=2<y,q1-q2><=d2*(||q1||^2-||q2||^2).

The difference of squared norms must be nonnegative; the scalar product
difference then is nonnegative as well. Hence, for S>=0,
||y+S P_D(y/d)||^2 is nonincreasing in d.

In particular the stronger complete-block statement

 R_H(x):=||x||^2-||y+S P_D(y)||^2>=0                 (5.3)

would prove the desired U law for every d>=1, with retained reserve
R_H(x)/(d+2S). This is a sufficient strengthening, not an equivalence.
For F_D, its right-hand square is
||y||^2+2S F_D(y)+S(S+1)||P_D(y)||^2.
This explains its relationship to a primitive/s-composability potential.
Section7 gives a legal actual counterexample to (5.3) for general bodies.

Even the one-step thresholds show that (5.3) is stronger. For h>0, set
q=P_D(x-hg), e=g-q, N0=<x-g,g-q>, N1=<x-hg-q,q-g>.
Both normals are nonnegative and direct expansion gives

 ||x||^2-||x-hg+hq||^2
   =(2-h^2)||e||^2+2(1+h)N0+2N1.                   (5.4)

For the derivation, N0+N1=h<g,e>-||e||^2 and
||x||^2-||x-he||^2=2h<x,e>-h^2||e||^2.
Thus (5.3) holds for h<=sqrt(2). But for a core point and h=3/2 its shifted
endpoint is (1-h^2)x, of larger norm than x, whereas (3.1) still proves the
U law. This comparison is a logical warning, not an altered physical step.

## 6. Exact gluing through TWO actual children and their bridge

Let each real child be the same H_j with mass S=S_j, and b=b_j. For one
actual parent run define

 x --H_j--> y --b--> z=y-b*p --H_j--> t,
 p=P_D(y), q=P_D(t), w=p-q,
 e=z-(t+S*q)=sum_(right child i) hi*(gi-q),
 Rleft=||x||^2-||y+S*p||^2,
 Rright=||z||^2-||t+S*q||^2,
 Rparent=||x||^2-||t+(2S+b)q||^2.

Keep the actual nonlocal normals

 Ny=<y-p,p-q>, Nt=<t-q,q-p>,
 NR=-<t-q,e>=sum_(right child i)hi*<t-q,q-gi>.

All three are nonnegative; in particular NR uses the endpoint normal
against EVERY real right-child gradient. They are not independent reserves
from arbitrarily chosen runs.

The exact parent identity is

 Rparent = Rleft +(2S+b+1)/(S+1)*Rright
   -(S+b)/(S+1)*||e||^2 -2(S+1)<e,w>
   +[2(S+1)^2-(S+b)^2]||w||^2
   +2(2S+b+1)Ny +2(S+1)Nt +2(S+b)/(S+1)*NR.        (6.1)

Proof by displayed substitutions. First expand the three squared norms:

 Rparent=Rleft+Rright+(S+b)*[
   2<y,p>-2<t,q>+(S-b)||p||^2-(3S+b)||q||^2].

Since t=y-bp-Sq-e, the bracket is
2<q,e>+2<y+Sp,w>-(S+b)||w||^2. Next,

 Rright=||e||^2+2(S+1)<q,e>-2NR,
 Ny+Nt=(S+b)<p,w>-(S+1)||w||^2+<e,w>.

Substituting these two equalities gives (6.1) term by term.

The formula contains a negative ||e||^2 coefficient and a mixed term.
Even replacing both child residues and all three normals by unspecified
nonnegative numbers does not yield the parent sign. The w-square
coefficient itself changes sign at

 b-1=(sqrt(2)-1)(S+1).                               (6.2)

This is a concrete failure of an attempted composition from scalar child
signs. It is not a proof that the actual U debt in Section4 is unpaid.
The next section makes the stronger-law failure actual, rather than merely
assigning values to an unconstrained signed quadratic.

## 7. Actual all-depth counterexample to the GENERAL-BODY strengthening

Let r_s=sqrt(2)-1=1/lambda. Put Cchild_j=1+S_j and k_j=b_j-1=a*c^j.
For j>=2 define the ONE static interval

 D_j=[-r_j,1], r_j=k_j/Cchild_j,
 x0=Cchild_j, parent word H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+k_j,H_j).        (7.1)

This is a compact convex body containing0. It may depend on m and j,
but is fixed for the entire actual run. Its F_D has unique minimizer0,
since the interval contains a neighborhood of0. The true initial radius
is exactly Cchild_j.

Every left-child query is at least1: after physical prefix mass s<=S_j
it is Cchild_j-s>=1. Thus all its actual gradients equal1 and its endpoint y=1.
The central step sends y to z=-k_j. During the right child, at physical
prefix mass s<=S_j the point is -k_j+s*r_j<=-r_j. Its actual gradient
therefore equals-r_j at EVERY right query. At the actual terminal

 t=-k_j+S_j*r_j=-r_j.

Boundary projections at y and t are unique; no query is omitted. In the
notation of Section6, Rleft=Rright=0, e=0, Ny=Nt=NR=0. The parent shifted
endpoint has magnitude (2S_j+b_j+1)r_j. Therefore (5.3) fails precisely when

 k_j*(2Cchild_j+k_j)>Cchild_j^2,
 equivalently k_j/Cchild_j>r_s.                           (7.2)

This condition holds for EVERY j>=2 with j+1<=m and EVERY m>=1536.
To prove it first use the exact mass solution

 Cchild_j=(1-v)2^j+v*c^j, v=a/(c-2), 0<v<1.             (7.3)

The recurrence Cchild_(j+1)=2Cchild_j+a*c^j and v(c-2)=a prove the formula by
substitution, with Cchild0=1. The inequality0<v<1 follows from
c(c-2)>lambda(lambda-2)=1>beta.
Thus k_j/Cchild_j=a/[v+(1-v)(2/c)^j] is increasing in j.
At j=2, Cchild2=5-epsilon+2r_s*(1-epsilon)/(1+epsilon) and
k2=beta*c=lambda*(1-epsilon^2). Since lambda=5r_s+2r_s^2,

 k2-r_s*Cchild2
   =epsilon*[r_s+4r_s^2/(1+epsilon)-lambda*epsilon]>0. (7.4)

For the final sign, epsilon<1/8, r_s>2/5 and lambda<5/2 imply
lambda*epsilon<5/16<2/5<r_s. This proves (7.2).
The counterexample begins with the literal H3 whose two children are H2.
It continues at every greater parent depth, not merely on a synthetic bridge.

This refutes the general-body terminal shifted-norm strengthening and any
universal proof of it using only valid projection normals. It does NOT
refute the requested U law. It also does NOT refute its centered-triangle
specialization: r_j<c-2<1/2, whereas the centered triangle's opposite
axial boundary lies at-1/2. The required negative cap is not a triangle cap.
This is a specific place where the triangle's fixed geometry matters.

### 7.1 The same complete two-cap family actually PASSES the U test

More generally keep x0=Cchild_j, y=1, z=-k_j and take a fixed interval[-r,1]
with0<r<=k_j/Cchild_j. The same complete-child guard proof gives terminal
 t=-k_j+S_j*r<=-r.
For any d>=1 its scaled initial maximizer is in the interval interior,
so U_(d+2Sparent)(x0)=Cchild_j^2/(d+2Sparent), where
Sparent=2S_j+1+k_j. Put S=S_j,k=k_j for this paragraph.
The exact terminal formula is

 U_d(t)=2kr-(2S+d)r^2 if r<=k/(S+d),
 U_d(t)=(k-Sr)^2/d if r>=k/(S+d),                    (7.5)

restricted throughout to0<r<=k/(S+1). The branches agree at their boundary.
The first is maximized at r=k/(2S+d), which is admissible because S+d>=1.
On the second branch k-Sr is positive and decreasing, so the maximum there
is at its left endpoint; its value is k^2*d/(S+d)^2<=k^2/(2S+d).
Consequently the exact family maximum is

 max_r U_d(t)=k^2/(2S+d),
 max_r U_d(t)/U_(d+2Sparent)(x0)
   =(k/(S+1))^2*(d+4S+2+2k)/(d+2S)<3/4.           (7.6)

For the last bound, (7.3) gives k/(S+1)<=c-2<1/2. Indeed
lambda<49/20 and epsilon<=1/6144 imply c<5/2. Since S>=S1>1,
k<(S+1)/2<=S. Thus d+4S+2+2k<=d+6S+2<=3(d+2S), using d>=1.
This comparison holds for EVERY j>=1 and every d>=1. For each selected r
there is one fixed body throughout the run; maximizing across that family
in (7.6) does not splice different projections into one history.
The failing strengthening is quantitatively separated from the U target.

The d=1 maximizing cap is the accepted single-cut asymmetric-Huber
construction in fact b98bb807d514e80d, rescaled from initial radius1 to
Cchild_j. That existing construction is not claimed as new. The additional
claims here concern the different endpoint-cap choice r=k/Cchild_j that refutes
(5.3), its exact same-m all-depth threshold, and the all-d U comparison
over the full legal cap interval. Their proofs above retain the actual
queries explicitly for this distinct diagnostic purpose.

## 8. Quantitative ALL-DEPTH payment on the centered triangle's unit ball

Here D=D_triangle. It contains the ball of radius1/2 and is contained in
the unit ball. For any h>=4/3 and any ||x||<=1,

 ||T_h(x)||<=(h-1)||x||.                             (8.1)

For h>=2 this follows for any D containing0 from
<x,P_D(x)>>=||P_D(x)||^2 and ||P_D(x)||<=||x||:
||T_h x||^2<=||x||^2+h(h-2)||P_D(x)||^2<=(h-1)^2||x||^2.
For4/3<=h<2, a core input gives equality. A vertex projection with ||x||<=1
can occur only at that vertex: the two vertex guards force its longitudinal
coordinate to be at least1. At that point equality holds as well.
For an edge projection write x=z*n+y*t where n is its outward unit normal,
t its unit tangent, z>=1/2, and P_D(x)=n/2+y*t. Here1/2<=z<=1.
With k=h-1, direct subtraction factors as

 ||T_h x||^2-k^2||x||^2
  =h(2-h)(z-1/2)*(z-h/[2(2-h)])<=0,

because h/[2(2-h)]>=1 when h>=4/3. This proves (8.1) on every cell.

All physical h_i are greater than4/3: a>1/3 follows from lambda<5/2 and
epsilon<1/11; the smallest step is1+a. Also a<r_s<1/2.
For a prefix of length ell>=1 in ANY same-m H_j, its exact product is

 p_ell=product_(t<ell)(h_t-1)=beta^ell*c^(-sbit(ell))<=a, (8.2)

where sbit(ell) is the number of1s in the binary expansion of ell.
The ruler step has h_t-1=beta*c^(nu2(t+1)-1), where nu2(k) is the exponent
of2 dividing the positive integer k. Counting multiples of powers of2 gives
sum_(k=1)^ell nu2(k)=ell-sbit(ell), proving (8.2) symbolically.
No trajectory or recurrence has been executed to obtain this identity.

Starting with ANY ||x0||<=1, apply (8.1) inductively. Every nonempty prefix
satisfies ||x_ell||<=p_ell||x0||<=a<1/2. Thus every query AFTER the first
is in the quadratic core, including all bridge inputs and outputs.
At a complete H_j endpoint, put p_jfull=beta^(2^j-1)*c^(-j). Then

 ||x_end||<=p_jfull||x0||,
 x_end equals the signed core multiplier after the first step times T_A x0.

In particular this covers initial points of the unit ball outside D itself.
The initial and terminal scaled points for U_(d+2S_j) and U_d are both core
when j>=1,d>=1. Formula (7.3) gives Cchild_j<=c^j and hence
Cchild_j*p_jfull<=beta^(2^j-1). Therefore, for every d>=1,

 U_(d+2S_j)(x0)-U_d(x_end)
 >=[1-beta^(2(2^j-1))]*||x0||^2/(d+2S_j).           (8.3)

Indeed the exact gap is ||x0||^2/(d+2S_j)-||x_end||^2/d, and
[(d+2S_j)/d]*p_jfull^2
 <=(1+2S_j)*p_jfull^2<=Cchild_j^2*p_jfull^2
 <=beta^(2(2^j-1)).
The stronger shifted-norm reserve also obeys

 R_Hj(x0)>= [1-beta^(2(2^j-1))]||x0||^2.            (8.4)

At the endpoint P_D(x_end)=x_end, so its shifted norm is Cchild_j||x_end||.
These are uniform all-depth actual-block payments on the stated initial
unit ball. They are not arbitrary-start payments. They explain exactly
how the two complete children overcome the core long-step obstruction.

## 9. The actual H3 strict-switch calibration also passes the strengthening

We rederive this separately, without importing the previous unverified
calibration as a premise. In the centered triangle, let the ordered gaps
u,v>=0 be the adjacent gaps of the zero-sum labeled coordinates
q_i=(2/3)<x,v_i>. Their true squared norm is
Q(u,v)=u^2+uv+v^2. Projection is core when u+2v<=1, vertex when u>=1,
and edge when u<=1<=u+2v. On an edge the projected coordinates are
(1/6+u/2,1/6-u/2,-1/3); on a vertex they are(2/3,-1/3,-1/3).
These formulas satisfy (2.1) and give all required guards below.

Put K=beta*c, t0=(K+2)/4 and initialize

 u0=S2+K/2, v0=t0.

All queries of the actual left H2 are in vertex1, because its upper gap
is always at least K/2>1. The endpoint has gaps(K/2,t0). The actual bridge
1+K sends the ordered gaps to(t0,t0), in vertex2 since t0>1.
This is the old strict vertex change, with a positive longitudinal switch
and no release of its transverse square.

The elementary bounds used now hold uniformly for all m>=1536:

 7/5<A<17/12, 99/100<beta<1,
 12/5<K<5/2, 11/10<t0<9/8.

For K>12/5 use lambda>241/100 (since(141/100)^2<2) and
1-epsilon^2>240/241. Its other bounds and those for A follow directly
from epsilon<=1/6144 and the rational bounds on sqrt(2). In particular
r_s>41/100 and (1-epsilon)/(1+epsilon)>40/41 imply a>2/5,
which proves A>7/5 without rounding.
Set r=A-t0 and s=2t0-A. The first right leaf yields gaps(r,s), with
11/40<r<19/60, r<1 and r+2s=4t0-A>1, so this is an actual edge query.
Set

 w0=[(3-beta)t0-2-(1-beta)a]/2.

The next step B, using the edge projection, gives gaps(w0,beta*r-w0).
They are both positive: w0>0 from t0>11/10, a<1/2 and1-beta<1/100;
also
w0<[((201/100)*(9/8))-2]/2=209/1600<1/4,
whereas beta*r>(99/100)*(11/40)>1/4.
Its core guard holds because2beta*r-w0<2r<19/30<1.
The FINAL actual leaf A therefore yields gaps

 (a*(beta*r-w0),a*w0),

whose squared norm is at most a^2*beta^2*r^2<1/36, using a<1/2,r<1/3.
The initial squared norm exceeds25, since u0>S2+K/2>5. Also
S3=2S2+1+K<27/2. The terminal point is core, so

 ||x7+S3 P_D(x7)||^2
  =(1+S3)^2||x7||^2<(29/2)^2/36<6<(6/25)||x0||^2.

Thus R_H3(x0)>(19/25)||x0||^2, and (5.2) pays this entire actual block
for every d>=1. In fact the initial scaled point is core because
u0+2v0=S2+K+1<d+2S3, giving

 U_d(x7)<(6/25)U_(d+2S3)(x0).                       (9.1)

This bound is weaker than the earlier report's calibration constant but
is derived here through the NEW sufficient terminal-norm mechanism.
It covers both real H2 children, their bridge, and the final physical leaf.
Rotations give the same statement. It remains a calibration family.

## 10. Attempts, failures, and the exact remaining interface

Attempt1 was direct summation of the full normal-mixing identity (3.2).
It exposes and retains every physical and auxiliary reserve. H2 is paid
in the explicit high-d range (4.2). On the triangle, all-depth unit-ball
inputs are paid quantitatively by (8.3), and the actual large strict-switch
calibration is paid by (9.1). None covers all low-d H2 inputs.

Attempt2, within the SAME projection-normal mechanism, sought a stronger
child reserve via the terminal shifted norm. Its implication for U was
proved directly, and its real-child gluing was derived in (6.1).
Counterexample construction then produced actual complete histories (7.1).
They refute the general-body strengthening at every parent depth>=3.
The full two-cap U comparison (7.6) excludes a false diagnosis: the desired
U law survives these counterexamples with a fixed margin.

For the centered triangle the norm strengthening is neither proved nor
refuted on arbitrary starts. The general-body interval counterexample
cannot be inserted into its different projection geometry. Conversely,
passing the unit ball and strict-switch families does not imply a general
triangle result. There is no permissible inference that a large initial
radius fixes all physical faces.

The exact open payment, already nontrivial at H2, is (4.3) against its
actual correlated leaf and normal reserves. At greater depths, the
retained signed sum (3.2) must be estimated through both child histories
and genuinely nonlocal normals without replacing the actual initial and
terminal orientations by independent maxima. The new identity (6.1) says
why one particularly natural stronger induction is unavailable for general
bodies; it supplies no substitute all-depth estimate.

No coefficient SDP, exponential case enumeration, independent maximizer
relaxation, representation premise, or unaudited arithmetic output is
presented as a solution. No new fact_id exists. No result in this report
may be used downstream as an accepted fact without a newly authorized
fact_submit gate.

## 5. Even length and total sum together do not determine the final-stage value

This is a separate, quantitative obstruction to a sum-only state. For a fixed
prefix h_pre of length k define directly

    rho_last(h_pre)=inf_(b>=0) W_(k+1)(h_pre,b).

For every integer k>=1 let S=2k, and compare the two legal prefixes

    h_even=(2,...,2),             k entries,
    h_burst=(S,0,...,0),          k entries.

They have the SAME length and the SAME total sum. Nevertheless

    rho_last(h_even)<=1/4,
    rho_last(h_burst)>=S^(2/3)/12.                       (12)

In particular their values are different for k>=3 and have an unbounded
separation as k increases. This refutes an exact value closure depending
only on (k,S), not merely a claim about one selected function.

For the first upper bound, the norm calculation in Section3 with h=2 gives
||x_k-x_star||<=||x_0-x_star|| for every legal function. Append b=3/2.
At that final stage write x=x_k-x_star, z=grad f(x_k)/L and w for the next
normalized gradient, and use values divided by L and shifted by f_star.
The normalized one-step identity, with the signed Q on samples 0,1,star,
is

    (1/4)||x||^2-2f_1+Q_(0,star)+Q_(1,star)+Q_(1,0)
                      =||(1/2)x-z-w||^2.

Expanding Q and the recursion x_1=x-(3/2)z verifies every term. Since all
Q<=0, it bounds the last objective by (1/4)||x||^2 in the W normalization.
The initial norm bound proves rho_last(h_even)<=1/4 in all dimensions.

For the second lower bound use the verified single-cut fact b98bb807d514e80d,
whose full statement and proof have been read. Its cut at the first step S
and its cut at the appended final step b yield, for EVERY b>=0,

    W_(k+1)(h_burst,b)
       >=max{(S-1)^2/(1+2b), (b-1)_+^2/(S+1)^2}.        (13)

The internal zero steps change neither prefix nor suffix sums in that fact.
If b<=S^(4/3), then S>=2 and the first term in (13) is at least

    (S^2/4)/(1+2S^(4/3)) >= S^(2/3)/12.

If b>S^(4/3), then b>2, b-1>=b/2 and S+1<=3S/2. The second term is
therefore at least

    (b^2/4)/[(3S/2)^2] > S^(2/3)/9.

Taking the infimum over b proves the second part of (12). No compactness
or attainment assertion for this infimum is needed for either bound.

The explicit prefix-sum guard in research/prefix_sum_terminal_guard.md
accordingly supplies a universal LOWER boundary condition q_S, not an exact
schedule-level state variable. The proof of (12) does not use that new,
unverified guard or the unverified minimax result.

# Max39: conjugate heights, true nearest minima, and paid retained prefixes

Status: COMPLETE JOINT PROOF DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. This is the second and
final analytical round of the wave authorized by guidance2765a48d4ff7259e:
completed baseline37, completed before this session38, intended completion39,
completed ceiling39, attempted ceiling40 including the historical interruption.
The runtime records completion after return; this document is not a runtime
exit claim. No numerical, solver, machine-algebra, replay, formal-verifier,
installation, consult, or subagent resource is used.

The original all-h, all-sufficiently-large-horizon target
W_n(h)>=(1-o(1))/B_n, with W=2R, remains OPEN. Neither this manuscript nor
the independent analytical audit of the old triangle is a verified fact.

Accepted inputs:1252b8823bb5a290 for smooth-convex interpolation and
normalization; b98bb807d514e80d for K_n and the bound B_n<=1+4n^log_2(3);
1fca3e5dd555cb4a for the complete alternating-ray reference F_ray_n.
During final readback, fact62a7d9329121fd75 was newly accepted and its
full statement and proof were read. It now supplies the fixed finite
triangle data and its stated THREE-step reference comparisons. Guidance
8f3b8c871d7017ae explicitly permits constructors to cite that predecessor;
it accepts none of the predecessor or prefix extensions below.
Every additional geometric and quantitative ingredient is proved below.
No unverified finding is a proof premise.

All short symbols below are local abbreviations, namespaced by39 in the
shared glossary. They do not redefine project-wide p,q,c or other notation.
Norms and inner products are Euclidean. A displayed two-coordinate vector
lies in a fixed plane E. Additional coordinates are orthogonal to E.

## 1. Fixed actual data and the enlarged question

Write s=sqrt(3), and put

    p=(1,2s), q=(-2,-s), e=(1,0), n=(1,-s),
    C0=conv{p,q,e},
    Wedge={v:<e,v><=1, <n,v><=1}.

The full retained physical suffix is exactly(2,2,1), with all four jets
from accepted62a7d9329121fd75 (its p_ft,q_ft,e_ft are our p,q,e)

    position       gradient      absolute value
    p              p             13/2
    -p             q              9/2
    3e             e              5/2
    2e             e              3/2.                       (1)

Indeed p-2p=-p, -p-2q=3e, and3e-e=2e.

For a preceding c>=0 a new gradient z must occur at X=p+c z.
The larger family treated here comprises ALL finite-dimensional convex,
everywhere differentiable, globally1-smooth functions interpolating(1),
and such actual predecessors, subject only to the explicit retained-wedge
restriction z in Wedge. No restriction to a zero conjugate height, fixed
closest minimizer, planar new gradient, or projection gradient map is made.
The physical old jets and their directed inequalities are all retained.

Let D(c) be the infimum of dist(X,argmin F)^2 over this family. It will be
attained explicitly for every c>=0. The actual minimum value is forced0
by(1), as proved in Section2, so the exact best family payoff is3/D(c).
This is an upper bound on this retained-jet family's payoff, not on W.

The sharp table is as follows. For c>1 use k=c-1.

I. For0<=c<=sqrt(2),

    z=p, X=(1+c)p, D(c)=13(1+c)^2.                         (2)

II. For sqrt(2)<=c<=12/5, put

    Delta=sqrt(k^2+58k+9),
    u=(11k-9-3Delta)/(8k),
    z=(u,(u-1)/s),
    D(c)=(1+c u)^2+[2s+c(u-1)/s]^2.                       (3)

III. For12/5<=c<=6, put

    v=((k-7)/(2k),-(k+7)/(2s k)),
    a0^2=(5-k)(5k-7)/(6k^2),
    tau0=k a0^2, a=sqrt(tau0),
    z=v+a w, m=(k-1)a w, beta=(k-1)tau0,                 (4)

where w is a unit vector orthogonal to E. Then

    D(c)=||p+c v||^2+4tau0
        =(k^4-18k^3+73k^2-42k+49)/(3k^2).               (5)

The explicit function is

    C=conv{p,q,e,z},
    F(x)=max_(b in C){<x-m,b>-||b||^2/2}.                 (6)

On the open interval12/5<c<6, its minimum set in E+span{w} is the ray
{t w:t<=(k-1)a}; its closest minimizer to X is m. Thus the actual initial
orthogonal distance is2a, not c a and not0. The old absolute values stay
unchanged. On this open interval every optimal member of the larger family
needs a nonzero transverse gradient and a nonzero closest minimizer.

IV. For c>=6,

    z=-p/k, X=z, D(c)=13/k^2.                            (7)

For the planar branches I,II,IV use(6) with m=0 and C=conv{p,q,e,z}
in the actual plane E. Their minimum is unique0. At12/5 the common
choice is z=q, and at6 it is z=-p/5; a=beta=m=0 at both ends of III.
At sqrt(2) different minimizing gradients may coexist. No continuous
inverse-state selection is asserted.

In particular,

    c=1: D=52, payoff3/52 < F_ray_4(1,2,2,1)=1/13;
    c=2: D=60-9sqrt(17), payoff1/(20-3sqrt(17))>1/11;
    c=3: D=43/4, payoff12/43 < F_ray_4(3,2,2,1)=4/11.     (8)

The first statement in(8) holds even without the retained-wedge
restriction. Section8 proves that the wedge is a real extra restriction
for the sharp c2 claim; it cannot silently be inferred from the old jets.

## 2. What every interpolant of the old data is forced to retain

For each old jet, f_i-<x_i,g_i>+||g_i||^2/2=0. The original directed
smooth-convex inequality from accepted1252b8823bb5a290 is

    F(x)>=f_i+<g_i,x-x_i>+||grad F(x)-g_i||^2/2.           (9)

At x=0, writing g=grad F(0), this gives

    F(0)>=||g||^2/2-<p,g>,
    F(0)>=||g||^2/2-<q,g>,
    F(0)>=||g||^2/2-<e,g>.

The positive relation p/6+q/3+e/2=0 gives F(0)>=||g||^2/2.
The smooth descent upper bound from the p jet gives
F(0)<=13/2-13+13/2=0. Consequently F(0)=0 and grad F(0)=0,
so0 is a global minimizer and the minimum value is exactly0.

If b is ANY minimizer, (9) at b yields <p,b>,<q,b>,<e,b><=0.
The same positive relation forces all three inner products to be0.
Hence the entire minimum set is contained in E-perpendicular. It is a
nonempty closed convex set containing0, so every X has a closest point.

There is also full core rigidity. If v is any convex combination of
p,q,e, multiply their versions of(9) at x=v by those weights. One obtains

    F(v)>=||v||^2/2+||grad F(v)-v||^2/2.

Smoothness from the zero minimum gives F(v)<=||v||^2/2. Thus F(v)=
||v||^2/2 and grad F(v)=v throughout C0. This conclusion allows arbitrary
extra dimensions and arbitrary unsampled values. Neither shifting the
minimum level nor changing the function inside this triangle is available
while all old jets are retained.

For any jet define its conjugate height and residual by

    beta_i=<x_i,g_i>-f_i-||g_i||^2/2, y_i=x_i-g_i.

All old heights are0; their residuals are0,n,2e,e in the order(1).
For ANY two such jets the exact signed interpolation residual is

    Q_ij=beta_j-beta_i+<y_j,g_i-g_j>.                      (10)

This follows by expanding the Q_ij definition in1252b8823bb5a290.
It fixes both orientations; no row is inferred from its reverse.

For the new jet, put beta=<X,z>-F(X)-||z||^2/2 and y=p+(c-1)z.
Every original new-old row is precisely

    beta>=0, beta>=<n,z>-1, beta>=2(<e,z>-1),
    beta>=<e,z>-1,
    beta<=<y,z-b>, b=p,q,e.                              (11)

The duplicate e gradient still carries BOTH old residuals e and2e.
The row against a minimizer b additionally gives beta>=<b,z>.
The restriction z in Wedge is stronger than the lower inequalities in
(11) when beta>0; it is explicitly imposed in our main family.

## 3. The static construction, all rows, and exact minima

For any compact convex C containing0 and any m, define F by(6).
Completing the square shows that its unique maximizing gradient is
P_C(x-m). The elementary normal criterion is

    g=P_C(x-m) iff g in C and
    <x-m-g,b-g><=0 for every b in C.                      (12)

Necessity follows by differentiating squared distance along the segment
to b. Sufficiency follows by expanding squared distances. Adding(12)
at two points proves firm nonexpansiveness of P_C and hence that it is
1-Lipschitz. The two maximizing inequalities then show

    0<=F(x')-F(x)-<P_C(x-m),x'-x><=||x'-x||^2.

Therefore F is convex, finite, differentiable, and globally1-smooth.
Its minimum value is0 and its exact minimum set is m+C^polar: the
maximum in(6) is0 exactly when P_C(x-m)=0, equivalently when
<x-m,b><=0 for all b in C. This also proves there is no unpriced minimum.

For C0 one has n dot(p,q,e)=(-5,1,1) and e dot(p,q,e)=(1,-2,1).
Thus0,n,2e,e are valid old normals. A convex set C containing C0 and
contained in Wedge retains them as supporting normals. The origin is
in the relative interior of C0 by its positive relation in Section2.

In branches I,II,IV, all chosen gradients lie in E and C has0 in its
interior relative to E. Thus C^polar={0} in E. In branchIII the added
vertex has positive a on the open interval. The old triangle forces
a polar vector to have zero E component; the added vertex then gives
C^polar={t w:t<=0}. Formula(6) has exactly the minimum set stated above.
Unused extra coordinates would add their orthogonal linear subspace.

The branches with m=0 have old normals unchanged. In III, m is
orthogonal to E and beta=<m,z>>=0. At every old vertex b, its actual
normal for the translated projection is y_i-m. Against old vertices
this has the same nonpositive products as y_i. Against z its product
is <y_i,z-b>-beta<=0, since z is in Wedge. Thus every old jet in(1),
including its absolute value, is preserved. In particular F(x_i)=
<x_i-m,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2=f_i. The physical old residual y_i remains
a valid subgradient of the conjugate height <m,b>+indicator_C(b).

At the new point it suffices to check

    <p+(c-1)z-m,b-z><=0, b=p,q,e.                       (13)

Those explicit checks are included in Section5. Its value is then
F(X)=<X-m,z>-||z||^2/2, with height beta=<m,z>.

For clarity, substituting these values into(10) gives

    Q_ij=<y_j-m,g_i-g_j><=0                              (14)

for EVERY ordered old-old, new-old, old-new pair. The normal criterion
gives each inequality separately, including equality and duplicate-point
cases. The minimum points satisfy the same criterion at gradient0, so
both directed rows to every actual minimizer hold as well. This verifies
one static function for the full run and does not reset any earlier jet.

## 4. Eliminating height while paying the TRUE initial distance

Fix an arbitrary member of the larger family and let m be its closest
minimizer to X. By Section2, m is orthogonal to E. Projection of X onto
the closed convex minimum set, which contains0, gives

    <X,m>>=||m||^2, hence c<m,z>>=||m||^2.                (15)

For c>0 this implies <m,z>>=0. Write z=v+u_perp with v in E and u_perp
orthogonal to E. For c>1 put k=c-1 and define

    S_b(v)=k||v||^2+<p-k b,v>-<p,b>, b=p,q,e,
    tau_raw=k||u_perp||^2-<m,u_perp>, tau=max(0,tau_raw).

The upper rows in(11) and beta>=<m,z> imply

    S_b(v)+tau>=0, b=p,q,e.                              (16)

If tau_raw<0, increasing it to0 only relaxes these necessary tests.
The old wedge gives <n,v><=1 and <e,v><=1. The true squared distance is

    ||X-m||^2=||p+c v||^2+||c u_perp-m||^2.              (17)

Define

    gamma=c^2/k when0<k<=1, and gamma=4 when k>=1.

Then the following exact lower bound is valid:

    ||X-m||^2 >= J(v,tau):=||p+c v||^2+gamma*tau.         (18)

Only the case tau_raw>=0 and a=||u_perp||>0 needs proof. Set
mu=<m,u_perp> and b_len=k a-mu/a. Then0<=b_len<=k a by(15),
tau=a b_len, and the component of c u_perp-m along u_perp has length
a+b_len. Thus the last term of(17) is at least(a+b_len)^2.
When k>=1, (a+b_len)^2>=4a b_len. When0<k<=1 and a b_len>0,
put r_len=b_len/a in(0,k]; the ratio is r_len+2+1/r_len,
which decreases on(0,1], so it is at least k+2+1/k=c^2/k.
When a b_len=0 the desired bound has zero right side. If u_perp=0
or tau_raw<0,
(18) just drops a nonnegative squared norm.

This is an explicit inequality, not an assumed feasibility oracle. We
will solve the resulting two-dimensional lower problem exactly and give
actual smooth functions attaining its bound. Equality in(18) for k>1,
tau>0 requires m=(k-1)u_perp and ||u_perp||^2=tau.

For0<c<=sqrt(2) a direct argument is sharper to present. Write d=z-p.
The p upper row in(11) and beta>=<m,z> give

    c<p,d>+(c-1)||d||^2 >= <m,z>.

Expand the true distance, use m orthogonal to p, and substitute this
lower bound for c<p,d>. The result is

    ||p+c z-m||^2 >=13(1+c)^2
                 +(2-c^2)||d||^2+2<m,z>+||m||^2
                 >=13(1+c)^2.                            (19)

This proof does NOT need the old wedge. At c=0, X=p is an old query,
and its closest minimizer is0 by Section2, giving squared distance13.
BranchI attains(19) on the old C0: its new residual c p is normal at p
because <p,p-q>=21 and <p,p-e>=12. Its value is13(c+1/2).

## 5. Exact sharpness certificates and construction checks

For all c>1, S_p=k||v||^2+(1-k)<p,v>-13 and
S_q=k||v||^2+<p-kq,v>+8. The following identities use the complete
original directed rows through(16); none changes their signs.

### BranchII

Let v0=(u,(u-1)/s) be the vector in(3), with
sqrt(2)-1<=k<=7/5. On this line,

    <n,v0>=1, <p,v0>=3u-2,
    3S_p(v0)=4k u^2+(9-11k)u+7k-45=0.                   (20)

The negative root is precisely(3). At u=-2 the polynomial is9(5k-7)
<=0, so its negative root u<=-2 and the e cap holds. Put b=(k-7)/(2k).
The same polynomial at b is (k-5)(5k-7)/(2k)>=0; b lies left of
its vertex by(-7k-19)/(8k)<0. Hence b<=u. For y=p+k v0,

    <y,p-q>=3[7+k(2u-1)]>=0,
    <y,p-e>=12+2k(u-1)>=5-k>0.                           (21)

Together with S_p(v0)=0, these prove S_q(v0),S_e(v0)>=0, so(13)
holds with m=0 and all construction assertions follow from Section3.

For sharpness define

    E_k=k^2+2k-1,
    ell=3c E_k/Delta,
    A=(c^2-ell)/k,
    dcap=[5c E_k+ell(5-7k)]/(4k).

The exact identity for every planar v and tau is

    J(v,tau)-D(c)
      =A(S_p(v)+tau)+dcap(1-<n,v>)
        +ell||v-v0||^2+(gamma-A)tau.                    (22)

To check the coefficients, Ak+ell=c^2 and
A(1-k)p-dcap n-2ell v0=2c p. The latter follows directly from
n=(3e-p)/2 and u=(11k-9-3Delta)/(8k); alternatively, use the orthogonal
directions n/2 and(s,1)/2. The components of v0 there are1/2 and
(4u-1)/(2s), and -2k(4u-1)/9-(1-k)=Delta/3. Substitution gives
the displayed ell and dcap. Both sides vanish at v=v0,tau=0, fixing
the constant. The tau coefficients are also identical.

Every coefficient in(22) is nonnegative. E_k>=0 and
Delta^2-9c^2=8k(5-k)>=0 imply0<=ell<=E_k=c^2-2, hence A>0.
For dcap, after factoring c E_k/(4k), the bracket is
5+3(5-7k)/Delta. It is positive when7k<=5; otherwise it is at least
5-(7k-5)/c=(10-2k)/c>0. At E_k=0, dcap=ell=0.
For k<=1, gamma-A=ell/k>=0. For1<=k<=7/5,
Delta<5c because25c^2-Delta^2=8(3k^2-k+2)>0; thus ell>=6/5,
whereas(k-1)^2<=4/25. Consequently gamma-A=
[ell-(k-1)^2]/k>0. This proves sharpness of II for the FULL larger
family, paying an arbitrary actual closest minimizer.

### BranchIII

Let v=v0 be the planar vector in(4), with7/5<=k<=5. Direct substitution
gives

    <n,v0>=1, <e,v0>=(k-7)/(2k)<=1,
    <p,v0>=-(k+21)/(2k),
    ||v0||^2=(k^2-7k+49)/(3k^2),
    S_p(v0)=S_q(v0)=-tau0,
    S_e(v0)+tau0=5-k>=0.                                (23)

For the actual candidate z=v0+a w, m=(k-1)a w, a^2=tau0,
its height is beta=(k-1)tau0. The new normal is
p+kz-m=(p+k v0)+a w. Equations(23) make its products against p-z
and q-z zero and its product against e-z equal to-(5-k). Thus every
new normal row in(13) holds. Section3 supplies one static function,
all absolute values, the actual minimum ray, and all directed rows.
Its true distance is exactly(5).

For sharpness let ell=(k-1)^2 and define

    B=(3k-1)/k+ell(k-14)/(9k^2),
    A=4-B,
    dcap=k B+7ell(5-k)/(9k).

For every planar v and tau the exact identity is

    J(v,tau)-D(c)
      =A(S_p(v)+tau)+B(S_q(v)+tau)
        +dcap(1-<n,v>)+ell||v-v0||^2.                    (24)

Here gamma=4. The tau coefficient is A+B=4; the quadratic coefficient
is k(A+B)+ell=4k+(k-1)^2=c^2. For the vector coefficient use

    q=-(p+3e)/2, n=(3e-p)/2,
    v0=-(k+7)p/(12k)+7(k-5)e/(12k).

These identities reduce the coefficient equality to the displayed B
and dcap. The constant agrees because all three slacks in(24) vanish
at(v0,tau0). Thus every term has been checked without machine algebra.

The coefficients are strictly positive except possibly ell at k=1,
which is outside this interval. Indeed

    9k^2 B=k^3+11k^2+20k-14>0,
    9k^2 A=-k^3+25k^2-20k+14
            >=20k^2-20k+14>0,

where1<=k<=5 was used. Also dcap>=k B>0. Equation(24), together
with(16)-(18), proves the sharp bound for ALL functions in the declared
larger family.

In the interior7/5<k<5, equality forces v=v0, both p and q row slacks
zero, and tau=tau0>0. Equality in(18) then forces ||u_perp||^2=tau0 and
m=(k-1)u_perp. The height bounds in(11) force beta=(k-1)tau0>0.
Consequently neither a planar predecessor nor a zero-height predecessor
attains this enlarged optimum. The positive orthogonal distance2a and
the nonzero closest minimizer are forced, not optional normalizations.

To compare with the smaller flat projection family, set m=0 and beta=0.
The transverse cost coefficient in its reduction is c^2/k. The same
v0 with transverse square a0^2 attains

    D_flat(c)=||p+c v0||^2+(c^2/k)tau0.

This is sharp in that smaller family: use the identity without a
squared-distance term and coefficients

    Aflat=c(k^2+1)/(2k^2),
    Bflat=c(k^2+2k-1)/(2k^2), dflat=k Bflat.

Their sum is c^2/k, their vector coefficient is2c p, and their
constant is fixed at(v0,tau0), so
J_flat-D_flat=Aflat(S_p+tau)+Bflat(S_q+tau)+dflat(1-<n,v>).
All coefficients are positive. In particular

    D_flat(c)-D(c)=(k-1)^2 a0^2>0                       (25)

throughout the open interval. This rederives the comparison without
assuming the correctness of the previous unverified four-branch draft.

### BranchIV

For k>=5 and gamma=4, the exact identity is

    J(v,tau)-13/k^2
      =(2/k)(S_p(v)+tau)+(c^2-2)||v+p/k||^2
        +(4-2/k)tau >=0.                                (26)

The candidate v=-p/k, tau=0 attains equality. It lies in C0: the three
defining planar inequalities of C0 are <e,v><=1, <n,v><=1,
<(-3,s),v><=3, whose values there are-1/k,5/k,-3/k. The new residual
is0, so(13) holds. This proves IV and completes the full parameter domain.

## 6. Concrete requested jets and SAME-word comparisons

At c1 the function with C=C0,m=0 has X=2p,z=p,F(X)=39/2.
Section4 proves its radius52 optimal even over all smooth interpolants
without a wedge cap. It is a legal positive-payoff unit prepend, but its
best retained-jet payoff3/52 is below the accepted ray1/13.

At c2 write r=sqrt(17). With

    u=(1-3r)/4, z=(u,(u-1)/s), X=p+2z,
    ||z||^2=13, <p,z>=A0:=(-5-9r)/4,
    F(X)=(73-9r)/4, ||X||^2=60-9r,                      (27)

the exact wedge-constrained payoff is1/(20-3r)>1/11 since r>3.
The new p row is equality; the new normal products against q-p and e-p
are(-39+9r)/2<0 and(-21+3r)/2<0. The complete output keeps the
new normal p+z and the new value in(27), not only the old wedge.

At c3, the genuinely height-repaired candidate is

    z=(-5/4,-3s/4,s/2), m=(0,0,s/2), beta=3/4,
    X=(-11/4,-s/4,3s/2), ||z||^2=4, F(X)=7/2.

The actual minimum set is{(0,0,t):t<=s/2}; its true squared initial
distance is43/4 and its payoff is12/43. The smaller flat-family radius
is89/8, so the gain is strict. Nevertheless12/43<4/11.

For the SAME physical word(c,2,2,1), accepted b98bb807d514e80d gives

    K_4=max{1/(2c+11),(c-1)_+^2/11,
             1/[7(c+1)^2],1/[3(c+3)^2]}.

The fourth term is below the first because
3(c+3)^2-(2c+11)=3c^2+16c+16>0.
The FULL accepted ray reference is

    R(c)=F_ray_4(c,2,2,1)
      =1/[7(1+c)^2]                 for0<=c<=2/7;
       1/(2c+11)                   for2/7<=c<=b1;
       (c-1)^2/11                  for b1<=c<=b2;
       2c-9                        for b2<=c<=8;
       (c-1)^2/7                   for c>=8,              (28)

where b1=(sqrt(209)-7)/4 and b2=12-2sqrt(11).

Here is an exhaustive check of the extra checkpoint choices, using
1fca3e5dd555cb4a with its original offsets and areas. A step1 cannot
be a nonterminal checkpoint, since the next required positive magnitude
would be nonpositive. As a final checkpoint after another cut it fails
the positive old-magnitude constraint; alone it contributes0. The two
steps2 together have first magnitudes1/(1+c),1/(1+c), terminal budget
(1-c)/(1+c), and require at least2/(1+c), so are infeasible. The triple
(c,2,2), when its intermediate magnitudes are positive, has magnitudes
1,k,k and terminal budget k but requires at least2k. Thus it is infeasible.

The remaining extra pair(c,first2) has ordinary sums(0,0,3), is feasible
for k>=4, and gives2k-7 on[4,7], then k^2/7. Pair(c,second2) has sums
(0,2,1), is feasible for k>=6, and gives2k/3-3 on[6,9], then k^2/27.
The first pair dominates the second: on[6,7] their difference is
4k/3-4>0; for k>=7 the second is at most k^2/27, since
k^2/27-(2k/3-3)=(k-9)^2/27. Comparing the surviving terms gives(28):
the first/third single-cut crossing is2/7, the Huber/c-cut crossing
reduces to c(2c^2+7c-20)=0, and the pair/c-cut crossing is
k^2-22k+77=0, with relevant root11-2sqrt(11). This includes all boundaries.

Consequently the exact comparison for EVERY c>=0 is the explicit expression

    (3/D(c))/F_ray_4(c,2,2,1)=3/[D(c)R(c)].              (29)

There is no remaining parameter optimization in(29). The ratio is not
asserted to exceed1 everywhere. On0<=c<=sqrt(2) it exceeds1 exactly
when c<(-10+6sqrt(10))/13. On c>=6 it always exceeds1: for6<=c<=8,
3(c-1)^2-13(2c-9)=3c^2-32c+120 is positive, and for c>=8 the ratio
is21/13. On the intervening intervals(3),(5),(28) give the exact sign test.

All scalar convex quadratics and orthogonal sums are also below R(c).
A scalar curvature t in[0,1] gives

    t(1-c t)^2(1-2t)^4(1-t)^2.

The factor without(1-c t)^2 is at most1/16. On[0,1/2] it is at most
t(1-t)^6, whose maximum6^6/7^7 is less than1/16; its derivative changes
sign at1/7 and16*6^6=746496<823543=7^7. On[1/2,1], |1-2t|<=t,
so it is at most t^5(1-t)^2<=t^2(1-t)^2<=1/16. The whole value is
therefore at most max{1,(c-1)^2}/16. For c<=2 this is below1/(2c+11);
for c>=2 it is below(c-1)^2/11. Orthogonal quadratic sums are convex
averages with squared-distance weights; zero-curvature directions belong
to the actual minimum set and contribute no true distance.

This does not exhaust arbitrary nonlinear scalar witnesses or the complete
complex-polynomial channel on the NEW word. Those comparison gaps remain.
No comparison from the separate audit on(2,2,1) is transferred to a different
physical word, and no upper bound on unrestricted W is asserted.

## 7. Every stable prefix: a sharp paid obstruction beyond holding gradients

Let d=(d_0,...,d_(j-1)) be ANY finite physical prefix with0<=d_i<=1,
total H=sum d_i. For a static convex1-smooth function of minimum0, let
x_(i+1)=x_i-d_i g_i, g_i=grad F(x_i), f_i=F(x_i). Fix ANY minimizer b.
The following inequality holds for the actual ending jet(x_j,g_j,f_j):

    ||x_0-b||^2 >= ||x_j-b||^2
                   +2H f_j+(H^2+H)||g_j||^2.           (30)

It follows directly from the original rows, not from an assumed direction
pattern or a constant-gradient condition. First, adding the two adjacent
rows yields cocoercivity
d_i<g_i,g_i-g_(i+1)> >= ||g_i-g_(i+1)||^2. For d_i>0 this implies

    ||g_i||^2-||g_(i+1)||^2
       >=(2/d_i-1)||g_i-g_(i+1)||^2>=0.

For d_i=0 the positions, gradients and values coincide. Thus every
||g_i||^2 is at least G:=||g_j||^2. The lower adjacent value row gives

    f_i-f_(i+1) >= d_i<g_(i+1),g_i>
                         +||g_i-g_(i+1)||^2/2
      =d_i(||g_i||^2+||g_(i+1)||^2)/2
                         +(1-d_i)||g_i-g_(i+1)||^2/2
      >=d_i G.

Writing H_i=sum_(r=i)^(j-1)d_r, it follows that f_i>=f_j+H_i G.
The row to the minimizer yields <g_i,x_i-b>>=f_i+||g_i||^2/2,
so the exact distance decrement satisfies

    ||x_i-b||^2-||x_(i+1)-b||^2
       >=2d_i f_i+d_i(1-d_i)||g_i||^2
       >=2d_i f_j+[2d_i H_i+d_i-d_i^2]G.

Sum, and use2sum d_i H_i-sum d_i^2=H^2, to obtain(30).
The same statement applies to the closest minimizer of x_0 and therefore
pays the true initial distance. No gradient correlations were discarded.

For ALL smooth interpolants retaining(1), append the original suffix after
this stable prefix, so x_j=p. Section2 gives ||p-b||^2>=13, f_j=13/2,
G=13, and hence

    dist(x_0,argmin F)^2 >=13(1+H)^2.                    (31)

Equality is attained for EVERY such prefix by the original static C0:
x_i=(1+H_i)p, g_i=p, f_i=13(H_i+1/2). Its residual H_i p is normal
at p, all physical updates hold, and(14) includes every old/new pair.
Thus the exact best payoff over ALL static smooth interpolants retaining
the old triangle, in every dimension and with arbitrary earlier gradients,
on this full word(d,2,2,1) is

    V_old(d)=3/[13(1+H)^2].                              (32)

This is stronger than a failure of one proposed holding policy. It is a
sharp obstruction for the entire stated retained-jet family. No wedge
restriction is needed for(31)-(32).

The COMPLETE accepted same-word ray and K references are

    R_old(d)=max{1/(2H+11),1/[7(1+H)^2]}.                (33)

Any selected prefix step<=1 either prevents a positive next magnitude or
is a final zero contribution. The only positive checkpoints can be the
two steps2; selecting both requires1-H>=2 and is impossible. Their
single-cut terms are1/[7(1+H)^2] and1/[3(H+3)^2], with the second below
the Huber term. This exhausts the complete accepted theorem. The two
terms in(33) cross at H=2/7; (32) exceeds(33) exactly for
H<(-10+6sqrt(10))/13. Its ratio to the Huber term tends to0 as H grows.

There is a second sharp conclusion after keeping the useful c2 jet(27).
Retain ALL five original/new jets and prepend ANY stable word d to the
actual word(2,2,2,1). The endpoint of d is the fixed planar X in(27),
so Section2 again gives ||X-b||^2>=||X||^2. Equation(30) yields

    dist(x_0,argmin F)^2 >= D_2(H)
      :=13+2(H+2)A0+13(H+2)^2.                           (34)

Indeed its right side is ||X||^2+2H F(X)+13(H^2+H), by(27).
This bound is attained on the fixed C=conv{p,q,e,z} with m=0 and z
from(27). Both p+z and z are normals at z: the first was checked in(27),
and for the second use ||z||^2=13>=<z,p>,<z,q>,<z,e>.
Here <z,q>=1-3u<13 because u>-4, equivalent to sqrt(17)<17/3.
Thus every residual p+(1+H_i)z is normal at z. The actual earlier points
are p+(2+H_i)z, their gradients z, and all values and rows follow from
the SAME static function. Its minimum remains unique0 in E.

Therefore3/D_2(H) is the exact best payoff for ALL stable changing-gradient
prefixes of this FIXED c2 state, even across all smooth interpolants and
extra dimensions. Allowing earlier gradients to change cannot improve it.
The formula is also a feasible holding construction for arbitrary
nonnegative prefix steps, but its optimality claim is restricted to[0,1].

For the full word(d,2,2,2,1), the complete ray and K references are

    R_2(d)=max{1/(2H+15),1/[11(1+H)^2]},                 (35)

crossing at H=2/11. The other two single-cut terms have denominators
7(H+3)^2 and3(H+5)^2, both greater than2H+15. A pair of the three
steps2 gives next magnitude m0 or m0/3, and final budget no larger
than m0; the required terminal amount is at least2m0, so fails.
For all three checkpoints, positivity and the same-ray condition force
H=0, after which the final budget is m0 and its required amount2m0.
As before, no step<=1 can supply another positive checkpoint. This proves
the exhaustion in(35). The exact comparison is3/[D_2(H)R_2(d)], and
it tends to0 as H grows.

For completeness all scalar convex quadratics are also below the Huber
terms in(33) and(35). Write r=2 or3 for the number of consecutive steps2.
Their scalar payoff is

    t(1-2t)^(2r)(1-t)^2 product_i(1-d_i t)^2, 0<=t<=1.

If t<=1/2, it is at most t exp[-(2r+2+2H)t], hence at most
1/[exp(1)(2r+2+2H)]<1/(4r+3+2H), using exp(1)>2.
If t>=1/2, |1-2t|<=t, so the original factor is at most
t^(2r+1)(1-t)^2<=1/16; the prefix product is at most exp(-H).
Since16 exp(H)>=16(1+H)>4r+3+2H for r=2,3, this again is below
the same Huber term. The elementary bound1-u<=exp(-u) for u>=0
gives each product estimate. Orthogonal sums obey the same true-distance
weighted comparison. Arbitrary nonlinear scalar and complex-channel
comparisons remain unclaimed.

For j unit prefix steps, H=j. The payoff in(32), or3/D_2(H) from(34),
is O(j^-2). The accepted bound B_(j+r)<=1+4(j+r)^log_2(3), for fixed
suffix length r=3 or4, shows that their best retained-family payoff
divided by U_(j+r)^ZJ tends to0. This is a quantified all-interpolant
retained-data obstruction. It neither upper-bounds unrestricted W nor
forbids switching to another witness or redesigning the old suffix.

## 8. A boundary counterexample: the wedge cannot be dropped silently

This section tests an overextension of the table, not the retained-wedge
claim. Keep all old jets, their absolute values, the physical word(2,2,2,1),
and a single static smooth convex function, but allow the NEW gradient
to leave the old wedge. With s=sqrt3 put

    z=(-1-s,1-2s), y=(-s,1), beta=4-2s>0,
    X=p+2z=(-1-2s,2-2s).

Define on the old plane

    psi(v)=max{0,<n,v>-1,2(<e,v>-1),beta+<y,v-z>},
    F(x)=sup_v{<x,v>-||v||^2/2-psi(v)}.                  (36)

The quadratic plus convex maximum is1-strongly convex and coercive.
The same strong-subgradient/maximizing argument used for the accepted
interpolation construction1252b8823bb5a290 proves that F is finite,
convex, differentiable and globally1-smooth. Explicitly, its maximizer
g(x) is unique, the sum of two strong subgradient inequalities gives
||g(x)-g(x')||<=||x-x'||, and the two maximizing inequalities give
differentiability with gradient g(x).

At p,q,e, psi=0. The last plane in(36) is0 at p and q, and is
-2s at e; the n and2e planes give the same old normals as before.
Thus0 is a subgradient at p, n at q, and both2e and e at e, preserving
ALL jets(1) and all their directed rows. At z the n plane equals beta,
the new plane equals beta, and the other two are smaller. Therefore y
is a subgradient of psi at z and X=z+y is its actual primal query.
Its exact data are

    ||z||^2=17-2s, <p,z>=-13+s,
    F(X)=17/2, ||X||^2=29-4s.                            (37)

At v=0, only the zero plane is active: the others are-1,-2,-s.
Thus psi(0)=0 and its subgradient there is uniquely0, so the exact
minimum of F is0 at the unique point0. Its terminal gap remains3/2.
Every original/new directed row follows either from the displayed
subgradients or directly from(10).

Its new gradient violates the old wedge: <n,z>=5-2s>1. It is therefore
NOT an admissible member of Sections1-6. It has

    29-4sqrt3 < 60-9sqrt(17),                            (38)

because9sqrt(17)<31+4sqrt3 reduces after squaring positive quantities
to46<31sqrt3, which follows from2116<2883. It supplies a strictly
better c2 payoff if the wedge is removed. The exact unrestricted c2
optimum is NOT determined here. In contrast, the unit-prepend lower
bound(19) and stable-prefix obstruction(30)-(32) are independent of
the wedge and survive this test.

## 9. Reusable output, precise limits, and provenance

For each c, the output is the full set of actual old jets(1), the new
X,z,F(X), its conjugate height beta, the compact set C, translation m,
the whole minimum set m+C^polar, and the exact true distance D(c).
The original wedge is retained, including its old supporting faces.
Every later extension must retain the NEW jet and its height-dependent
normal, as well as every earlier pair. Reapplying the table while dropping
the added row is not justified. The table is not an arbitrary-depth
closure rule for changing large-step prefixes.

The new third-coordinate construction improves the exact one-step table
on its entire open middle interval, with an explicitly paid closest
minimum. The sharp stable-prefix theorem closes a larger potential
repair than the previous holding-policy obstruction: for fixed retained
triangle data, and for the fixed c2 output state, it covers ALL earlier
gradients and ALL smooth interpolants on the stated stable prefixes.
The wedge-free c2 example shows the one-step table still has a genuine
geometric scope. None of these conclusions in the draft is a statement of the
original all-word theorem, and no original target is declared solved.

Retrieval was conditioned on the active retained-triangle program, seeking
a paid moving-minimum/conjugate-height interface. Matlas returned

The exact local Taylor--Hendrickx--Glineur paper arXiv1502.05666 was read
