# Full original primitive coordinates with positive semidefiniteness built in

Status: COMPLETE JOINT RESEARCH DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. This document belongs
to max2's ONE analytical pilot after completed44/attempted45, under
TASK.md and master_guidance5017191b944783b7. No mathematical evaluator,
numerical/solver call, machine algebra, fact_submit, consult, or native
subagent is used. The original exact-profile Goal remains OPEN.

The accepted inputs are fact04219554e41d9750, Section10, for the full
original primitive identity, fact1252b8823bb5a290 for interpolation
orientation, and fact4c92d3fc4eb6153e for the unchanged capacity target.
Earlier drafts841a70f2ad83841d, c6572c6316ca1876, and
443f4b4325a9901d are awareness only. Every new identity, reconstruction,
boundary treatment, and strict-start claim used below is proved jointly
here; Appendix A reproduces and checks the entire needed seed proof.
No draft is adopted as a fact-graph predecessor.

The result of this pilot is an exact representation of the original
certificate cone, at draft level. It is not a seed above the threshold,
a numerical convergence result, or a solution of the original problem.

## 1. Original object and precise coverage claims

Fix an integer r>=0 and a physical word h=(h_0,...,h_(r-1)) with every
h_i>=0. Put n=r+1, s=sum_i h_i, A=1+s, and let e be the all-ones
column in R^n. All n formal gradients remain distinct coordinates.
Use exactly the original matrices

    T_ii=1, T_it=h_t for t<i, T_it=0 for t>i,
    omega=(-2h_0,...,-2h_(r-1),2s)^T,
    D=diag(h_0(2-h_0),...,h_(r-1)(2-h_(r-1)),-s(s+2)).   (1)

For a real zero-diagonal array Lambda define

    L=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda,
    M=D+sym(LT),       sym(B)=(B+B^T)/2.                 (2)

The original cone requires L^T e=omega, M>=0 on ALL of R^n, and
Lambda_ij>=0 for EVERY distinct ordered pair i,j. The full gradient
remainder in (2) and the original values/positions are not changed.

Here are the exact statements to be proved.

For h nonzero, define v=T^(-1)e and chi=1-A^2 v_r^2. Before any signs
or PSD are imposed, the possible M arising from every real Lambda
with L^T e=omega are EXACTLY the real symmetric matrices satisfying

    e^T M e=0,              v^T M v=chi.                (3)

Every such M has the explicit full family of original L in Section4.
This is the literal flow-only real equality space. The equality slice
used in the previous chart additionally imposes M e=0. Restricting (3)
to PSD forces precisely this stronger condition. For the PSD slice,
the necessary and sufficient matrix conditions before Lambda signs are

    M>=0,       M e=0,       v^T M v=chi.               (4)

Sections5-6 parameterize all of (4), with no further PSD test or matrix
completion left. Section7 recovers every original coefficient and
retains ALL its signs as explicit inequalities. For h=0, the equality
image changes: even without PSD it is exactly M=M^T, M e=0, and the
all-zero formulas in Section6 replace the two-direction formulas.

The real equality-space statement does not call an indefinite M a
certificate. Conversely it does not incorrectly replace e^T M e=0 by
M e=0 before PSD is imposed. These two scopes are distinguished here.

## 2. Explicit inverse and the actual two trajectory directions

The inverse of T requires no assumed nonsingular submatrix. Define R by

    R_ii=1, R_ij=0 for i<j,
    R_ij=-h_j product_(k=j+1)^(i-1)(1-h_k) for i>j.      (5)

An empty product is1. To prove (5), solving Tz=y by forward
substitution gives z_i=y_i-sum_(t<i)h_t z_t. For y the j-th coordinate
vector, z_i=0 for i<j, z_j=1, z_(j+1)=-h_j, and the equations at i
and i+1 give z_(i+1)=(1-h_i)z_i for i>j. Thus the proposed column is
the unique solution. All columns prove TR=I; a square matrix with a
right inverse is invertible (its columns span R^n), so R=T^(-1).
Equivalently the forward recursion itself supplies its unique inverse.

For v=Re the same recursion gives

    v_0=1, v_(i+1)=(1-h_i)v_i,
    v_i=product_(t<i)(1-h_t).                            (6)

For the actual scalar function f(x)=x^2/2, x_0=1, GD gives
x_i=g_i=v_i and f_i=v_i^2/2. All ordered Q_ij vanish for this function,
including pairs with the optimum0. It has unique minimizer0 and true
initial distance1. Thus v is the actual unit-curvature gradient
trajectory, not the common-gradient vector e. Coordinates after a
unit step may vanish; they remain original independent formal
coordinates in the certificate.

The direction e is also an actual gradient trajectory. For example
take the 1-smooth convex scalar Huber function equal to x^2/2 for
|x|<=1 and |x|-1/2 for |x|>=1, with initial point s+2. Since all
h_i>=0, every query lies in its right linear part, so all g_i=1.
Here all finite Q_ij and V_h vanish. The matrix proof of the common
kernel below does not rely on deleting any formal variables.
For the value assertion, f_i-f_r=sum_(t>=i)h_t, and substituting this
in V_h gives sum_i h_i^2+2sum_(i<t)h_i h_t-s^2=0.

Write z=Te, so z_i=1+sum_(t<i)h_t. Elementary finite sums give

    e^T D e=-sum_i h_i^2-s^2,
    omega^T T e=sum_i h_i^2+s^2,
    e^T omega=0.                                        (7)

For the second formula, expand
-2sum_i h_i(1+sum_(t<i)h_t)+2s(1+s), then use
2sum_(t<i)h_t h_i=s^2-sum_i h_i^2. No step positivity beyond the
original nonnegative domain is needed for these identities.

If L e=0 and L^T e=omega, (2),(7) imply e^T M e=0. If in addition
M>=0, for any y and real t the nonnegative quadratic
(e+t y)^T M(e+t y)=2t y^T M e+t^2 y^T M y
forces y^T M e=0. Hence M e=0.

Also (LT)v=Le=0, so for every such real L,

    v^T M v=v^T Dv
      =sum_(i<r) h_i(2-h_i)v_i^2-s(s+2)v_r^2
      =1-A^2 v_r^2=chi.                                (8)

The telescoping step is h_i(2-h_i)v_i^2=v_i^2-v_(i+1)^2.
Independently, substituting the actual unit quadratic into V_h gives
exactly chi. Thus chi>=0 is an original necessary condition whenever
the full certificate is feasible. No static-function surrogate or
restricted principal submatrix is being substituted for the cone.

Finally, e and v are independent exactly when h is nonzero. A multiple
v=c e must have c=v_0=1. Then (6) forces each h_i=0. The converse
follows directly. This identifies the only degeneracy of the pair.

## 3. A fixed, explicit orthonormal frame, including zero steps

Assume r>=1. Define the n-by-r Helmert matrix E, with columns indexed
j=0,...,r-1 and rows i=0,...,r, by

    E_ij=1/sqrt((j+1)(j+2))        if 0<=i<=j,
         -(j+1)/sqrt((j+1)(j+2))  if i=j+1,
         0                        if i>j+1.             (9)

Every column sums to zero and has squared norm1. For columns j<k,
the entries of column k are constant on the support of column j, so
their inner product vanishes. There are r orthonormal columns in the
r-dimensional space e-perp, hence E^T E=I and EE^T=I-ee^T/n.

If h is nonzero, set

    mu=e^T v/n, u=v-mu e, nu=u^T u>0,
    q=u/sqrt(nu), w=E^T v/sqrt(nu), eta_frame=1+w_0.     (10)

Then w^T w=1, Ew=q, and, crucially for this nonnegative-word domain,

    w_0=(v_0-v_1)/sqrt(2nu)=h_0/sqrt(2nu)>=0,
    1<=eta_frame<=2.                                   (11)

For j=1,...,r-1 define the n-by-(r-1) matrix F by

    F_j=E_j-w_j(q+E_0)/eta_frame.                       (12)

Thus there is no unidentified nullspace basis. Let f_0 be the first
coordinate vector in R^r, t=w+f_0, and H=I-t t^T/eta_frame. Since
t^T t=2eta_frame, H^T H=I and H f_0=-w. Its other columns are
orthonormal and orthogonal to w. Equation(12) is exactly E times
these other columns. Consequently

    F^T F=I, F^T e=F^T v=0, F^T q=0,
    FF^T=Pi:=I-ee^T/n-u u^T/nu.                        (13)

For r=1, F has zero columns and every formula involving its zero-size
coordinates has its empty-matrix meaning. No denominator h_i or
1-h_i occurs. Thus individual zero or unit steps require no new chart.
Only h=0 requires the separate rule in Section6.

## 4. Complete explicit skew reconstruction and proof of both directions

Still let h be nonzero. Suppose M=M^T satisfies the two scalar
conditions (3), without assuming PSD or M e=0. Define

    a=(M-D)e-T^T omega,
    b=(D-M)v,
    c=b-mu a,
    d=c+e(a^T u)/n,                                    (14)
    K0=(a e^T-e a^T)/n+(d u^T-u d^T)/nu.              (15)

Here a,b,c,d are reconstruction columns, not physical steps or seed
constants; they will be denoted a_sk45,...,d_sk45 in shared metadata.
The complete formula is

    K=K0+F Xi F^T,       Xi^T=-Xi arbitrary,
    B=M-D+K,
    L=B R.                                             (16)

Xi has size(r-1)-by-(r-1). This includes every homogeneous freedom.
On the PSD/common-kernel slice, (14) simplifies to
a=-De-T^T omega, but the general expression is used to prove (3).

Proof of existence. Equations(3),(7),(8) give

    e^T a=0,  v^T b=0,  e^T b+v^T a=0.                (17)

For the last identity the M and D terms cancel by symmetry, and the
remaining term is -v^T T^T omega=-e^T omega=0. Now
e^T c=-a^T u and
u^T c=v^T b-mu(e^T b+v^T a)+mu^2 e^T a=0.
It follows that e^T d=u^T d=0. Therefore (15) is skew and satisfies

    K0 e=a,
    K0 u=-e(a^T u)/n+d=c,
    K0 v=mu a+c=b.                                    (18)

The homogeneous term in(16) kills both e and v by(13). Thus Bv=0,
so L e=B R e=0. Also

    B^T e=(M-D-K)e=(M-D)e-a=T^T omega,
    L^T e=R^T B^T e=omega.                            (19)

Since LT=B and K is skew, the actual matrix D+sym(LT) is exactly
the prescribed M. This proves sufficiency without any sign condition.

Proof of necessity and full homogeneous coverage. Start with ANY real
zero-diagonal Lambda having L^T e=omega. Its definition gives Le=0.
Equations(7)-(8) prove (3). Let K=skew(LT)=(LT-T^T L^T)/2. Then
(LT)v=0 and (LT)^T e=T^T omega imply Ke=a and Kv=b exactly as in
(14). Therefore N=K-K0 is skew and kills both e and u. Skew symmetry
then also gives e^T N=u^T N=0, whence N=Pi N Pi.
With the explicit F in(13), N=F(F^T N F)F^T. Thus Xi=F^T N F is
skew and (16) reproduces the starting L exactly. Moreover

    F^T K0 F=0, so Xi=F^T skew(LT)F.                  (20)

Uniqueness of Xi for a fixed M follows from F^T F=I. Every original
real equality array has appeared. Nothing was selected by support,
symmetry of Lambda, rank of M, or a positive-point assumption.

## 5. PSD and the unit-curvature moment built into the coordinates

Let h be nonzero. If chi<0 there is no PSD M in(4), by(8). If chi>=0,
put m=r-1. Let C be an arbitrary real lower triangular m-by-m matrix
whose diagonal is nonnegative. All its strictly lower entries are
free real numbers. Let Xi be an arbitrary real skew m-by-m matrix.

For chi>0, put alpha=chi/nu>0 and choose an arbitrary z in R^m. Set

    k=sqrt(alpha)q+Fz,
    M=k k^T+F C C^T F^T.                              (21)

Then M>=0 on all of R^n, Me=0, and v^TMv=nu*alpha=chi. Feed it
into(14)-(16). Thus all equalities and PSD are automatic. The only
remaining original coefficient conditions are the explicit signs in
Section7. Neither C nor z is fitted by an unmentioned completion.

For chi=0, use the different rule

    M=F C C^T F^T.                                    (22)

There is no division by chi or sqrt(alpha). In this case Mv=0,
as PSD and zero quadratic moment require. Xi is still unrestricted
skew of the same size. In particular all extra-kernel and zero-
remainder cases are included.

Proof of surjectivity. The orthonormal basis [q F] of e-perp expresses
any symmetric M with Me=0 by the block

    [q F]^T M[q F]=[[alpha,beta^T],[beta,G]],           (23)

where alpha=chi/nu. If alpha>0, minimizing its quadratic in the first
coordinate proves that M>=0 is equivalent to
S=G-beta beta^T/alpha>=0. Set z=beta/sqrt(alpha), and choose a
lower triangular factor C with CC^T=S by the explicit procedure below.
Then (21) gives exactly the original M, not merely the same moment.
If alpha=0, the quadratic on each two-coordinate plane forces beta=0,
and M>=0 is equivalent to G>=0. Factor G to obtain(22).

Finite factor construction, including every singular case. For a
symmetric PSD matrix S, begin with its full residual. At pivot j,
its diagonal p is nonnegative. If p>0, set C_jj=sqrt(p), the lower
column entries to the residual entries divided by sqrt(p), and remove
that column outer product on the trailing block. Choosing the first
coordinate to minimize the quadratic proves that the trailing
residual is PSD. If p=0, positivity of every quadratic in the pivot
coordinate and one later coordinate forces the entire corresponding
residual row/column to be zero. Set that whole factor column to zero
and pass to the trailing block. Induction gives CC^T=S in finitely
many scalar operations, with no division by a zero pivot. Conversely
every lower triangular C generates a PSD CC^T. This proves coverage;
free factors can have redundant descriptions on singular boundaries.

In the positive branch, the rank of M is 1+rank(C), by the invertible
block shear reducing(23) to diag(alpha,CC^T). In the zero branch it
is rank(C). Thus the common kernel is only REQUIRED to contain e;
kernel exactly span(e) occurs when chi>0 and C is nonsingular.
No fixed-rank ansatz has been imposed.

For r=5, the positive branch has four z coordinates, ten lower
triangular factor entries, and six skew entries: twenty real
coefficient coordinates, in addition to the five physical steps.
Its interior has C_jj>0; permitting zero diagonal values retains all
singular PSD remainders. Requiring h_i>0, chi>0, C_jj>0 and all
Lambda_ij>0 is a useful strict search domain, but is only that stated
restricted domain. The full rules (21),(22),(24) cover the boundaries.

## 6. The all-zero word, the empty word, and other boundary cases

If h=0, then T=R=I, omega=D=0 and v=e. Both Le=0 and L^Te=0 imply
M e=sym(L)e=0, even if M is indefinite. Conversely, given any real
symmetric M with Me=0 and any skew K with Ke=0, L=M+K has exactly
those two flows and sym(L)=M. This proves the all-zero equality image.

For its PSD cone let E be the explicit n-by-r matrix(9), choose any
lower triangular r-by-r C with nonnegative diagonal and any skew
r-by-r Xi, and set

    M=E C C^T E^T, K=E Xi E^T, L=M+K.                (24)

The same finite factor proof gives ALL PSD M with Me=0, and any skew
matrix killing e is E Xi E^T. All original Lambda signs must still be
imposed. Every formal gradient remains an original coordinate even
though the physical queries are duplicated. In particular this is
not a compressed positive-step certificate.

If r=0, all arrays have size1; the only zero-diagonal Lambda is0,
L=M=0, and the empty word is included. The zero-dimensional E,C,Xi
in(24) have exactly this meaning.

If a nonzero word contains a zero physical step, nu remains positive;
(5),(9)-(22) remain valid with that step zero. If it contains a unit
step, v_r=0 and chi=1, so it belongs to the positive branch. No inverse
product of 1-h_i is ever needed. If chi=0 and h is nonzero, e and v
are independent and must both be in the PSD kernel, exactly as(22)
enforces. These statements cover mixtures of zero and unit steps.

A one-step audit makes the all-zero exception concrete. For r=1 and
h_0=x>0, write Lambda_01=a0, Lambda_10=b0. Flow says b0=a0+2x,
and direct row summation gives (Me)_0=x(1-a0/2). Thus on the PSD
slice a0=2, b0=2(1+x), and

    M=(2-x^2)[[1,-1],[-1,1]].                         (25)

It is PSD exactly for 0<x<=sqrt2, in agreement with
chi=x^2(2-x^2). At x=0, instead, every balanced a0=b0>=0 is allowed,
with M=a0[[1,-1],[-1,1]]. A single positive-word chart cannot silently
stand in for this dimension increase. Formula(24) includes it.

## 7. Every original sign, diagonal, value flow, and full remainder

After any appropriate matrix construction above, define

    Lambda_ii=0, Lambda_ij=-L_ij for i!=j.              (26)

Since Le=0, L_ii=-sum_(j!=i)L_ij=sum_j Lambda_ij, so its diagonal
convention is exactly L=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda. There is no free or
ignored diagonal parameter. The explicit remaining conditions are

    -[(M-D+K)R]_ij>=0 for EVERY ordered i!=j.          (27)

For clarity these can be written as individual finite expressions.
Writing B=M-D+K, if j<r,

    Lambda_ij=-B_ij+h_j sum_(t=j+1)^r
                         B_it product_(k=j+1)^(t-1)(1-h_k),

and if j=r, Lambda_ir=-B_ir, in both cases for i!=j. For the all-zero
case R=I. There are all30 original inequalities at r=5 and all42 at
r=6, with none grouped away, deleted, or replaced by signs of factors.

The actual value-flow equation is(19), and the actual full remainder
is D+sym(LT)=M. The accepted identity04219554e41d9750 therefore gives,
in its original normalization and original formal variables,

    V_h+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij
                      =<M,Gram(g_0,...,g_r)>.         (28)

The coordinates for M are an orthonormal representation of a full
quadratic form. They do not set a gradient equal to another. The
original x_0 row is zero as explained in that accepted fact; optimum
edges are not being repurposed to repair any missing positive entry.
All actual Q_ij<=0 have the orientation fixed by1252b8823bb5a290.

Conversely start with ANY feasible original cone point. Equations
(7)-(8) force(4). In the nonzero case(23) and the finite factor rule
recover coordinates for its exact M, and(20) recovers its exact Xi.
Equations(16),(26) then give its exact L and every exact Lambda entry.
The all-zero converse is proved in Section6. Thus (27) completes an
if-and-only-if representation of the whole original cone. Choosing a
PSD M with the right moment does NOT by itself solve(27); the free
skew parameters and every original sign remain essential conditions.

## 8. Optional physical coordinates that enforce chi>=0 automatically

The preceding full matrix chart accepts all h>=0 with chi>=0 and
declares chi<0 impossible. The following optional finite rule also
builds this scalar domain into the physical word, with no loss.

For r>=1 choose the first r-1 steps arbitrarily nonnegative, and put

    Bcap=1+sum_(i<r-1)h_i>=1,
    Ppre=product_(i<r-1)(1-h_i).

Let x=h_(r-1). If Ppre=0, take any x>=0; then chi=1. If Ppre!=0,
put ccap=1/abs(Ppre)>0 and define

    lo=0                                    if Bcap<=ccap,
    lo=(1-Bcap+sqrt((Bcap+1)^2-4ccap))/2     if Bcap>ccap,
    hi=(1-Bcap+sqrt((Bcap+1)^2+4ccap))/2,
    x=lo+theta(hi-lo),         0<=theta<=1.              (29)

In the second lower-bound branch the radicand exceeds(Bcap-1)^2,
so lo>0. In both branches lo<1 and hi>1. Every root and denominator
is therefore defined and hi-lo>0.

To prove exact coverage, g(x)=(Bcap+x)(1-x) is continuous and strictly
decreasing for x>=0: for y>x,
g(y)-g(x)=(y-x)(1-Bcap-x-y)<0. Its value at0 is Bcap and at1 is0,
and it tends to minus infinity. The condition chi>=0 is exactly
-ccap<=g(x)<=ccap. The roots g(x)=ccap when needed and
g(x)=-ccap are exactly lo and hi. Thus the allowed interval is
precisely[lo,hi], including both saturation boundaries. The all-zero
word occurs in this rule and is then sent to(24), not to(10).

For any given eligible word with Ppre!=0 its inverse coordinate is
theta=(x-lo)/(hi-lo), with a strictly positive denominator. The case
Ppre=0 has x itself as coordinate. Hence the optional rule leaves
only the original signs(27), alongside the displayed coordinate
domains (nonnegative steps/factor diagonals and theta in[0,1]). It
does not assert that the unit-quadratic condition alone makes a word
feasible. Every failure of another original constraint appears among
the actual Lambda signs for all choices of the remaining coordinates.

## 9. Exact strict length-five point in the new variables

All constants and all30 entries of the point are given in Appendix A,
with their complete original proof. To state its new coordinates,
take exactly

    a_seed=sqrt2,
    b_seed=(-a_seed+sqrt(10+8a_seed))/2,
    t_seed=99/100, delta_seed=1/100,
    X=t_seed*a_seed, Y=t_seed*b_seed, C_seed=1+X+Y,
    d_seed=(a_seed-1)C_seed-delta_seed,
    h_seed=(X,Y,1+d_seed,X,Y), A_seed=2C_seed+d_seed.

Put Pseed=(1-X)(1-Y). Its unit-curvature column is explicitly

    v_seed=(1,1-X,Pseed,-d_seed*Pseed,
             -d_seed*Pseed*(1-X),-d_seed*Pseed^2)^T,
    chi_seed=1-A_seed^2*d_seed^2*Pseed^4.               (30)

Use n=6 in(9)-(12) to obtain E_seed,q_seed,F_seed,nu_seed. To make
every entry an explicit algebraic expression, the five entries of
E_seed^T v_seed are

    [sum_(i=0)^j (v_seed)_i-(j+1)(v_seed)_(j+1)]
                       /sqrt((j+1)(j+2)), j=0,...,4.  (31)

Divide these by sqrt(nu_seed) to get w_seed, and use exactly(12).
In particular w_seed,0=X/sqrt(2nu_seed)>0 and the frame denominator
exceeds1. There is no unspecified choice of orthogonal basis.

Let Lambda_seed be the complete table(A23), L_seed its original
Laplacian, M_seed=D_seed+sym(L_seed T_seed), and
K_seed=skew(L_seed T_seed). The entire factor-coordinate point is

    alpha_seed=chi_seed/nu_seed,
    z_seed=F_seed^T M_seed q_seed/sqrt(alpha_seed),
    S_seed=F_seed^T M_seed F_seed-z_seed z_seed^T,
    C_factor,seed=Chol_psd(S_seed),
    Xi_seed=F_seed^T K_seed F_seed.                    (32)

Here Chol_psd means the finite fully specified construction in
Section5. At this point all four pivots are strictly positive, so
one may equivalently use, in order j=1,...,4,

    C_jj=sqrt(S_jj-sum_(k<j)C_jk^2),
    C_ij=(S_ij-sum_(k<j)C_ik C_jk)/C_jj, i>j,
    C_ij=0, i<j.                                      (33)

These equations specify all ten factor entries by a finite nest of
arithmetic and positive square roots of the stated seed constants.
Together with four entries of z_seed and the six entries Xi_ab for
1<=a<b<=4, they express all twenty coefficient coordinates exactly.
They are not a request for a nonlinear solver or a matrix-completion
oracle. None of these expressions has been numerically evaluated in
this round.

To justify every denominator and the branch selection, Appendix A
proves, on the entire original six-gradient space,

    M_seed >=(1/900)(I-ee^T/6),
    M_seed <(7/2)(I-ee^T/6) on e-perp,
    Lambda_seed,ij>=1/10000000 for every i!=j.          (34)

Since v_0-v_1=X>1, the inequality
(u_0-u_1)^2<=2||u||^2 gives nu_seed>=X^2/2>1/2. Equations(8),(34)
give chi_seed>=nu_seed/900>1/1800 and
1/900<=alpha_seed<7/2. Thus the positive branch applies.
In the orthonormal coordinates[q_seed F_seed] the full block is
at least I/900. For any y in R^4, minimizing that block quadratic
over its first coordinate gives y^T S_seed y>=||y||^2/900.
The same reasoning at each following Schur pivot gives

    S_seed>=I_4/900,       (C_factor,seed)_jj>=1/30.    (35)

Hence(33) divides only by positive quantities with explicit lower
bounds. The upper bound on S_seed is also below(7/2)I, since the
subtracted outer product is PSD, although no numerical conditioning
claim for the whole coordinate map follows from this.

For exact reconstruction, (21),(23),(32) first give M=M_seed. By(20),
the skew parameters(32) give K=K_seed. Equation(16) then gives
L=L_seed and(26) gives every original Lambda_seed entry, including
the tiny entries and all diagonals. Thus no unverified existence of
missing entries is being hidden in the new coordinates.

If the optional physical coordinates(29) are wanted at the seed,
use prefix capacity Bcap=A_seed-Y and product
Ppre=-d_seed*Pseed*(1-X), which is nonzero. Formula(29), with
theta_seed=(Y-lo)/(hi-lo), specifies its last physical coordinate.
Equation(34) implies chi_seed>0 and Y>0, so 0<theta_seed<1.

The capacity remains

    19/2<A_seed<48763/5000<49/5<Gamma5_c107.            (36)

This point supplies a strict chart start, not an improving seed.
The new representation itself supplies no route across the threshold.
The repeated X,Y coordinates and their path relations specify only this
one starting point. They place no restriction on the general physical
coordinates or the free factor/skew coordinates of the representation.

## 10. Finite implementation recipe: specified, not executed

Forward generation, with exact arithmetic as a mathematical definition:

1. Choose r and an original nonnegative word h, either directly with
   chi>=0 or by(29). Form T,D,omega,A and the product column(6).
2. If r=0, return the unique zero arrays. If h=0, use(9),(24).
3. Otherwise compute nu, q and the explicit frame(9)-(12). If chi<0,
   return impossible for the original PSD cone. If chi>0 use(21) with
   arbitrary z and lower triangular C; if chi=0 use(22) and no z.
4. Choose every upper skew entry of Xi freely, reflect it with a minus
   sign, and set its diagonal to zero. Form(14)-(16), using(5).
5. Recover every Lambda by(26) or its individual expressions in
   Section7. Test every ordered sign(27). All equalities and full PSD
   already hold algebraically; passing signs gives an original point.
6. An implementation should still reconstruct the original T,L,M,
   flows and all entries independently for its arithmetic readback.
   In floating-point arithmetic those checks concern implementation
   error; they do not authorize clipping, averaging, jitter, or
   identification of any zero-step formal gradients.

Inverse extraction from an actual original cone point:

1. Retain its full arrays and form its actual M and skew(LT).
2. Use the h=0 branch(24) if needed: factor E^TME and take
   Xi=E^T skew(L) E. Otherwise form(9)-(12).
3. For chi>0, take z=F^TMq/sqrt(chi/nu), factor the full Schur
   residual as in(32), and take Xi=F^T skew(LT)F.
4. For chi=0, factor F^TMF, omit z, and use the same Xi formula.
5. For every zero Cholesky pivot use its proved zero residual row,
   not a hidden inverse or a perturbation. Reconstruct(16),(26).

All operations are finite sums/products, explicit reciprocals of
proved nonzero scalars, square roots of proved nonnegative scalars,
and stated equality/sign branches. There is no eigenvector oracle,
unknown matrix completion, arbitrary pseudoinverse, optimization,
or fixed positive-rank assumption. The recipe is UNEXECUTED.

## Appendix A. Complete joint proof of the dense starting point

The following is the full analytical seed derivation, incorporated here
as part of this joint draft rather than used as an unverified predecessor.
Its notation is local to this appendix: in particular rho=a-1 is a seed
constant, whereas the unit-curvature slack in the main text is chi.
Main-text references (Ak) mean equation(k) in this appendix. The prior
round resource/handoff narrative is not part of this mathematical appendix.

### A.1. Original notation and the concrete word

Normalize smoothness to1. All six formal gradients g_0,...,g_5 remain
independent variables. For a five-step word h, put s=sum h_i, A=1+s,
and x_i=x_0-sum_(j<i)h_j g_j. The ordered residual and primitive
potential, in the accepted original normalization, are

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,
    V_h=2sum_(i<5)h_i(f_i-f_5)
          +sum_(i<5)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-s(s+1)||g_5||^2.

Every actual smooth convex trajectory has Q_ij<=0. Set

    T_ii=1, T_it=h_t for t<i, T_it=0 for t>i,
    L_flow=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda,
    omega=(-2h_0,-2h_1,-2h_2,-2h_3,-2h_4,2s)^T,
    D_prim=diag(h_i(2-h_i) for i<5,-s(s+2)),
    M_prim=D_prim+sym(L_flow T),  sym(B)=(B+B^T)/2.

The accepted coefficient identity is

    L_flow^T e=omega  ==>  V_h+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij
                          =<M_prim,Gram(g_0,...,g_5)>.    (1)

Thus the required object is in the original coordinates, with no
rescaling of Lambda, physical steps, or M_prim. The project retains
W=2R. The ordinary benchmark symbols retain their accepted meanings;
in particular C below is a child capacity, not the sequence C_n.

Define exact algebraic numbers

    a=sqrt(2), lambda=1+a, rho=a-1,
    b=(-a+sqrt(10+8a))/2,  p=a+b.

They satisfy a^2=2, rho=1/lambda, and

    b^2+a b=2(1+a),     p b=2lambda.                     (2)

For the joint derivation allow 99/100<=t<1, and set

    delta=1-t, X=t a, Y=t b, C=1+X+Y,
    d=rho C-delta,
    h=(X,Y,1+d,X,Y),
    A=2C+d=lambda C-delta=a+t(lambda p+1).              (3)

The concrete output means precisely t=99/100, delta=1/100 in every
formula below. There are no parameters to be fitted or solved for.

Here are elementary strict bounds used throughout:

    7/5<a<71/50,  8/5<b<161/100,  3<p<303/100,
    397/100<C<403/100,
    1<X<71/50, 1<Y<161/100,
    3/2<d<17/10,   5/2<1+d<27/10,
    39/100<d/C<21/50,
    (29/50)C<C-d<(61/100)C<3.                          (4)

For completeness, F(u)=u^2+a u-2(1+a) is increasing for u>0.
F(8/5)=14/25-(2/5)a<0. Also
F(161/100)=5921/10000-(39/100)a>383/10000>0.
This proves the bounds on b and then p>3 and p<303/100.
The bounds on C,X,Y follow from t>=99/100. Since
rho>2/5, rho<21/50, delta<=1/100 and C>397/100,

    d>(2/5)(397/100)-1/100>3/2,
    d<(21/50)(403/100)<17/10,
    d/C>2/5-1/397>39/100.

The remaining bounds in(4) follow. Every denominator below is positive.

### A.2. Joint derivation of the full baseline array

First consider the two-step child (X,Y). Put S=X+Y=C-1 and

    u=2S(1+X)/Y=2(p/b)(1+t a),
    v=2SC/Y=2(p/b)C,
    w0=2-X^2=2delta(1+t),
    w1=(S/Y)[2-Y^2+(2-Y)X]
       =2(p/b)delta(1+lambda t).                       (5)

The full child array, in its three original coordinates, is

             0          1          2
    row0     0          2          0
    row1     2(1+X)     0          u
    row2     0          v          0.

Its divergence is (-2X,-2Y,2S), since v-u=2S. Direct substitution in
the original matrix formula gives all its entries as

    [[w0,-w0,0],[-w0,w0+w1,-w1],[0,-w1,w1]].           (6)

For example M_12=[v(Y-1)-u]/2=-w1 and M_22=-S(S+2)+v=w1;
M_00=w0, M_01=-w0 and M_02=0 follow from the first row of the array.
The middle diagonal is
Y(2-Y)+2(1+X)+u(1-Y)=w0+w1: here uY=2S(1+X) and
w1=u-S^2 show the equality by direct expansion.
To obtain the second equality for w1 in(5), expand its bracket as
2+2t a-t^2 b(a+b)=2[1+t a-lambda t^2]
=2(1-t)(1+lambda t), using(2).

Next define

    c=(C+d)/(C-d),  k=1+2c=(3C+d)/(C-d),
    Delta=d(2C+d)-C^2=-delta(2a C-delta),
    tau=-Delta/(C-d)=delta(2a C-delta)/(C-d),
    eta=2tau.                                        (7)

All c,k,tau,eta are positive. Concatenate two copies of(X,Y) with
bridge step1+d. The bridge gradient is g_2 and terminal gradient g_5.
For this paragraph write P=2,Z=5, beta_3=X,beta_4=Y, and let

    E=sum_(j=3,4)beta_j Q_(j,P)+Q_(Z,P)+d Q_(P,Z)
                                      +sum_(j=3,4)beta_j Q_(j,Z).

The value part of E is

    2sum_j beta_j f_j+(d-C)f_P+(2-C-d)f_Z,

and its gradient part after the actual GD substitutions is

    sum_j beta_j(1-beta_j)||g_j||^2
       +[(C-d)/2-d^2]||g_P||^2+(C+d)||g_Z||^2/2.       (8)

Here is the mixed-entry check for(8). In each Q_(j,P), the position
difference is (1+d)g_P+sum_(3<=l<j)beta_l g_l. In Q_(j,Z), it is
-sum_(j<=l<=4)beta_l g_l. For the pair of tail indices3,4 the two
weighted position contributions cancel. Bridge-to-tail terms from
sum beta_j Q_(j,P) equal d sum beta_j<g_j,g_P>, canceled by the
position part of d Q_(P,Z). Tail-to-terminal terms from
sum beta_j Q_(j,Z) equal -sum beta_j<g_j,g_Z>, canceled by Q_(Z,P).
The bridge-to-terminal contributions of Q_(Z,P) and d Q_(P,Z) are
d<g_P,g_Z> and -d<g_P,g_Z>. These exhaust all mixed pairs.
Collecting diagonal terms gives(8).

It now follows coefficient by coefficient that

    V_h=V_left+k V_right-2c E
                         +c Delta(||g_Z||^2-||g_P||^2). (9)

Indeed c(C-d)=C+d matches the value coefficients. For the bridge
diagonal use c Delta=k d^2-C^2; for the terminal diagonal the required
identity is
-(2C+d-1)(2C+d)+k(C-1)C+c(C+d)=c Delta.
Both follow by inserting k=(3C+d)/(C-d). The tail diagonals are
-2c beta_j(1-beta_j), exactly as in(8). Thus no mixed coefficient is
being suppressed in(9).

Add eta to BOTH original coefficients(P,Z) and(Z,P). Set

    U_L=g_0-g_1, V_L=g_1-g_2,
    U_R=g_3-g_4, V_R=g_4-g_5,
    zeta=g_2-g_5, J=X U_R+(X+Y)V_R.

The exact identity

    Q_(P,Z)+Q_(Z,P)
      =-d||zeta||^2-(C+d)<zeta,g_Z>-<zeta,J>

follows from x_P-x_Z=(1+d)g_P+Xg_3+Yg_4. Since
tau=-c Delta/(C+d), adding eta times this identity to the last term
in(9) gives tau[(C-d)||zeta||^2-2<zeta,J>].

Consequently the baseline full array Lambda^0 is

          0          1       2         3          4          5
    row0  0          2       0         0          0          0
    row1  2(1+X)     0       u         0          0          0
    row2  0          v       0         0          0          2cd+eta
    row3  0          0       2cX       0          2k         2cX
    row4  0          0       2cY       2k(1+X)    0          ku+2cY
    row5  0          0       2c+eta    0          kv         0.       (10)

Its exact full remainder is

    M^0[g]=w0||U_L||^2+w1||V_L||^2
          +k(w0||U_R||^2+w1||V_R||^2)
          +tau[(C-d)||zeta||^2-2<zeta,J>].             (11)

This also gives M^0 e=0 directly. For an independent full flow check,
the first two divergences of(10) are -2X,-2Y. The other four are

    v-u+2c(d-C)=-2(1+d),
    4cX-2kX=-2X,
    4cY+2kX+k(u-v)=-2Y,
    k(v-u)+2c(2-C-d)=2(2C+d-1)=2s.                   (12)

Thus every one of the six original flow equations is satisfied.

### A.3. A quantitative full baseline margin

Complete the square in(11). Define

    R=X^2/w0+(X+Y)^2/w1=Rbar/delta,
    Rbar=t^2[1/(1+t)+lambda/(1+lambda t)],
    q=tau/(C-d), sigma=k-qR,
    E0=zeta-J/(C-d).

The identity for R follows from a^2=2 and pb=2lambda. Both summands
in Rbar are increasing for t>=0, by differentiating t^2/(1+t) and
lambda t^2/(1+lambda t). Therefore

    Rbar<=1/2+lambda/(1+lambda)=lambda/2.              (13)

For our whole parameter interval sigma>5/2. To see this, (4) gives

    k>(3+39/100)/(1-39/100)=339/61>11/2,
    qR=(2a C-delta)Rbar/(C-d)^2
       <(7/2)C/[(29/50)^2 C^2]
       =8750/(841C)<17500/5887<3,                    (14)

where a lambda=2+a<7/2 and C>7/2 were used. Hence k-qR>5/2.

The exact sum of squares is

    M^0[g]=w0||U_L||^2+w1||V_L||^2
          +sigma(w0||U_R||^2+w1||V_R||^2)
          +q||(X+Y)sqrt(w0/w1)U_R-Xsqrt(w1/w0)V_R||^2
          +tau(C-d)||E0||^2.                         (15)

Expanding the fourth term uses
R(w0||U_R||^2+w1||V_R||^2)-||J||^2
=||(X+Y)sqrt(w0/w1)U_R-Xsqrt(w1/w0)V_R||^2.
Thus(15) is an identity on the entire original space.

We now turn it into an explicit Euclidean margin. From(4)-(5),(14),

    w0>3delta, w1>3delta,
    sigma w0>(15/2)delta, sigma w1>(15/2)delta,
    tau(C-d)=delta(2a C-delta)>11delta.               (16)

Also

    alpha=X/(C-d)<7100/11513<2/3,
    beta=(X+Y)/(C-d)<15150/11513<4/3.

Since zeta=E0+alpha U_R+beta V_R, Cauchy--Schwarz gives

    ||zeta||^2<=3||E0||^2+(4/3)||U_R||^2+(16/3)||V_R||^2.

Define the unweighted tree energy

    E_tree=||U_L||^2+||V_L||^2+||zeta||^2
                                +||V_R||^2+||U_R||^2.

It is at most
||U_L||^2+||V_L||^2+(7/3)||U_R||^2+(19/3)||V_R||^2+3||E0||^2.
Each of these coefficients is dominated by its corresponding
coefficient in(16) after division by delta. Dropping the fourth
nonnegative square in(15) consequently gives

    M^0[g]>=delta E_tree.                            (17)

The tree is the path with vertex order v=(0,1,2,5,4,3). If g is a
scalar vector with sum g_i=0, then

    ||g||^2=(1/6)sum_(i<j)(g_i-g_j)^2.

Bound each difference by Cauchy--Schwarz along its path. The total
coefficients of the five successive squared edge differences are
15,24,27,24,15. For example the middle one is
sum_(i=0,1,2)sum_(j=3,4,5)(j-i)=27; the other four follow from
the same finite sums. Therefore ||g||^2<=(9/2)E_tree, and

    g^T M^0 g >= (2delta/9)||g||^2   for g perpendicular to e. (18)

This argument is also valid coordinate by coordinate for vector
gradients. No equality between two original formal gradients is used.

### A.4. Exact tree compensation that fills every missing coefficient

This is the new construction step. Let e_i be the original coordinate
vectors, retain v=(0,1,2,5,4,3), and put

    z=T e=(1,1+X,C,C+1+d,A-Y,A)^T,
    Z=sum_i z_i=2+C+3A-2Y,
    K=6I-ee^T,
    b_l=e_(v_l)-e_(v_(l+1))             for l=0,...,4,
    S_l={v_0,...,v_l},
    F_l=sum_(i in S_l)(Kz)_i,
    w_l^tree=F_l/(z_(v_l)-z_(v_(l+1))),
    gamma_l=1-w_l^tree.                              (19)

The edge differences in their stated order are

    -X, -Y, -(C+d), Y, X.

They are all nonzero, with absolute value greater than1. To eliminate
any implicit linear solve, the five weights are explicitly

    w_0^tree=(Z-6)/X,
    w_1^tree=[2Z-6(2+X)]/Y,
    w_2^tree=[3Z-6(2+X+C)]/(C+d),
    w_3^tree=[6(2+X+C+A)-4Z]/Y,
    w_4^tree=[Z-6(C+1+d)]/X.                         (20)

Define the symmetric matrix

    H=K-sum_(l=0)^4 w_l^tree b_l b_l^T.              (21)

We claim H e=H z=0, as exact identities. The first is immediate
from the displayed matrices. For the second, set F_(-1)=F_5=0.
At vertex v_l, the vector sum_l F_l b_l has entry F_l-F_(l-1)
=(Kz)_(v_l); at the last vertex this remains true because sum_i(Kz)_i=0.
But (b_l b_l^T)z=(z_(v_l)-z_(v_(l+1)))b_l, so(19) proves H z=0.
This is a full coordinate check; it does not infer preservation of
the kernel merely from conservation of total flow.

Choose the exact positive fill size

    epsilon=delta/100000.                            (22)

For unordered tree pairs {v_l,v_(l+1)}, add epsilon gamma_l to BOTH
ordered coefficients. For every other distinct pair, add epsilon.
Equivalently, with all entries in original index order, the final
array is the following complete specification:

         0                     1                    2                    3                    4                    5
 row0    0                     2+eps*gamma0          eps                  eps                  eps                  eps
 row1    2(1+X)+eps*gamma0      0                    u+eps*gamma1         eps                  eps                  eps
 row2    eps                   v+eps*gamma1          0                    eps                  eps                  2cd+eta+eps*gamma2
 row3    eps                   eps                  2cX+eps              0                    2k+eps*gamma4         2cX+eps
 row4    eps                   eps                  2cY+eps              2k(1+X)+eps*gamma4    0                    ku+2cY+eps*gamma3
 row5    eps                   eps                  2c+eta+eps*gamma2     eps                  kv+eps*gamma3         0.           (23)

Here eps is epsilon and gamma0,...,gamma4 mean gamma_0,...,gamma_4
from(19)-(20). In particular the diagonal is exactly zero.

Let L^0=diag(Lambda^0 e)-Lambda^0. The signed symmetric change in(23)
has Laplacian epsilon H, so the resulting ORIGINAL quantities are

    L_flow=L^0+epsilon H,
    M_prim=M^0+epsilon sym(H T).                     (24)

Since H^T e=H e=0, (12) proves L_flow^T e=omega exactly. Moreover

    sym(H T)e=(H z+T^T H e)/2=0.                     (25)

Thus the full common-gradient kernel equation is also exact. Equations
(11),(20)-(24) give the entire M_prim, including every mixed entry.

It would not be valid to add arbitrary positive entries without this
compensation. For example a nonzero symmetric nonnegative addition
with Laplacian N and N z=0 would satisfy
z^T N z=sum_(i<j)Nweight_ij(z_i-z_j)^2=0. Here every z_i is distinct,
so every added weight would have to vanish. The signed compensation
on already positive tree edges is essential. The final coefficients,
as opposed to their increments, are all positive by the next section.

### A.5. All signs and the full perturbed spectral margin

First bound every tree weight in(19). Because K is the complete-graph
Laplacian,

    F_l=sum_(i in S_l,j not in S_l)(z_i-z_j).

Every difference has absolute value at most s=A-1<9. There are at
most9 crossing unordered pairs, since |S_l|(6-|S_l|)<=9. Hence

    |F_l|<81,  |w_l^tree|<81,  |gamma_l|<82.          (26)

Every baseline coefficient on a tree pair, in both directions, is
at least2. To check the bridge pair, c>1 and d>3/2 imply2cd+eta>3,
and2c+eta>2. On the two child pairs this follows directly from(5)
and(10). Since epsilon<=1/10000000, each final tree coefficient is

    Lambda_ij >=2-82epsilon >epsilon.               (27)

Every non-tree pair has a nonnegative baseline coefficient and is
increased by epsilon. Thus ALL30 final ordered coefficients obey
Lambda_ij>=epsilon. This includes edges that were positive in only
one direction in the baseline; neither direction is deleted.

For explicit upper ranges, c<71/29<5/2, k<6 and tau<5delta, so
eta<10delta<=1/10. From p/b<303/160 one obtains

    u<10, v<16,
    2cd+eta<10, 2c+eta<6,
    2cX<8, 2cY<9, 2k(1+X)<30,
    ku+2cY<69, kv<96.

Together with(26) these prove, uniformly on the stated t interval,

    epsilon<=Lambda_ij<97 for i!=j,
    |Lambda_ij-Lambda^0_ij|<82epsilon.               (28)

For instance v<16 follows from
2(303/160)(403/100)=244218/16000<16, and
tau<2a delta/(2-a)<(142/29)delta<5delta.
These are proved ranges, not the coordinate boxes of any earlier
numerical search. The original array has not been rescaled to fit
such boxes.

Next control the ENTIRE matrix perturbation. All operator norms below
are Euclidean. K has norm6, each b_l b_l^T has norm2, and(26) gives

    ||H||<6+5*81*2=816.

There are six diagonal entries1 and fifteen strictly lower triangular
entries in T; every physical step is less than3. Consequently

    ||T||<=||T||_F<sqrt(6+15*9)=sqrt141<12,
    ||sym(H T)||<=||H|| ||T||<9792<10000.             (29)

For every g perpendicular to e, (18),(22),(24),(29) give

    g^T M_prim g > (2delta/9-delta/10)||g||^2
                   =(11delta/90)||g||^2
                   >(delta/9)||g||^2.              (30)

Equation(25) and M^0 e=0 then imply positivity on the FULL R^6,
with kernel exactly span{e}. In particular, no omitted off-diagonal
entry, hidden gradient identification, or principal-block shortcut is
needed for(30).

A conservative upper bound also quantifies conditioning in this
coordinate system. In(11), w0<4delta and w1<14delta. The norms of
the four child squared-difference matrices are2, and k<6, giving
an upper norm bound252delta for their sum. The vector defining J
is (0,0,0,X,Y,-X-Y), whose norm is less than4:
X<3/2, Y<2, X+Y<31/10 give its squared norm less than16.
The vector defining zeta has norm sqrt2. Since C-d<3 and tau<5delta,
the norm of the last part of(11) is less than

    tau[2(C-d)+2sqrt2*4]<18tau<90delta.

It follows that ||M^0||<342delta. Adding(29) costs less than delta/10,
so, conservatively, ||M_prim||<350delta. With P=I-ee^T/6 this proves

    (delta/9)P <= M_prim <=350delta P,               (31)

where both inequalities are strict on e-perp. The condition number
on that complement is therefore less than3150. This is a bound for
this matrix in the stated Euclidean coordinates, not a claim about
the condition number of an entire QCQP or a nonlinear solver.

Let B be the leading five-by-five principal block of M_prim. For
y in R^5 and g=(y,0),
||Pg||^2=||y||^2-(sum y_i)^2/6>=||y||^2/6. Hence

    B >= (delta/54)I_5.                             (32)

At t=99/100, equations(22),(28),(31),(32) give exactly the advertised
margins epsilon=1/10000000, full eigenvalue bound1/900 on e-perp,
upper bound7/2 there, and leading-block bound1/5400.

### A.6. Capacity, nonbasic status, and exact limits of the result

The concrete capacity is exactly

    A=a+(99/100)[(1+a)(a+b)+1].                      (33)

Using a>7/5, lambda>12/5 and p>3 gives
A>7/5+(99/100)(41/5)=4759/500>19/2. On the other hand

    A<lambda(1+p)<(121/50)(403/100)
                        =48763/5000<49/5,
    Gamma5_c107-49/5=78807/806000>0.                 (34)

Thus the sufficient hypothesis of accepted4c92d3fc4eb6153e is NOT
satisfied. Nothing here asserts an improving seed or a result on
the original asymptotic minimax target.

Here is the promised nearby justified endpoint. At t=1 the word is

    h_*=(a,b,1+rho(1+a+b),a,b),
    A_*=lambda(1+a+b).

It is an ordinary basic primitive construction under the accepted
join law: a=sqrt2 joins two empty words, the next bridge b has excess
b-1 satisfying (b-1)(lambda+b)=lambda by(2), and the final bridge
excess rho C_* solves d(2C_*+d)=C_*^2, with C_*=1+a+b. These checks
justify the endpoint without using any unverified path claim.
At our chosen t,

    ||h-h_*||_infinity
       <=delta max{a,b,1+rho p}<23/1000,
    0<A_*-A=delta(lambda p+1)<41663/500000<17/200.    (35)

In contrast, h itself is not a basic primitive tree. In every nonempty
primitive tree built from empty leaves the first step is at least
sqrt2. This follows by induction: a nonempty left child preserves
its first step; with an empty left child, right capacity C_right>=1,
and bridge excess d_right solving
d_right(1+C_right+d_right)=C_right, substitution of rho gives

    C_right-rho(1+C_right+rho)
       =(1-rho)(C_right-1)>=0.

The left side of the bridge equation is strictly increasing in its
positive unknown, so d_right>=rho. Hence that first step is at least
1+rho=sqrt2. Our first step X=(99/100)sqrt2 is strictly smaller.
This establishes the stated nonbasic status in exactly that class;
it is not a claim about all other possible composition formalisms.

The construction proves two initialization properties: every original
sign constraint has a positive quantitative slack, and the full
remainder has a quantitative spectral margin after removing its
unavoidable common-gradient kernel. The upper bound in(31) supplies
a conservative condition-number bound on that complement. It does
not prove faster numerical convergence, explain why C112 failed,
make an infeasible word feasible, or find a path to an improving
endpoint. Any future numerical usefulness is an initialization
hypothesis that has not been tested in this round.

The bidirected tree is only the set of entries used for compensation.
The FINAL array is dense on every ordered off-diagonal pair; no
support restriction is asserted to be lossless. The final full
remainder is nonzero and has the maximum possible rank5. No reflection
or multiplier-symmetry restriction is imposed on the original cone;
only the added correction is symmetric.

# Positive off-diagonal entries and exact chronological decimation

Status: **unverified proof draft**, sixth launch, first continuation round.
No kernel evaluation, optimizer, installation, or fact submission was used.
The uniform positive-semidefinite conclusion and the original minimax Goal
remain open. This document proves some of the actual coefficient claims;
it does not assume the remaining thirteen-band estimates.

## 1. Definitions and the coefficient that is being estimated

Fix an integer m>=128 and keep epsilon=1/m fixed throughout. Set

 lambda=1+sqrt(2), c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=(1-epsilon)/c,
 d_k=1+a*c^k  (k>=0), H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,d_j,H_j).

The extension of d_k past m-1 is only a coefficient device. The actual
queried word is H_m with its last d_0 removed. Write h_i for its steps,
s_i=sum_(r<i)h_r, and s'=s_i+h_i when discussing a single step.

On the infinite original ruler word define B_00=1 and

 B_(i+1),k=(1-h_i/d_k)B_ik+(h_i/d_(k-1))B_i,(k-1),

with a zero second term at k=0. For each occupied state k, h_i<=d_k:
the first occurrence of d_j kills state j, and all smaller states have
already died. This also proves B_ik>=0, sum_k B_ik=1, and
B_ik=0 for i>=2^k or k>i.

For arbitrary formal vectors y_k put g_i=sum_k v_ik*y_k, where
v_ik=B_ik/d_k. The row-finite symmetric matrix E is defined by

 sum_(k,l)E_kl<y_k,y_l>
 =sum_i [2s_(i+1)h_i<g_(i+1),g_i>
       +s_(i+1)||g_(i+1)-g_i||^2+h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2].   (1)

Each coefficient is a finite sum: a term containing state k occurs by
the step immediately before query2^k. In particular E_kl=0 for k<l
and l>2^k. The m-principal block is the actual finite residual kernel.
The coefficient convention in (1) means that an off-diagonal entry
appears twice in the quadratic form.

## 2. Two elementary bounds for the actual chronological word

For the elementary parameter bounds used below, lambda>12/5 and
lambda<29/12 give 12/5<c<39/16. The upper comparison follows from
(29/12)(129/128)=3741/1536<39/16. Consequently
a>(127/128)/(39/16)=127/312>2/5. Moreover

 d_0=sqrt(2)-2epsilon/[lambda(1+epsilon)],
 d_2=2+sqrt(2)-lambda*epsilon^2,

so 7/5<d_0<3/2 and d_2<7/2; for the first lower bound use
sqrt(2)>141/100 and 2epsilon/[lambda(1+epsilon)]<5/768.
In particular 2<c<3 and 1<d_0<2. Also

                         d_(k+1)>2d_k  (k>=2).            (2)

Indeed d_(k+1)-2d_k=(c-2)a*c^k-1. At k=2 the quantity
before subtracting1 is (1-epsilon)c(c-2), and

 c(c-2)=1+2lambda(lambda-1)epsilon+lambda^2 epsilon^2
        >1+6epsilon.

Consequently (1-epsilon)c(c-2)>1+5epsilon-6epsilon^2>1.
The expression increases with k, proving (2).

Second, every original physical step satisfies

                             h_i<s_i+2.                  (3)

Let S_j=sum H_j and C_j=S_j+2-d_j. Then C_0=2-d_0>0.
The recurrences S_(j+1)=2S_j+d_j and
d_(j+1)=c*d_j-(c-1) give the exact identity

 C_(j+1)=2C_j+(3-c)(d_j-1)>0.

The prefix before the FIRST d_j is H_j, so (3) holds there. Every later
occurrence has a larger elapsed clock and the same step size. This proves
(3) at every position, without changing the enclosing epsilon.

There is a sharper envelope that does exclude the recorded swap foil.
Put rho=c-2 and gamma=rho-a=c-1-d_0>0. The exact word-sum formula gives

 rho(S_j+1)+1-d_j=gamma*2^j.

Thus every original occurrence h_i=d_j obeys

 rho(s_i+1)+1-h_i=gamma*2^j+rho(s_i-S_j)>=0.              (3a)

If the third and fourth steps are exchanged, the first d_2 has
s_i=d_0+d_1 instead of S_2=2d_0+d_1. Its slack in (3a) becomes
4gamma-rho*d_0<13/128-14/25<0. Here rho>2/5, d_0>7/5, and
gamma<(13/4)epsilon, as proved explicitly in Section7 below. This
distinguishes the original chronology from that particular foil.
Equation (3a) alone is not asserted to prove b_i or u_i.

## 3. Nonnegative off-diagonal entries, term by term

For a fixed step h=h_i, write v_k=v_ik and v_(-1)=0. The scaled birth
recurrence is

 v'_k=v_k+(h/d_k)(v_(k-1)-v_k).

For k<l, expansion of that step's contribution to E_kl gives exactly

 C_kl*v_k*v_l
 +[s'h^2(d_l-1)/(d_k*d_l)]v_(k-1)*v_l
 +[s'h^2(d_k-1)/(d_k*d_l)]v_k*v_(l-1)
 +[s'h^2/(d_k*d_l)]v_(k-1)*v_(l-1),                     (4)

where

 C_kl=h[2s'+1-h-s'h(1/d_k+1/d_l-1/(d_k*d_l))].           (5)

To check (4), the first term of (1) contributes
s'h(v'_k*v_l+v_k*v'_l), the second contributes
s'(v'_k-v_k)(v'_l-v_l), and the third contributes
h(1-h)v_k*v_l. Expanding these three expressions gives (4)-(5).

The last three coefficients in (4) are nonnegative. Suppose k>=2.
If v_k*v_l=0, the possibly signed coefficient C_kl costs nothing.
Otherwise state k is occupied, so h<=d_k. Equation (2) gives
d_l>=d_(k+1)>2d_k and hence

 h(1/d_k+1/d_l-1/(d_k*d_l))<3/2.

It follows from (3) that

 C_kl>h(s'/2+1-h)=(h/2)(s_i+2-h)>0.                     (6)

Thus every physical step contributes a nonnegative amount to E_kl
when 2<=k<l. This proof keeps the signed terms of the original step
until they combine into C_kl; it does not take their absolute values.

For k=0,1, support leaves only E_01 and E_12. The first two steps,
whose unscaled state vectors are successively V_0,V_1,V_2, give

 E_01=(d_0-1)/d_1>0,
 E_12=(d_0+d_1)(d_1-1)/(d_1*d_2)>0.

These calculations and (4)-(6) prove

                    E_kl>=0 for every k!=l.              (7)

In particular the actual second-neighbor weights obey e_0=e_1=0 and

                    e_k=E_(k,k+2)>0 for k>=2.             (8)

For strictness, just before the first d_k both B_ik and B_i,(k+1)
are positive when k>=2. For B_ik, use the path that takes a birth on
each of its first k steps and then stays. All those birth probabilities
are positive, and every subsequent step before the first d_k is smaller
than d_k. For B_i,(k+1), take the first k+1 births and then stay; this
is possible because k+1<=2^k-1, and all later pre-d_k steps are smaller
than d_(k+1). At the d_k step, the third term in (4) with l=k+2 is
strictly positive: s'>0, d_k>1, v_k>0, v_(k+1)>0. This proves (8).

No uniform lower bound such as e_k>=1/15 is proved by this strictness
argument: the displayed positive path probabilities have not been
estimated uniformly.

## 4. Exact consequences for the unresolved row margins

For all nonnegative indices define

 A_i=E_(i,i+1), e_i=E_(i,i+2),
 b_i=A_i-2(e_(i-1)+e_i),
 u_i=E_ii-A_(i-1)-A_i+e_(i-2)+e_i
                         -sum_(|l-i|>=3)|E_il|,

with negative subscripts equal to zero. Let F_i=sum_l E_il be the
finite unweighted row sum, and ellhat_i=sum_l E_il*d_l the constant
load obtained by polarizing (1) against the constant gradient.

Equation (7) removes all absolute values from the far off-diagonal
sum. Therefore the exact full-row identity is

                     u_i=2E_ii+2(e_(i-2)+e_i)-F_i.         (9)

In particular a proof of u_i does not need to identify alternating
signs of individual far entries. It still needs signed cancellation
between the diagonal, the neighboring entries, and the row sum.
Nonnegative entries do not imply either b_i>=2/15 or u_i>=0.

This does not discard a terminal coefficient. In the inherited finite
decomposition, the triple starting at m-2 remains the square
e_(m-2)||y_(m-2)+2y_(m-1)||^2. Its coefficient now has the sign in (8).
The last diagonal remains

 u_(m-1)+sum_(l>=m+2)E_(m-1,l)+b_(m-1)+e_(m-1).

Neither its required quantitative lower bound nor the completed
constant-column remainder follows from (7) alone.

## 5. A scalar constant-load formula with the signed difference retained

For this section all sums run over the infinite coefficient word, but
every state-specific sum is finite. Set

 mu_ik=h_i B_ik/d_k,
 L_k=sum_(r=0)^k d_r,
 M_k=sum_i h_i*mu_ik,
 N_k=sum_i s_(i+1)*h_i*mu_ik,

and put M_(-1)=N_(-1)=0. The flow equation is
B_(i+1),k-B_ik=mu_i,(k-1)-mu_ik. Summing it proves
sum_i mu_ik=1 for each transient state. Weighting it by s_(i+1)
and summing by parts gives

 sum_i s_(i+1)(mu_ik-mu_i,(k-1))=sum_i h_i B_ik=d_k.

Starting with a zero predecessor moment shows
sum_i s_(i+1)mu_ik=L_k. Thus M_k and N_k are respectively the mean
exit step and the mean product of exit clock and exit step for state k.

Polarizing (1) against the constant gradient gives

 ellhat_k=sum_i h_i[(s_i+1)v_ik+s_(i+1)v_(i+1),k].         (10)

For example, the first term in (1) contributes
s'h(v_ik+v_(i+1),k), its increment-square term contributes zero,
and its last term contributes h(1-h)v_ik. Their sum is (10).
Substitute the birth recurrence into (10), then use the two occupation
identities just proved. The result is

             ellhat_k=2L_k+1-M_k-(N_k-N_(k-1))/d_k.        (11)

In detail, before the recurrence contribution the sum is
2L_k+1-M_k. The remaining term is
sum_i s'h_i^2(v_i,(k-1)-v_ik)/d_k
=(N_(k-1)-N_k)/d_k. This checks its sign and its denominator.

Formula (11) is an identity, not a new condition replacing the load
inequalities. It identifies the chronological mixed moment that must
be estimated. Bounding N_k and N_(k-1) separately loses the signed
quantity needed for the desired 2d_k<=ellhat_k<=11d_k/4.

## 6. An exact reduction that distinguishes the original lower-level order

Put t=d_0, ell=c-1, gamma=ell-t, and

 Z(z)=c*z/(1+ell*z),
 A(z)=(1-t*z)(1+ell*z)=1+gamma*z-t*ell*z^2.

These are new scalar functions, unrelated to the adjacent entries A_i.
They are well-defined at every nonnegative reciprocal physical node.
The affine-geometric node relation gives

 (1-t*z)(1-d_(j+1)*z)=A(z)(1-d_j*Z(z)),
 Z(1/d_(j+1))=1/d_j.                                    (12)

Write the original infinite step word in consecutive pairs. The exact
pair at position r is (t,d_(nu_2(r+1)+1)), where nu_2 is the exponent
of2 in a positive integer. After removing every t and lowering the
remaining level by1, the compressed word is the same original ruler
word, with the SAME epsilon. For its first r steps write
P_r(z)=product_(i<r)(1-h_i*z) and s_r=sum_(i<r)h_i. Then

 P_(2r)(z)=A(z)^r P_r(Z(z)),
 P_(2r+1)(z)=(1-t*z)A(z)^r P_r(Z(z)),
 s_(2r)=c*s_r-gamma*r,
 s_(2r+1)=c*s_r-gamma*r+t.                               (13)

All four identities follow by multiplying or summing the individual
pairs in (12). This is a chronological identity, not an identity of
the full multiset alone. The foil that exchanges the third and fourth
steps destroys the displayed consecutive-pair description.

The Newton factors also transform exactly:

 phi_(k+1)(z)=(1-t*z)(1+ell*z)^k phi_k(Z(z)),              (14)

where phi_k(z)=product_(j<k)(1-d_j*z). At epsilon=0, interpreted only
as a coefficient limit, t=ell=sqrt(2), gamma=0, and A(z)=1-2z^2.
At every actual epsilon>0 the linear term gamma*z and the clock defect
-gamma*r in (13) must both be retained. In fact

 gamma=lambda*epsilon+2epsilon/[lambda(1+epsilon)]>0.

Equations (12)-(14) do not yet establish a bound for E or the mixed
moments in (11). They give an exact original-order transformation in
which those estimates can be pursued without replacing an embedded
block's parameters by epsilon=1/j.

## 7. The decimation multiplier changes sign around one

It is false that the multiplier A at all required physical nodes can
be discarded by a uniform inequality A<=1, or by a uniform inequality
A>=1. Both failures occur at every actual m>=128, without using a
standalone-depth parameter in an embedded block.

For z=1/d_j,

             A(1/d_j)-1=(gamma*d_j-t*ell)/d_j^2.           (15)

We have 7/5<t<3/2, t<ell<3/2, and

 2epsilon<gamma
   <(lambda+2/lambda)epsilon
   =(3sqrt(2)-1)epsilon<(13/4)epsilon.                    (16)

At j=2, d_2<7/2, so
gamma*d_2<91/1024<49/25<t*ell. Therefore A(1/d_2)<1.
At the actual highest level j=m-1,

 gamma*d_(m-1)>(4/5)2^(m-1)/m>9/4>t*ell.

Here a>2/5 and c>2 give the first bound. For the second, 2^(m-1)/m
is increasing for m>=2, and even 2^10/128=8 already exceeds45/16.
This proves A(1/d_(m-1))>1. Both indices are in the original residual
coordinate set. These are counterexamples only to discarding the
decimation factor in one direction, not to the desired band bounds.

There is nevertheless a useful uniform estimate. For k>=8 define

 X_k=(65/16)epsilon*(2/c)^k+(225/32)*(2/c^2)^k.

Then, for every 0<=z<=1/d_k and every integer 0<=r<=2^(k-1),

 0<A(z),
 r|A(z)-1|<=X_k<1/80,
 |A(z)^r-1|<=(80/79)X_k<1/79,
 0<=gamma*r/d_k<=(65/16)epsilon*(2/c)^k.                 (17)

For r=0 all expressions with r vanish. For positive r, (16),
t*ell<9/4, a>2/5, and d_k>a*c^k give

 r|A(z)-1|
 <=2^(k-1)[gamma/d_k+t*ell/d_k^2]
 <(65/16)epsilon*(2/c)^k+(225/32)*(2/c^2)^k.

Also c>12/5, so 2/c<5/6 and 2/c^2<3/8. For k>=8, the exact rational
comparisons (5/6)^8<1/4 and (3/8)^8<1/2500 show

 X_k<65/8192+9/3200<1/80.

Positivity follows from z<=1/d_k<1/t. With delta=|A(z)-1|, if A(z)>=1
the binomial expansion gives

 A(z)^r-1<=sum_(j>=1)(r*delta)^j<=X_k/(1-X_k).

If A(z)<1, 1-A(z)^r<=r*delta<=X_k. These prove the third estimate in
(17); the last follows from the linear part of the same calculation.

Thus the multiplier and clock defects are uniformly small far up the
actual spectrum, despite the change of sign in (15). Their precise
rates include BOTH epsilon*(2/c)^k and (2/c^2)^k. This does not yet
control E: converting pointwise spectral estimates to a birth coefficient
requires retaining the signed divided differences, not taking a large
absolute bound on the entire inverse Newton matrix.

## 8. An early-time bound for the entire nearby band

This localization is in PHYSICAL TIME, in addition to the earlier
localization in matrix-column distance. It controls the part of a near
entry accumulated before the low reciprocal columns die.

Fix j>=2 and let E^early_kl be the contribution to (1) of the first
2^j steps, namely the prefix (H_j,d_j). If k,l>=j+6, then

                         |E^early_kl|<10^(-10).           (18)

Here is a uniform proof. Write T=sum(H_j,d_j). The exact word sum is

 sum H_j=a*c^j/(c-2)+(1-a/(c-2))*2^j-1.

Because 0<a/(c-2)<1 and 2<c, this sum is less than c^j-1.
Consequently T<(1+1/a)d_j<(7/2)d_j. All steps in the prefix are at
most d_j, and d_j>3.

For an integer r>=0 define

 p_r=min{1,(7/2)^r/[r!*2^(r(r-1)/2)]}.

At each query of this prefix, the probability of state j+r is at most
p_r. To prove this, condition on a birth path's first arrival at j.
Reaching j+r requires r subsequent births on increasing distinct
steps. Each specified set has probability at most the product of its
steps divided by d_j...d_(j+r-1); all stay factors are at most one.
The sum of those step products is at most T^r/r!, and (2) bounds the
denominator below by d_j^r*2^(r(r-1)/2). This proof applies at either
end of every step and for every arrival time.

The sequence p_r decreases for r>=3. Thus, for k>=j+6,

 0<=v_ik,v_(i+1),k<=p_6/d_k,
 |v_(i+1),k-v_ik|<=h_i[(5/2)p_5+p_6]/d_k^2.

For the second line, apply the birth recurrence and use
d_k/d_(k-1)<c<5/2. Define Q_6=(5/2)p_5+p_6. The exact fractions give

 p_5=16807/3932160<1/230,
 p_6=117649/1509949440<1/12500,
 Q_6<1/92+1/12500<11/1000.

The first and last terms of (1), polarized for a general k,l, are at
most [T^2+2d_j*T]p_6^2/(d_k*d_l) in absolute value. This uses
2sum_i s_(i+1)h_i=T^2+sum_i h_i^2 and
sum_i h_i^2<=d_j*T. The increment-square term is at most
T^2*d_j*Q_6^2/(d_k^2*d_l^2), since
sum_i s_(i+1)h_i^2<=T^2*d_j. These statements also apply when k=l.

Now d_k,d_l>2^6*d_j. The two contributions are respectively less than

 77/[4*2^12*12500^2] < 1/30000000000,
 49*121/[12*2^24*1000^2] < 1/30000000000.

Their sum is less than10^(-10), proving (18). Every estimate is
uniform in both the row indices and the enclosing m.

For a thirteen-entry row centered at i>=14, take j=i-12. Then both
indices of each entry E_(i,i+r), -6<=r<=6, are at least j+6, so (18)
controls its entire initial prefix through the first d_(i-12). This
does not remove any of the later repeated low steps; their physical
order and elapsed clock remain part of the late contribution.

## 9. Only eighteen reciprocal nodes remain in the late contribution

For this section fix an ACTUAL required row 24<=i<=m-1, put j=i-12,
and consider l=i+r with -6<=r<=6. Extended indices l>=m are only the
same-epsilon coefficient device, as in Section1. All contributing
physical steps are still through the first d_i and are in the original
queried word.

Define R_kb=(d_k/d_b)phi_k(1/d_b) for b>=k, and zero for b<k.
Its triangular inverse D has entries

 D_bk=product_(0<=r<=k,r!=b)(1-d_r/d_b)^(-1),  0<=b<=k,
 D_bk=0 for b>k.                                         (19)

One direct check uses Lagrange interpolation at the distinct nodes
1/d_0,...,1/d_k. Comparing highest-degree coefficients shows that the
weights reciprocal to the products of node differences annihilate
polynomials of degree below k and recover the leading coefficient
at degree k. Apply this identity to phi_r, whose leading coefficient
is (-1)^r product_(s<r)d_s, and rescale by d_b/d_k. The resulting
inverse of R is exactly (19).

Writing P_n(z)=product_(t<n)(1-h_t*z), the corresponding scalar
coefficient identity is

                  v_nk=sum_(b=0)^k D_bk*P_n(1/d_b)/d_b.  (20)

Indeed P_n(z)=sum_k B_nk phi_k(z), by the birth recurrence. Evaluation
at the first k+1 reciprocal nodes kills every phi_r with r>k, and
inversion by (19) gives (20).

For every query n>=2^j and every b<=j, P_n(1/d_b)=0 exactly; the same
is true at the following query. Thus all such nodes disappear from
the late part of (1). Define M^late_ab to be the quadratic coefficient
of the normalized modal columns P_n(1/d_a)/d_a and P_n(1/d_b)/d_b,
using only physical steps 2^j<=n<2^i, with their ORIGINAL elapsed
clocks s_n. Then (20) gives the exact identity

 E_il=E^early_il
          +sum_(a=j+1)^i sum_(b=j+1)^l D_ai*M^late_ab*D_bl. (21)

For l<i every term vanishes already by query2^l; for l>=i the first
column vanishes by query2^i. Consequently the displayed final cutoff
is correct in both cases and adds no physical step beyond the original
trajectory. Only the eighteen nodes i-11,...,i+6 can occur in (21).

Crucially, the remaining inverse columns have a uniform absolute sum.
For each k in [i-6,i+6],

                         sum_(b=j+1)^k |D_bk|<6.          (22)

To prove this, put q_c=1/c and P^q_s=product_(r=1)^s(1-q_c^r),
with P^q_0=1. Factoring each difference in (19) gives

 |D_bk|=q_c^((k-b)(k-b+1)/2)
             *(d_b/(d_b-1))^k/(P^q_b P^q_(k-b)).

Since q_c<5/12, the elementary inequality
product_r(1-x_r)>=1-sum_r x_r for 0<=x_r<=1 gives
P^q_s>=1-q_c-q_c^2>59/144>2/5 for s>=1.
Also P^q_1>7/12. For b>=i-11 and k<=i+6,

 k/(a*c^b)<(5/2)(i+6)/2^(i-11)<=75/8192<1/100.

The middle expression decreases with i>=24. Hence
(d_b/(d_b-1))^k<=exp(k/(a*c^b))<exp(1/100).
Summing with r=k-b and then extending the positive sum to all r>=0,

 sum_b |D_bk|
 <(5/2)exp(1/100)
       *[1+5/7+(5/2)*sum_(r>=2)(5/12)^(r(r+1)/2)]
 <=(5/2)exp(1/100)[1+5/7+625/3206]
 <5 exp(1/100)<500/99<6.

For the second line, ratios of successive terms starting at r=2
are at most(5/12)^3, so their sum is at most125/1603. For the last
line, exp(x)<=1/(1-x) for 0<=x<1 follows by comparing its power
series with the geometric series. This proves (22).

For example, if an analytic approximation changes every normalized
late modal coefficient in this eighteen-node window by at most eta,
then (21)-(22) change each reconstructed near entry by at most36eta.
Discarding the early part costs an additional10^(-10), by (18).
This is a genuine uniform coefficient-stability estimate; no small
fixed-index inverse coefficient is included in its norm.

The pointwise decimation bound (17) is only needed while a reciprocal
column is alive. For the node 1/d_b it is identically zero after
query2^b, so its even-query formula only requires r<=2^(b-1), exactly
the range of (17). Multiplying that bound by an unnecessarily longer
horizon would lose the extinction information.

This leaves a specific analytic step: control the signed late K/T
coefficients under (12)-(14), with the original clock, and bound the
resulting finite window uniformly. Neither (21) nor (22) asserts those
remaining estimates or the signs of b_i and u_i.

## 10. Scope and remaining work

The new proved-in-draft part of the stated interface is e_i>=0 for
every required index. The stronger entrywise result (7), the identities
(9), (11), and (13), the uniform decimation estimates (17), and the
time localization and stable finite inverse (18)-(22) have explicit
proofs above.

The unresolved claims remain b_i>=2/15, u_i>=0, the quantitative
e_i>=1/15 and u_i>=1/16 at the required terminal indices, and the
two-sided constant-load bounds. This document does not prove the full
kernel positive semidefinite, and it is not a counterexample to those
remaining actual-word claims. Even their proof would only concern the
specified reciprocal-level temporal quotient, not arbitrary legal GD.

The existing same-multiset foil is still a useful warning: its first
few steps also satisfy h_i<s_i+2, and entrywise off-diagonal positivity
alone does not distinguish its negative diagonal-margin behavior.
Thus (7) must not be promoted into a substitute for the original-order
estimates. The exact pair identity (13), unlike that coarse envelope,
does distinguish the original word.
