# All circular parameters for the unchanged actual two-checkpoint law

Status: COMPLETE JOINT ANALYTICAL DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. No formal gate was
allocated or called, and this document has no fact_id. All new assertions
below, including the lemmas, form one candidate proof. The only accepted
input is fact04219554e41d9750, used for the original benchmark ray and its
enclosure. The unrestricted all-word/all-horizon target remains OPEN.

Project gd-exact-profile-20260926; worker max3; one analytical round after
completed57, completed ceiling58 / attempted ceiling59. Current assignment
TASK.md; guidance235f55c47f06b19a; selected plan6a516e8ff97d78ce.
Latest guidance reread83b74bbdf2d83c86 leaves this assignment unchanged.
No automatic renewal. The physical function and word are unchanged.

The complete drafts7d145b115817e82d and4c4a21a484564bdc were read in full.
Neither is an accepted predecessor. Every construction, radius, trajectory,
finite-tail and local-data ingredient used here is reconstructed below.
The improvement is one normalization-aware last-crossing comparison: its
cap deficit is paid by the ACTUAL flat width. No comparison repair was
needed. No additional checkpoint, bridge, component or tail reordering is
introduced.

## 1. Statement, including all parameter quantifiers

Put lambda=1+sqrt(2), d=lambda-2=1/lambda. For an integer m>=4 and
tau in[1/5,1/4], define the SAME enclosing parameters in every child:

    eta_m=1-tau/m, c=lambda(1+tau/m), a=eta_m/c,
    b_j=1+a*c^j,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,b_j,H_j), S_j=sum H_j,
    n_m=2^m-1, J=m-2, L=m-1, S=S_J.                 (1.1)

Only the first occurrences of levels J and J+1 are selected. Their
zero-based indices are2^J-1 and2^(J+1)-1. Thus the entire physical word is

    H_m=(H_J,b_J,H_J,b_(J+1),H_L).                  (1.2)

For EVERY beta=beta_arc in(0,1/4], use the three-dimensional static circular
function F defined in Sections2--3, initialized at e_1=(1,0,0). All updates
are x^+=x-h*grad F for the original physical step h in(1.2). Set

    E_m(tau,beta)=2F(x_(n_m))/R_true^2,
    R_true=dist(e_1,argmin F)>0,
    C=beta+sqrt(1+2beta),
    T_1=b_J/2-1, T_2=b_(J+1)/2-1, A_loc=1+S/2.     (1.3)

Write x_fin=x_(n_m); a component evaluated at x_fin means its value
on the corresponding two coordinates of this three-coordinate point.

The local numbers u,y,M=u+y/beta and scales D_2=T_1/M,
D_3=T_1*T_2/M^2 are defined by the ACTUAL discrete backward construction
in Section2. Let B_n be the original all-split denominator defined in
Section8. Define the following word-only quantities:

    K_J=2+2^(J+1),
    Q_m(tau)=B_(n_m)*T_1^2*T_2^2
                     /[2(1+S_L)*A_loc^4],
    epsilon_m(tau)=16*B_(n_m)*K_J/S^2.              (1.4)

There is one finite integer m_0 such that for EVERY integer m>=m_0,
EVERY tau in[1/5,1/4], and EVERY beta in(0,1/4], the following hold.

(a) With Phi_1 and Phi_2 denoting BOTH old components of this same F,

    0 <= [Phi_1(x_fin)+Phi_2(x_fin)]/R_true^2
                                             <=16*K_J/S^2;             (1.5)
    B_(n_m)*E_m(tau,beta)
                         <= Q_m(tau)/C^4+epsilon_m(tau).               (1.6)

The terminal-coordinate Huber is included in(1.6). No old value is
discarded before(1.5) is proved. The bound is valid even if R_true tends
to zero along a choice beta=beta_m.

(b) Both old terminal components are EXACTLY zero whenever

    beta^2*T_1 >= K_J.                              (1.7)

They are also exactly zero whenever the actual incoming margin in(4.7)
is nonpositive. Criterion(1.7) holds uniformly in tau for beta=1/m,
and also for beta=8/m, for all sufficiently large m. These are statements
about their actual full H_L tails, not their post-root snapshots.
No exact-zero claim is required for every smaller beta.

(c) Let Z_1=lim_m lambda^(-m) B_(2^m-1)>0, supplied by accepted
fact04219554e41d9750. Uniformly for tau in[1/5,1/4],

    Q_m(tau) -> Z_1*exp(-tau)/(2lambda),
    epsilon_m(tau) ->0.                             (1.8)

More precisely the full family has the sharp supremal limit

    sup_(tau in[1/5,1/4])
      | sup_(0<beta<=1/4) B_(n_m)*E_m(tau,beta)
                              -Z_1*exp(-tau)/(2lambda) | ->0.          (1.9)

The specific beta_m=8/m gives the lower comparison needed for(1.9),
with its actual backward data proved in Section7. There is no exchange
of an uncontrolled beta supremum and a horizon limit. In particular,

    lim_m sup_(0<beta<=1/4) B_(n_m)*E_m(1/4,beta)
                            =Z_1*exp(-1/4)/(2lambda)<7/10,             (1.10)
    lim_m sup_(tau in[1/5,1/4],0<beta<=1/4) B_(n_m)*E_m(tau,beta)
                            =Z_1*exp(-1/5)/(2lambda)<3/4.             (1.11)

Consequently the respective finite-horizon suprema are below7/10 and3/4
for every sufficiently large m. This closes the remaining0<beta<8/m,
including every parameter choice depending on m, for this PRECISE
two-checkpoint circular family. It is an upper bound on these witnesses,
not an upper bound on W_n. Other checkpoint selections/counts, other
functions and the original minimax problem are separate and remain open.

## 2. Circular body, smooth envelope and actual backward data

Fix0<beta<=1/4 and write k=sqrt(1+2beta), C=beta+k. Define

    p(q)=beta+sqrt((1+beta)^2-(q+beta)^2), 0<=q<=1,
    v(q)=(p(q),-q),
    K_ctr=conv({0} union {v(q):0<=q<=1}).             (2.1)

This is the same centered circular body as in the two source drafts.
The circle has center(beta,beta) and radius1+beta. Its closed disk
contains0, since2beta^2<=(1+beta)^2. The radial vector at each displayed
arc point therefore supports K_ctr. The endpoints are(C,0) and(beta,-1).
All points(p,-q) of K_ctr satisfy

    0<=p<=C, 0<=q<=1, p>=beta*q, (p,0) in K_ctr.     (2.2)

The horizontal assertion uses the segment from0 to(C,0); the other
assertions hold on the generating arc and are preserved by convex hulls.
The cone of K_ctr is generated by its endpoints, because

    (p,-q)=((p-beta*q)/C)(C,0)+q(beta,-1).            (2.3)

For a compact convex K containing0, put

    E_K(r)=max_(g in K){<g,r>-||g||^2/2}.            (2.4)

Completing the square gives the unique maximizer Pi_K(r), the Euclidean
projection. Its characterization is

    <r-Pi_K(r),g-Pi_K(r)> <=0 for all g in K.        (2.5)

Adding this inequality at two points proves
||Pi_K(r)-Pi_K(s)||^2<=<Pi_K(r)-Pi_K(s),r-s>, so the projection is
1-Lipschitz. Comparing the two maximizing expressions in(2.4) gives
the linear increment <Pi_K(r),s-r> with remainder at most||s-r||^2.
Thus E_K is differentiable with gradient Pi_K, convex, nonnegative and
1-smooth. Its zero set is the polar cone: if a supporting inner product
is positive, a sufficiently small positive multiple of that vector
gives positive value in(2.4); if all are nonpositive,0 maximizes.
It follows from(2.3), for every sigma>0, that

    E_(sigma K_ctr)(U,V)=0 iff U<=0 and beta*U<=V.    (2.6)

Define the one-sided Huber, for delta>0, by

    Hub_delta(z)=0                    for z<=0,
                 z^2/2                for0<=z<=delta,
                 delta*z-delta^2/2    for z>=delta.  (2.7)

Its derivative is min(delta,max(z,0)); hence it is convex and1-smooth.

Write the actual steps of H_J in order as h_1,...,h_N, N=2^J-1, and
let alpha_i=h_i/2. These are local half-times, not altered physical
steps. Set r_N=(beta,-1), q_N=1, and construct r_i=(u_i,-y_i) backward.
At each i=N,...,1 choose the unique q_(i-1) in[0,1) satisfying

    (q_(i-1)+beta)/(p(q_(i-1))-beta)
        =max{beta/k,
              [y_i+(1-alpha_i)beta]/[u_i-(1-alpha_i)beta]},             (2.8)
    r_(i-1)=r_i+alpha_i*v(q_(i-1)).                  (2.9)

All steps are positive. Inductively u_i>=beta, y_i>=1, so the denominator
on the right is at least alpha_i*beta>0. The left side is continuous
and strictly increasing from beta/k to infinity. Thus the construction
is finite and well defined. It is a mathematical definition; no recurrence
was executed in this round.

Here are its projection and ordering checks. At an interior current
point, the supporting radial relation is
(y_i+beta)/(u_i-beta)=(q_i+beta)/(p(q_i)-beta).
Subtracting alpha_i*beta from the first numerator and adding it to the
denominator can only decrease this positive ratio. This proves
q_(i-1)<=q_i. At q_i=1 the latter bound follows from q_(i-1)<1. At the
capped point q_i=0, the supporting condition is

    y_i<=beta*(u_i-C)/k.                            (2.10)

It forces the second entry in(2.8) to be at most beta/k, so the new
point is also capped. For an interior new point, equations(2.8)--(2.9)
place r_(i-1) on the outward centered ray through v(q_(i-1)). Its radial
factor is(y_(i-1)+beta)/(q_(i-1)+beta)>=1, because y_(i-1)>=1 and
q_(i-1)<=1. The circular supporting normal proves(2.5). For a capped
new point, (2.8) instead gives(2.10) with index i-1. The displacement
from(C,0) is a nonnegative combination of(k,-beta) and(0,1), both
supporting normals there. This again proves(2.5). The terminal r_N
belongs to K_ctr. We have therefore proved

    Pi_(K_ctr)(r_i)=v(q_i), q_(i-1)<=q_i,
    u_i>=beta, y_i>=1,
    u:=u_0=beta+sum_i alpha_i*p(q_(i-1)),
    y:=y_0=1+sum_i alpha_i*q_(i-1),
    u>=beta*y, M=u+y/beta.                          (2.11)

For an incoming threshold ell>=0 and amplitude D>0 define

    sigma=D/M, ell_out=sigma*y, A=ell+ell_out/beta,
    Phi(X,Y)=E_(sigma K_ctr)(X-A,Y-ell_out).         (2.12)

It is zero whenever X<=ell and Y>=0, by(2.6). Its output derivative
is zero whenever Y>=ell_out, for EVERY X: replacing a nonzero negative
gradient coordinate -q by0 increases the maximized expression by
q(Y-ell_out)+q^2/2. The entire horizontal segment needed for that
replacement belongs to the body by(2.2).

The actual local states are

    (X_i,Y_i)=(A+sigma*u_i,ell_out-sigma*y_i).       (2.13)

They start at(ell+D,0). Equations(2.9),(2.11) and projection scaling
show that every physical step h_i updates them by-(h_i/2)*grad Phi.
They have X_i>ell and0<=Y_i<=ell_out-sigma<ell_out. Their final
gradient is sigma(beta,-1). A selected physical b>2 with T=b/2-1 gives

    (X^+,Y^+)=(A-beta*sigma*T,ell_out+sigma*T).       (2.14)

This lies in the zero set(2.6). Both the gradient change along the arc
and this checkpoint identity concern the unchanged physical word.

## 3. The whole static function, prefix and exact closest radius

The two ordinary blocks in(1.2) are identical, so use the SAME u,y,M
from Section2 for both. Set

    sigma_1=1/M, D_2=T_1/M, sigma_2=D_2/M,
    D_3=T_1*T_2/M^2,
    ell_2=y/M, ell_3=D_2*y/M,
    A_1=ell_2/beta, A_2=ell_2+ell_3/beta,
    delta=D_3/(1+S_L).                             (3.1)

For all sufficiently large m the two T_i are positive. Before starting
GD, define on R^3

    Phi_1(X,Y)=E_(sigma_1 K_ctr)(X-A_1,Y-ell_2),
    Phi_2(Y,Z)=E_(sigma_2 K_ctr)(Y-A_2,Z-ell_3),
    F(X,Y,Z)=[Phi_1(X,Y)+Phi_2(Y,Z)
                                  +Hub_delta(Z-ell_3)]/2.             (3.2)

The coordinate-stacking map(X,Y,Z)->((X,Y),(Y,Z),Z) has squared
norm at most2, since its squared output norm is X^2+2Y^2+2Z^2.
The separable sum of the three1-smooth functions has1-Lipschitz
gradient in the product space. Composition and multiplication by1/2
give a convex1-smooth F. All summands are nonnegative and F(0)=0.

If the two projections are(P_1,-Q_1),(P_2,-Q_2), and
P_3=min(delta,max(Z-ell_3,0)), its EXACT full update is

    X^+=X-h*P_1/2,
    Y^+=Y-h*(P_2-Q_1)/2,
    Z^+=Z-h*(P_3-Q_2)/2.                           (3.3)

During the first H_J and b_J, the first pair follows(2.13)--(2.14);
its output is below ell_2 at every query, and the remaining components
are inactive. The first checkpoint gives

    X=A_1-beta*D_2, Y=ell_2+D_2, Z=0.               (3.4)

During the next H_J and its checkpoint b_(J+1), the second pair follows
the same local law with ell=ell_2,D=D_2. Its input is at least ell_2
at every query. Meanwhile X stays below A_1. Thus the first component
has value and gradient0 at those queries by(2.6), and the last Huber
is inactive below ell_3. The actual state immediately after the root is

    v_in=A_1-beta*D_2,
    w_in=A_2-beta*D_3,
    Z_in=ell_3+D_3.                                (3.5)

This is a trajectory identity in the fixed function, not a free
assignment of incoming tail data.

The ENTIRE minimum set is, by nonnegativity and(2.6),

    X<=A_1, Y>=beta*X,
    Y<=A_2, Z>=beta*(Y-ell_2), Z<=ell_3.            (3.6)

From(2.11),

    1-A_1=u/M, ell_2=y/M=beta*A_1,
    0<A_1<=1/(1+beta^2).                           (3.7)

The point x_star=(A_1,ell_2,0) satisfies all of(3.6). Any feasible
point with X<0 has squared distance from e_1 at least1. For a feasible
point with0<=X<=A_1, that distance is at least
(1-X)^2+beta^2*X^2. This quadratic is decreasing until
X=1/(1+beta^2), so its minimum on[0,A_1] is at A_1. The displayed
x_star attains this lower bound, which is at most1. Hence

    R_true^2=(1-A_1)^2+ell_2^2=(u^2+y^2)/M^2>0.     (3.8)

This is the closest radius for the WHOLE function. It may shrink with
beta and has not been replaced by the distance to the specified point0.

All smooth-convex interpolation rows follow from this actual function.
For arbitrary test points x_test,y_test, subtract <grad F(y_test),z>
from F(z); the resulting function is minimized at y_test. Its1-smooth
descent bound at x_test with displacement
-(grad F(x_test)-grad F(y_test)) yields
F(x_test)-F(y_test)-<grad F(y_test),x_test-y_test>
>=||grad F(x_test)-grad F(y_test)||^2/2. Thus all ordered rows,
including the terminal sample and x_star, hold. The actual Euclidean
Gram matrices are positive semidefinite. For any smoothness L_smooth>0,
use f=L_smooth*F and x^+=x-(h/L_smooth)*grad f; the same trajectory and
normalized payoff2(f(x_fin)-min f)/(L_smooth*R_true^2)=E_m result.

## 4. Every physical tail step and the incoming-activity split

Throughout H_L the third-coordinate excess after traversed mass s' is

    Z-ell_3=D_3-s'*delta/2>=delta, 0<=s'<=S_L.       (4.1)

Indeed this identity, by induction, ensures Q_2=0 by output isolation,
so P_3=delta and the next physical update continues the identity.
For Z>=ell_3, maximizing over the second body's negative coordinate
sets it to0, and hence

    Phi_2(w,Z)=Hub_(C*sigma_2)(w-A_2).              (4.2)

The first coordinate never increases, so v<=A_1 throughout the tail.
For fixed negative gradient coordinate -q, its maximizer then chooses
the smallest permissible horizontal coordinate beta*q. Indeed reducing
p to beta*q increases p(v-A_1)-p^2/2. This segment is in K_ctr. Maximizing
along it and using beta*A_1=ell_2 gives exactly

    Phi_1(v,w)=Hub_((1+beta^2)*sigma_1)(beta*v-w)
                                                   /(1+beta^2).      (4.3)

Abbreviate sigma=1/M, kappa=1+beta^2, r=C*D_2. Here r is the cap
ratio, not a backward point; kappa is the tail coefficient, not a
condition number. For EVERY physical tail step h,

    q=min(sigma,max(beta*v-w,0)/kappa),
    s=min(r*sigma,max(w-A_2,0)),
    v^+=v-h*beta*q/2,
    w^+=w+h*(q-s)/2.                               (4.4)

The letter s in(4.4) denotes a capped derivative only, not a word mass.
The flat interval is beta*v<=w<=A_2, of width

    g=A_2-beta*v,
    g_in=D_2*(A_1+beta^2)>0,
    g^+=g+h*beta^2*q/2>=g.                         (4.5)

The two derivatives cannot both be positive. Once the pair is flat,
both vanish forever, with(4.1) maintaining decoupling from coordinate3.
The ENTIRE actual final payoff is

    E_m=[Phi_1(v_fin,w_fin)+Phi_2(w_fin,Z_fin)
                          +D_3^2/(2(1+S_L))]/R_true^2.               (4.6)

The final term follows by substituting(4.1) at s'=S_L into(2.7).
It is never used alone as an upper bound without bounding the old terms.

The incoming point is strictly below A_2. Its exact left margin is

    z_in=beta*v_in-w_in
         =D_2*(beta*T_2/M-A_1-beta^2).              (4.7)

If z_in<=0 it is already flat, and both old terms vanish for the
whole tail. If z_in>0, the exact inequality gives

    A_1<beta*T_2/M,
    y<beta^2*T_2.                                  (4.8)

These are constraints on ACTUAL discrete incoming data. An upper bound
such as exp(2/beta) is not being treated as attained displacement.

## 5. A radius-aware geometric estimate for every beta

The function p(q) in(2.1) is concave: differentiating gives
p'(q)=-(q+beta)/sqrt((1+beta)^2-(q+beta)^2), whose derivative is
negative on[0,1). Its endpoint chord therefore yields

    p(q)+k*q>=C, k=sqrt(1+2beta).                  (5.1)

Using the actual backward sums in(2.11) and beta+k=C proves

    u+k*y>=C*(1+S/2)=C*A_loc,
    M=u+y/beta>=C*A_loc,
    u<=beta+C*S/2.                                 (5.2)

The second inequality follows from1/beta>=k. For a stronger bound let
N_arc=sqrt(u^2+y^2). Since beta<=1/4, k^2<=3/2 and k<5/4, so

    2k+k^2<4<=1/beta.                             (5.3)

All coefficients below are nonnegative, and N_arc>=u,N_arc>=y. Split
the coefficient y/beta into2k*y, k^2*y and its nonnegative remainder:

    M*N_arc >=(u+2k*y)*u+(k^2*y)*y
               =(u+k*y)^2 >=C^2*A_loc^2.          (5.4)

This pays the exact radius even when it shrinks, since(3.8) gives

    D_3/R_true=T_1*T_2/(M*N_arc)
                         <=T_1*T_2/(C^2*A_loc^2). (5.5)

In particular the normalized FINAL Huber contribution obeys

    B_(n_m)*D_3^2/[2(1+S_L)*R_true^2]
                                      <=Q_m(tau)/C^4.                (5.6)

No estimate R_true approximately1 was used here.

## 6. The finite last-crossing residual, with the gap retained

First establish the word-only conditions used in this section. Write

    d_m=c-2, v_mass=a/d_m.

Here c>lambda>2, eta_m<1 and c(c-2)>lambda(lambda-2)=1, so
0<v_mass<1. Direct substitution into S_(j+1)=2S_j+b_j proves

    1+S_j=v_mass*c^j+(1-v_mass)*2^j,
    b_j=d_m*S_j+c-1-d_m*(1-v_mass)*2^j.             (6.1)

The same enclosing m,tau appears everywhere. Uniformly in
tau in[1/5,1/4], as m tends to infinity,

    c->lambda, v_mass->1,
    S_J/lambda^J ->exp(tau), S_L/lambda^L ->exp(tau),
    T_1/A_loc ->d, T_2/A_loc ->1.                  (6.2)

For example0<=tau-m*log(1+tau/m)<=tau^2/(2m), by integrating
x/(1+x)<=x. This proves the uniform exponential limits; the2^j terms
in(6.1) are smaller by a fixed geometric factor. Both T limits follow
by dividing their exact b formulas by S+2.

For each j let

    P_j=sum_(h in H_j, h>=2) h.

This is only a lower mass bound in the tail proof, not a replacement
of the ordered tail. Every omitted step is below2, and H_j has2^j-1
entries, so

    0<=S_j-P_j<=2(2^j-1)<=2^(j+1).                 (6.3)

Equations(6.1)--(6.3) imply that one word-only m_0 suffices for

    2<c<3, 0<d_m<1, T_1>0, 0<T_2<=S, P_L>=S.      (6.4)

Indeed T_2/S tends1/2 and P_L/S tends lambda>2 uniformly. Increase
m_0 if necessary; it has no dependence on beta.

Two consequences of(5.2) are essential. First the exact cap ratio is

    0<r=C*T_1/M <=(b_J-2)/(S+2)<d_m<1.             (6.5)

For the strict inequality, (6.1) gives
d_m*(S+2)-(b_J-2)=d_m*(1-v_mass)*2^J+d_m+1>0.
Second, if the incoming point is active, equations(4.8),(5.2),(6.4)
give

    A_1<beta*T_2/M<=2beta*S/(S+2)<=2beta<=1/2,
    R_true^2=(1-A_1)^2+beta^2*A_1^2>1/4.           (6.6)

This lower radius bound is asserted ONLY in the active case. The
inactive case already has exactly zero old values and uses(5.5).
Also D_2=r/C<1.

Now suppose the terminal pair is active. It must have been active at
every earlier sample, since the closed flat interval is absorbing. If
z=beta*v-w>0 at consecutive samples on the left, (4.4) gives

    z^+=z-h*kappa*min(sigma,z/kappa)/2.             (6.7)

For h>=2, staying strictly on the left requires the saturated cap
q=sigma: otherwise z^+=(1-h/2)z<=0. This also accounts for the cap
boundary, since landing at0 would not stay strictly left. Each such
high step loses h*kappa*sigma/2. Low steps give nonnegative loss.
On consecutive right samples, t=w-A_2>0 satisfies

    t^+=t-h*min(r*sigma,t)/2.                       (6.8)

Thus staying right across h>=2 requires the saturated cap r*sigma
and loses h*r*sigma/2. No quadratic-region or overshoot case has
been suppressed.

If there is no side change, all samples remain left because the
incoming point is below A_2. But(4.7) and(6.4)--(6.5) imply

    M*z_in < beta*D_2*T_2 <=S/4 < kappa*P_L/2.      (6.9)

Summing the high-step losses in(6.7) would make z_fin negative.
Therefore terminal activity entails at least one crossing. Take its
LAST side change. If this is at a step of level j,0<=j<=J, it is
immediately followed by a complete H_j inside the actual remaining
tail. This follows inductively from H_(j+1)=(H_j,b_j,H_j): the right
subtree is read immediately after its node. That full following block
has no further crossing, by lastness. Any steps after that block also
stay on the same side and only decrease the appropriate margin.

For a last left-to-right crossing, before it let z>0 and
g=A_2-beta*v>=g_in. Its new right margin is

    t^+=b_j*q/2-z-g < b_j*sigma/2-g_in.

Using the high-step losses of its following H_j in(6.8) gives the
necessary terminal bound

    0<t_fin< [b_j-r*P_j]/(2M)-g_in.                (6.10)

For a last right-to-left crossing, the first coordinate is unchanged
on that step. With t>0 at its query, the new left margin is
z^+=b_j*s/2-t-g<b_j*r*sigma/2-g_in. Equation(6.7) then gives

    0<z_fin< [r*b_j-kappa*P_j]/(2M)-g_in.          (6.11)

These inequalities retain the moving interval, both caps, all
overshoots and every original large bridge. They also show why an
equal-mass tail replacement would not justify the result.

Bound(6.10) simultaneously for every possible level. By(6.1),(6.3)
and r<d_m, and because S_j<=S for j<=J,

    b_j-r*P_j
      <=(d_m-r)*S+c-1+r*2^(J+1)
      = b_J-r*S+d_m*(1-v_mass)*2^J+r*2^(J+1).      (6.12)

The critical exact cancellation uses the upper bound on u from(5.2):

    1-C*S/(2M)<=A_1+beta/M,
    b_J-r*S=2+2*T_1*(1-C*S/(2M)).                 (6.13)

Consequently, subtracting the ACTUAL g_in=D_2*(A_1+beta^2),

    [b_j-r*P_j]/(2M)-g_in
      <= [1+beta*D_2+
            (d_m*(1-v_mass)/2+r)*2^J]/M-beta^2*D_2
      <= [K_J-beta^2*T_1]/M.                      (6.14)

For the last inequality use beta*D_2<=1/4,
d_m*(1-v_mass)/2+r<3/2, and
5/4+(3/2)*2^J <=2+2^(J+1)=K_J. The term D_2*A_1 cancels EXACTLY.
Thus the nonuniform local cap defect is paid by an actual part of the
flat interval, with no assertion M/S approximately C/2.

For(6.11), the coefficient r*d_m-kappa is negative by(6.5) and
kappa>=1. Using(6.1),(6.3) and dropping only nonpositive terms gives

    r*b_j-kappa*P_j
       <= r*(c-1)+kappa*2^(J+1)
       <= 2+2^(J+2),                              (6.15)

since c-1<2 and kappa<=17/16<2. Thus

    [r*b_j-kappa*P_j]/(2M)-g_in
       <=[1+2^(J+1)-beta^2*T_1]/M
       <=[K_J-beta^2*T_1]/M.                      (6.16)

Equations(6.9)--(6.16) prove that any active terminal margin, on
either side, is less than K_J/M. If beta^2*T_1>=K_J the strict
positive bounds(6.10)--(6.11) are impossible; this proves the exact
absorption criterion(1.7), including equality in that criterion.

For the quantitative bound, Hub_(kappa*sigma)(z)/kappa<=sigma*z
when z>=0, and Hub_(r*sigma)(t)<=r*sigma*t when t>=0.
At most one old term is active, by the positive gap. Therefore

    Phi_1(x_fin)+Phi_2(x_fin)<=K_J/M^2.            (6.17)

This also holds if the terminal point is flat. In the incoming-active
case, divide by the proved radius bound(6.6) and use M>=S/2:

    [Phi_1(x_fin)+Phi_2(x_fin)]/R_true^2
                                             <=16*K_J/S^2.           (6.18)

In the incoming-flat case the left side is exactly0. This completes
the all-beta proof of(1.5). Combining(6.18) with(5.6) and the exact
whole payoff(4.6) proves(1.6).

For beta=1/m, (6.2) shows T_1/m^2 grows like a positive constant
times lambda^m/m^2, uniformly in tau; K_J is at most a fixed
constant times2^m. Since m^2*(2/lambda)^m tends to0, (1.7) holds
eventually. The same argument applies to beta=8/m. More generally,
the explicit criterion(1.7) is valid as written, without restricting
beta to a fixed or polynomial scale.

## 7. Actual backward estimates only for the attaining comparison

To obtain the sharp lower comparison for the family supremum, it
suffices to analyze one permitted sequence beta_m=8/m. This section
derives the needed finite backward bound; it is not used in the
all-beta upper bound or as an assertion of attained exponential size.

For fixed beta let

    I_beta=sqrt(2)*(1+beta)-beta<2.

At each backward state put w_i=(y_i+beta)/(q_i+beta). If q_i>0,
the radial relation gives u_i-beta=(p(q_i)-beta)*w_i.
For a transition with q_i>0 and q_(i-1)>0, take the cross product
of(2.9) with v(q_(i-1)); the alpha_i term cancels, giving

    w_(i-1)=w_i*[1+
       (p(q_(i-1))*q_i-q_(i-1)*p(q_i))
                   /{beta*(p(q_(i-1))+q_(i-1))}]. (7.1)

If q_(i-1)=0, then y_(i-1)=y_i, so (7.1) follows directly from
w_i*(q_i+beta)=y_i+beta. If q_i=0, both q values are0 by their
ordering and w_(i-1)=w_i. Thus all cap cases are covered.

The nonnegative numerator in(7.1) is

    integral_(q_(i-1))^(q_i)
                  [p(q_(i-1))-q_(i-1)*p'(t)]dt.

After division by p(q_(i-1))+q_(i-1), its integrand is a weighted
mean of1 and -p'(t), at most max(1,-p'(t)). Multiplying(7.1) and
using1+x<=exp(x), q_N=1 and w_N=1 gives

    y+beta <=(q_0+beta)*exp[(1/beta)
                          integral_(q_0)^1 max(1,-p'(t))dt]
            <= beta*exp[(1/beta) integral_0^1 max(1,-p'(t))dt].         (7.2)

The last step uses log(1+q_0/beta)<=q_0/beta and the integrand's
lower bound1 on[0,q_0]. The derivative -p'(t) crosses1 at
t=(1+beta)/sqrt(2)-beta. Integrating separately below and above
that point yields exactly I_beta. The integrable endpoint derivative
at1 causes no problem. Hence for the actual finite backward word,

    1<=y<=beta*(exp(I_beta/beta)-1)
                                      <=beta*exp(2/beta).            (7.3)

Combine(5.1)--(5.2) with(2.11). They give the tighter elementary
finite estimates

    C*S/2+C-k*y <=u<=beta+C*S/2,
    0<M-C*S/2<=beta+y/beta<=beta+exp(2/beta).       (7.4)

For beta=8/m and m>=32, the rightmost exponential is exp(m/4).
Since exp(1/4)<sum_(j>=0)(1/4)^j=4/3<lambda and
S is bounded below by a positive constant times lambda^m uniformly
in tau, equations(7.3)--(7.4) imply uniformly in tau

    M/S-C/2 ->0, u/S-C/2 ->0, y/(beta*M)->0.        (7.5)

Here C depends on beta_m and tends to1. These are genuine estimates
for the constructed discrete data, not values assigned to them.
Equations(3.1),(3.8),(6.2),(7.5) imply

    D_2->d, D_3->d, R_true^2->1.                  (7.6)

The old terms are in fact0 by(1.7) for this sequence, as proved in
Section6; alternatively their nonnegativity and(1.5) suffice. The
exact whole payoff(4.6) therefore has the claimed attaining limit
after the original benchmark normalization in Section8.

## 8. Original benchmark and uniform all-beta limits

Keep the ORIGINAL all-split recurrences, without recalculation:

    C_0^bench=B_0=1,
    C_n^bench=max_(0<=r<n) Omega(C_r^bench,C_(n-r-1)^bench),
    B_n=max_(0<=r<n) G(C_r^bench,B_(n-r-1)),
    Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2,
    G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2,
    U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.                                  (8.1)

The superscript bench only distinguishes the original primitive C_n
from the local circular endpoint C; it changes no recurrence.
Accepted fact04219554e41d9750, statement and proof Section7, gives
Z_1=lim_m lambda^(-m)B_(2^m-1)>0 finite. Its b=1,j=0 enclosure gives

    Z_1<=(sqrt(B_1)+3/100)^2=(203/100)^2<21/5,      (8.2)

where B_1=G(1,1)=4. This unconditional benchmark conclusion is the
only verifier-accepted predecessor used. No conditional seed premise
or saved benchmark calculation is imported or replayed.

Using(6.2), uniformly in tau,

    B_(n_m)/(1+S_L) ->Z_1*lambda*exp(-tau),
    T_1*T_2/A_loc^2 ->d.                          (8.3)

These prove the Q_m limit in(1.8). Also B_(n_m)/S is uniformly
bounded and K_J/S tends to0 geometrically, since K_J=2+2^(J+1)
and S is uniformly comparable with lambda^m. Thus epsilon_m tends
to0 uniformly. All words and all beta choices share this same
word-only error bound.

Let V_m(tau)=sup_(0<beta<=1/4) B_(n_m)*E_m(tau,beta) and
V(tau)=Z_1*exp(-tau)/(2lambda). Since C>=1, (1.6) implies

    V_m(tau)<=Q_m(tau)+epsilon_m(tau).             (8.4)

On the other hand beta_m=8/m is allowed for m>=32. Equations(4.6),
(7.6),(8.3) show uniformly in tau

    B_(n_m)*E_m(tau,8/m)->V(tau).                 (8.5)

Thus V_m(tau) is bounded BELOW by a specific family member converging
uniformly to V(tau), and ABOVE by a word-only bound with the same
uniform limit. These two inequalities prove(1.9). No pointwise
limiting estimate was maximized over an uncontrolled beta interval.
They prove(1.10)--(1.11), since exp(-tau) is decreasing in tau.

For the stated strict constants, lambda>12/5 since sqrt(2)>7/5,
exp(1/4)>1+1/4 and exp(1/5)>1+1/5. Together with(8.2),

    Z_1*exp(-1/4)/(2lambda)
                         <(21/5)*(4/5)/(24/5)=7/10,
    Z_1*exp(-1/5)/(2lambda)
                         <(21/5)*(5/6)/(24/5)=35/48<3/4.             (8.6)

The strict margins and uniform convergence give a single sufficiently
large cutoff for each asserted finite deficit. These symbolic
inequalities required no numerical evaluation or arithmetic program.

## 9. Comparison mechanism, provenance and residual scope

The older large-beta argument estimated r approximately d_m before
comparing it to the gap. That approximation was not uniform below8/m.
Here the EXACT inequalities M>=C*A_loc, u<=beta+C*S/2 yield r<d_m
and(6.13); subtracting the true g_in cancels D_2*A_1. This is the
selected comparison's central identity. It is valid through every
small-beta transition, whether the initial radius shrinks or not.

The initial-radius issue is handled in two distinct proved cases,
not by assuming R_true tends to1 everywhere. If the old pair is
incoming-flat, it stays flat and(5.5) alone pays the radius. If it
reactivates, the exact root inequality itself gives R_true^2>1/4,
and the finite last-crossing bound controls its actual final value.

There is no remaining beta gap in this two-checkpoint comparison.
Both old terms and the terminal-coordinate term have an actual upper
bound, uniformly for the entire permitted interval0<beta<=1/4.
The limiting supremum and the finite eventual deficits are claims of
this complete draft, pending verification. Exact absorption has the
explicit sufficient criterion(1.7); claiming absorption for every
beta is unnecessary and is not part of the theorem.

The word is precisely(1.1)--(1.2), the function precisely(3.2), and
the closest radius precisely(3.8). Neither source-format normalization,
an older spiral radius, an extra bridge, nor a reordered tail enters.
The unrestricted W_n is a supremum over many other functions, so
this witness-family upper deficit cannot bound W_n above. Other
checkpoint selections and arbitrary counts are also outside this
statement. The original all-word minimax goal remains OPEN.

# Fixed-two-plane return debt and inherited-hierarchy failure

Project gd-exact-profile-20260926; max4 allocated invocation69.
Status: JOINT UNVERIFIED analytical finding, no new fact.
This text is the exact scoped-failure section of the complete small-ratio
report; its elementary guard proof is included here in full.
No computation, formal gate or original-target completion is claimed.


This is an exact scoped failure of a proposed THIRD repair, not a third
attained construction and not a general impossibility theorem.

Suppose ANY fixed max-affine function has two DISTINCT slopes q,r with
independently fixed offsets o_q,o_r. Other planes may be present.
Assume actual queries realize: a q-stage ending at proximal point y;
a bridge with excess D>=0; an r-stage of ordinary mass M2-1, ending
at z=y-Dq-M2r; a bridge with excess C>=0; and a RETURNED q-stage
of ordinary mass M3-1, ending at w=z-Cr-M3q. Here M2,M3>=1.
These are actual physical updates, not reset states. Heights at y,z,w
may be arbitrary positive values, and no endpoint is assumed minimal.
The named q-plane and r-plane must actually be active at their respective
queries; representing q as a mixture of unrelated active slopes is outside
this specific fixed-two-plane face policy.

Put s=<q,r>, alpha=||q||^2-s, beta=||r||^2-s, and
ell(u)=<q-r,u>+o_q-o_r. The q/r active-plane conditions demand

    ell(y)>=0,      ell(z)<=0,       ell(w)>=0,
    ell(z)=ell(y)-D*alpha+M2*beta,
    ell(w)=ell(z)+C*beta-M3*alpha.                       (15)

In any nontrivial feasible q-r-q path alpha,beta MUST be positive.
If alpha<=0, then beta>0 because alpha+beta=||q-r||^2>0;
ell(z)>=ell(y)+M2*beta>0 contradicts its r guard. If beta<=0,
alpha>0 and ell(w)<=ell(z)-M3*alpha<0 contradicts its q guard.
Thus the first two guards in(15) give beta/alpha<=D/M2, and the last
two give beta/alpha>=M3/C. If C=0 they directly contradict one another.
Consequently every such fixed-two-plane return NECESSARILY obeys

    D*C >= M2*M3.                                      (16)

Offsets, positive affine heights, minimum-level translations or subsequent
terminal completions cannot repair a violation: they cancel from these
ORIGINAL preterminal active-plane comparisons. Other planes only add
guards. The failure already precedes any smoothness or radius issue.

Apply this to the declared first-occurrence physical prefix

    (H_j,b_j,H_j,b_(j+1),H_(j+1),b_(j+2),
        H_(j+2),b_(j+3)),                               (17)

using ONE enclosing m>=7, j+3<m, and tau=1/4 throughout. Define
lambda=1+sqrt2, beta_h=1-1/(4m), c_h=lambda*(1+1/(4m)),
a_h=beta_h/c_h, H_0=(), H_(l+1)=(H_l,1+a_h*c_h^l,H_l),
S_l=sum H_l, b_l=1+a_h*c_h^l. Induction on the mass identity gives

    1+S_l=v_h*c_h^l+(1-v_h)*2^l,  v_h=a_h/(c_h-2) in(0,1).

Indeed c_h-2>1/lambda>a_h, c_h<3. For the q-r-q assignment in(17),
D=a_h*c_h^(j+1), C=a_h*c_h^(j+2),
M2=1+S_(j+1)>v_h*c_h^(j+1),
M3=1+S_(j+2)>v_h*c_h^(j+2). Therefore

    D*C/(M2*M3)<(a_h/v_h)^2=(c_h-2)^2<1.

This contradicts(16), even with independent affine offsets and POSITIVE
endpoint heights. Thus this specific first-occurrence two-slope-return
repair cannot be instantiated on the actual inherited hierarchy. The
explicit return Law2 is legal because its declared physical data instead
give D*C/(M2*M3)=4. These two statements concern different physical
words; no ratio is silently substituted or reset.

This does not exclude interleaved visits with changing gradients within
a face/edge, more than two returning slopes, nonlinear curved regions,
different physical checkpoint selections, or other full smooth functions.
It does not give an upper bound on W_n(H_m). It is useful because it
identifies a debt unaffected by the newly proposed affine-height freedom.

# Initialized small-ratio three-stage example

Status: JOINT UNVERIFIED; worker max4 invocation69. No new fact or original
target completion is asserted. This diagnostic is deliberately compared
with the existing same-word Huber channel at the end.

In R^3 with Euclidean norm initialize x0=e1 and use h=(1,2,1,2,1,3).
Freeze p=e1/2, q=(-1,sqrt(2),0)/12,
r=(1,-sqrt(2),sqrt(3))/48. Let Q=||q||^2=1/48 and R=||r||^2=1/384.
All affine offsets are0 and maximum weights1 in J(u)=max(0,p.u,q.u,r.u).
Define F_old(x)=max_(g in conv{0,p,q,r})[x.g-||g||^2/2]. Its gradient
is projection onto that tetrahedron; projection optimality at two points
gives firm nonexpansiveness, hence it is convex C1 and globally1-smooth.

The first two queries have proximal points e1/2 and0, gradients p,p.
Here q support is nonpositive and r support is strictly below p unless
the common height is0. The first bridge reaches X=-e1/2. For the next
two queries X-tq, t=1,2, the q height is(2-t)Q, r height is its negative
divided by4, and the first coordinate is at most-1/3. Thus the actual
next gradients are q,q, with last proximal endpoint
y=(-1/3,-sqrt(2)/6,0). Its q and r heights are0.
The second bridge reaches X_R=y-q. At the final two queries X_R-tr,
t=1,2, the r height is(2-t)R, q height is(-1+t/4)Q<0, and the first
coordinate is negative. Hence the actual gradients are r,r, with endpoint
z=(-7/24,-5sqrt(2)/24,-sqrt(3)/24), r.z=0, q.z=-1/96.

At each old proximal pair x=u+g, the value is J(u)+||g||^2/2.
Every ordered old interpolation residual is
Q_ij=J(u_i)-J(u_j)+g_i.(u_j-u_i)<=0 by the subgradient inequality.
This includes the optimum0, every query and both orientations.

Retain ALL six conjugate affine anchors:
(0,0), (e1/2,1/4), (y,0), (y+q,1/48), (z,0), (z+r,1/384).
Set H(v)=||v||^2/2 plus the maximum of these six expressions t.v-delta,
and define F=H^* BEFORE the run. Accepted fact1252b8823bb5a290, with
the checked ALL-prefix residuals, proves it is a single finite convex C1
globally1-smooth function preserving every preceding value and gradient.
All physical updates on F therefore coincide with those just established.

The genuinely unqueried terminal is x6=z-2r. At v=-2r the0,y,z affine
terms all equal0. The initial affine term is negative. The first q anchor
minus z has value -1/96<0, and the first r anchor minus z has value
-3R<0. Thus z is an active slope and x6=v+z belongs to partial H(v).
Strong convexity makes v the UNIQUE actual terminal gradient, and
2F(x6)=||2r||^2=1/96. Every terminal/prefix/optimum row in BOTH
orientations holds for this actual smooth convex function by the necessity
proof in accepted1252; no anchor or formal gradient has been dropped.

At v=0 the active affine slopes are exactly0,y,z, so F has minimum0
and COMPLETE minimum set conv{0,y,z}. The nonzero vertices have negative
first coordinate; origin is the unique nearest minimizer to e1, TRUE
initial radius1. On a small positive-e1 open set H is purely quadratic,
so F has an open Hessian-I region: smoothness1 is sharp. For any L>0,
f=L*F with actual steps h_i/L gives the same normalized payoff1/96.

The three ordinary masses are1 and both bridge excesses are1, so their
actual ratios are1/2. This literal physical word is not the inherited
hierarchy. Its payoff is BELOW the existing same-word global Huber1/21
(the total step mass is10), by accepted factb98bb807d514e80d. It therefore
supplies a legal initialized small-ratio diagnostic, not an improved
finite lower claim. The complete joint report supplies the scalable
unequal-mass rule and a DIFFERENT four-stage family with a real same-word
two-block/Huber/quadratic/universal-lens gap. No formal gate was authorized.

# Smaller-ratio stages, an actual return, and the price of affine heights

Project gd-exact-profile-20260926; worker max4; allocated invocation69.
Status: JOINT UNVERIFIED analytical draft. No assertion here is a new fact.
The allocation is ONE invocation after ended baseline68, ceiling69,
attempted ceiling70. ZERO mathematical programs, arithmetic/numerical
replays, trajectory/recurrence executions, optimizers, DP, formal calls,
installations, consults or subagents. No automatic renewal is inferred.

Two explicit static constructions are given. A third proposed repair,
independent affine heights for a two-slope return on the inherited hierarchy,
is excluded at its exact stated scope. The original all-word problem stays
OPEN. This report changes no frozen round68/audit input.

## 1. Accepted inputs and the original interface

The only correctness inputs used are:

* fact_id1252b8823bb5a290, including its constructive proof: for normalized
  smoothness1, Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2<=0 is the
  ordered interpolation residual. Given ALL prefix jets and the optimum,
  the conjugate of the maximum of their lower quadratics preserves every
  queried value and gradient, including repeated queries, and is convex,
  continuously differentiable and globally1-smooth. No old prefix row can
  be removed. The unqueried terminal is then evaluated on that fixed function.
* fact_idb98bb807d514e80d, full statement/proof: K_n(h) is the maximum of
  1/(1+2 sum h) and ALL (h_j-1)_+^2/[(1+P_j)^2(1+2T_j)], where P_j,T_j
  are the actual preceding/following sums. It is an attained lower channel.
  The ORIGINAL ALL-split benchmark is C_0=B_0=1,
  C_n=max_j Omega(C_j,C_(n-j-1)), B_n=max_j G(C_j,B_(n-j-1)), with
  Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2,
  G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2, U_n^ZJ=1/B_n,
  and B_n<=1+4n^(log_2(3)).
* fact_iddfde2fa326c908bf, its exact Chebyshev schedule and polynomial
  formula: Chi_N has N entries sec^2((2j-1)pi/[2(2N+1)]), 1<=j<=N,
  sum2N(N+1)/3, and polynomial
  P_N(t)=(-1)^N Cheb_(2N+1)(sqrt(t))/[(2N+1)sqrt(t)].

The full final UNVERIFIED round68 report b3a629e987978a22 was read.
Its exact25685-byte own copy has SHA256
5ab2e70ab0d90c767edf3e7323ff42bd30c40b017642b37b8440835709f020a8.
Latest authoritative strategy f51ffc777372c82f and complete elaboration
971c9cb839662d46 were read. Shared two-block formulas were recalled only
as awareness; the comparison bound needed below is proved inside this
joint draft. No unverified intermediate lemma is used as an accepted brick.
Final steering read06647dcc7594b68c leaves max4r69 unchanged; its separate
max2 formal slot is not allocated to this worker and was not used here.

The original quantity is W_n(h)=sup 2(f(x_n)-f_star)/(L||x_0-x_star||^2)
over the full smooth convex class and actual GD, so W=2R. A positive
resolution needs EVERY sufficiently large n and EVERY h>=0, with a
word-independent remainder relative to U_n^ZJ. A special-family gain,
including the one below, does not meet that requirement.

Notation below is local, with suffix _sr available for shared glossaries.
Specifically M_i=M_i_sr, A=A_sr, D=D_sr, E=E_sr, d=d_sr, k=k_sr,
p=p_sr, q=q_sr, r=r_sr, Q=Qnorm_sr and R=Rnorm_sr; local R is a
slope norm, distinct from the original objective R_n=W_n/2. Likewise
H3,H4,F3,F4,V3,V4 are aliases for H3_sr,H4_sr,F3_sr,F4_sr,V3_sr,V4_sr.
The vectors e1,e2,e3 are the standard basis of R^3. Inner products and
norms are Euclidean. Each ordinary list retains every original step;
its sum determines a constant-gradient segment but it is not replaced
by an oracle call. All affine maximum weights are1.

## 2. First initialized example at genuinely small ratios

Use the literal physical word

    h=(1,2,1,2,1,3),       x_0=e1,
    p=e1/2,
    q=(-1,sqrt(2),0)/12,
    r=(1,-sqrt(2),sqrt(3))/48.

There are three ordinary masses1; first and second bridge excesses1.
Thus both relevant ratios are1/2. This word is DECLARED here; it is
not the full inherited hierarchy. Every affine offset is0 in

    J(u)=max(0,<p,u>,<q,u>,<r,u>),
    F_old(x)=max_(g in conv{0,p,q,r}) [<x,g>-||g||^2/2].

The exact attained gradients at the six queried positions are p,p,q,q,r,r.
All region guards, including comparisons with all other slopes and zero,
are the specialization of the joint direct proof in Section3. The two
later zero-height proximal endpoints are

    y=(-1/3,-sqrt(2)/6,0),
    z=(-7/24,-5sqrt(2)/24,-sqrt(3)/24).

For M0=M1=M2=2, A=1/2, D=1, E=2, the SIX exact affine anchors
in Section4 define ONE fixed completed function before the run.
Its last position is x_6=z-2r and its actual terminal gradient is -2r.
Its minimum is0, complete minimum set conv{0,y,z}, TRUE radius1,
sharp global smoothness1, and terminal payoff2F(x_6)=1/96.
This finite diagnostic is BELOW the same-word global Huber1/21. It is
not advertised as an improved lower channel. Its relevance is the paid
small-ratio initialization. Sections3--4 give its scalable unequal-mass
mechanism, and Sections5--7 give a separate quantitative improved family.

## 3. Law1: a specified three-stage geometry for every positive ratio

Take any full known physical word

    h=(L0,1+A*M0,L1,1+D,L2,1+E),                    (1)

where L_i are ANY finite nonnegative lists of mass M_i-1, M_i>=1,
A>0,D>0,E>=0. Empty and zero-padded lists are allowed. Define

    k=D/(2M2),        B=D/M1,
    d=min{A/[2(1+B)], M1*M2/(M0*D)},
    p=e1/M0,
    q=(-d*e1+sqrt(A*d-d^2)*e2)/M1,
    Q=||q||^2=A*d/M1^2,
    r=-k*q+k*sqrt(Q)*e3,       R=||r||^2=2k^2 Q.

Here 0<d<A, all slopes are linearly independent, <q,r>=-kQ, and
the initial r/p competition is paid by k*d/M1<=1/(2M0).
All offsets in J=max(0,p.u,q.u,r.u) are0. Fix the above F_old from
the convex hull of these four slopes and initialize x_0=e1.

Existence, convexity and global smoothness of F_old are direct: the
strictly concave maximization has unique maximizer P_Dict(x), the Euclidean
projection onto Dict=conv{0,p,q,r}. Projection optimality at two points
gives <x-x',g-g'> >=||g-g'||^2. The maximizing inequalities give
grad F_old(x)=g, so it is convex C1 and1-smooth. If x=u+g with g an
active subgradient of J at u, the normal inequalities show that g is this
maximizer and F_old(x)=J(u)+||g||^2/2. Its complete minimum set is
{x:p.x<=0,q.x<=0,r.x<=0}, minimum0. The initial true radius is1.

The ACTUAL initialized chronology is as follows.

First block: at partial ordinary mass s, 0<=s<=M0-1, the proximal point
is u=(1-(s+1)/M0)e1. The p-plane is nonnegative, q-plane nonpositive,
and r-plane at most HALF the p-plane. Thus gradient p is selected at
EVERY original query, including the bridge query, whose proximal point
is0. The first bridge reaches X=-A e1.

Second block: at t=1+s in[1,M1], its proposed proximal point is X-tq.
The q-height is (M1-t)Q>=0. Its p-height is nonpositive, because its
first coordinate is -A+t*d/M1<=-A+d<0. The r-height is -k times its
q-height. Thus every query really has gradient q. The last proximal point

    y=-A e1-M1 q,          q.y=r.y=0,       y_1=-A+d<0

is reached. The second selected bridge sends the state to X_R=y-Dq.

Third block: its proximal point at t=1+s in[1,M2] is X_R-tr.
Its r-height is (M2-t)R>=0. Its q-height is (-D+t*k)Q<=-D Q/2<0.
Its first coordinate is bounded above by

    -A+d+(D/M1)d <= -A/2<0.

This pays the p-plane. Thus gradient r is selected at EVERY actual
third-block query, with last proximal endpoint

    z=y-Dq-M2r,        r.z=0,       q.z=-D Q/2<0,       z_1<0.

All values are exactly the active height plus half the squared gradient.
For any two old query/optimum pairs x_i=u_i+g_i, delta_i=J(u_i),
direct expansion gives the complete ordered identity

    Q_ij=delta_i-delta_j+<u_j-u_i,g_i><=0.             (2)

The last inequality is the subgradient inequality at u_i. For zero
offset homogeneous planes delta_i=<u_i,g_i>, this is also
Q_ij=<u_j,g_i-g_j><=0. Thus EVERY old directed row, including remote
stages, optimum and duplicates, is paid. No static-switching assumption
or freely prescribed matrix word is involved.

## 4. Law1's exact ALL-anchor terminal and every-word functional

Write a0=1-1/M0 and s1=M1-1. The SIX affine anchors are

    (0,0), (tP,deltaP)=(a0 e1,a0/M0),
    (y,0), (tQ,deltaQ)=(y+s1*q,s1*Q),
    (z,0), (tR,deltaR)=(z+(M2-1)r,(M2-1)R).

For M_i=1 an endpoint duplicates its mate; nothing is removed by a
new equality on formal gradients. Every ordinary and bridge jet in a
stage lies on its endpoint segment with its affine height. Therefore
ALL conjugate lower quadratics, including every intermediate physical
query and the optimum, have maximum exactly

    H3(v)=||v||^2/2+max(0,tP.v-deltaP,
                       y.v,tQ.v-deltaQ,z.v,tR.v-deltaR),
    F3=H3^*.                                             (3)

Fix THIS single function before running (1). Accepted1252 preserves
EVERY preceding update and value. The last physical step reaches
x_n=z-E r. For a CLOSED actual terminal calculation set

    U=s1+D/2,       N3=U^2+D^2/4,
    Tvec=tQ-z=(D+s1)q+M2r,
    theta=s1*(E*k-1)_+/N3,
    v=-E*r-theta*Tvec.                                   (4)

One has ||Tvec||^2=Q*N3 and
Tvec.(-E*r)-deltaQ=Q*s1*(E*k-1). If theta>0, z and tQ tie;
otherwise z is an active anchor. Every OTHER anchor is checked:

* y.v=0, and z.v=theta*(D+s1)*D*Q/2>=0, so0,y are below z.
* r.v=-2E*k^2 Q+theta*k*s1 Q<=-E*k^2 Q<=0, using
  theta<=E*k*s1/N3 and N3>=s1^2. Thus tR is below z.
* v_1=(d/M1)[-E*k+theta*U]<=0, since s1*U<=N3.
  Consequently tP.v-deltaP<=0<=z.v.

The remaining tQ condition is the defining equality or inequality in
(4). An active convex combination requires0<=theta<=1. If E*k<=1 it
is0; if E*k>1 its size is not bounded for unrestricted E. For that latter case (4) is
therefore licensed ONLY when theta<=1. This explicit cap is necessary
for z+theta*Tvec to be an active convex combination. To retain a simple
universal interface, restrict Law1 to

    E*k<=1+N3/max(1,s1).                                 (5)

This is sufficient for theta<=1, including s1=0. At theta1 the same
subgradient argument holds with tQ alone. Under(5),
x_n=v+z+theta*Tvec belongs to partial H3(v), so strong convexity gives
the UNIQUE ACTUAL terminal gradient v and value

    V3=2F3(x_n)=E^2 R-Q*s1^2*(E*k-1)_+^2/N3,
    E^2*k^2 Q<=V3<=2E^2*k^2 Q.                           (6)

For E=0 the value is0. In particular no strict-ratio assertion is made
at that boundary. The cap(5) is kept in the claimed theorem; no large-E
continuation is silently inferred.

The complete minimum set of F3 is conv{0,y,z}, value0. Indeed the
zero-offset active slopes of H3 at0 are exactly those three; when a
delta vanishes its corresponding endpoint duplicates one of them.
All nonzero vertices have negative first coordinate, hence origin is
the unique closest minimizer to e1 and the TRUE initial radius is1.
For small positive v=t e1 the y,z terms are strictly negative; every
positive-offset term is also negative. Thus H3=||v||^2/2 on a nonempty
open set, and F3 has an open Hessian-I region. Smoothness1 is sharp.
Since F3 is an actual convex1-smooth function, its actual terminal,
prefix and EVERY minimizer satisfy BOTH directed interpolation rows by
the necessity proof of accepted1252. All Gram matrices are actual R^3
Gram matrices, including duplicated queries.

For an arbitrary word with final step>=1, select ANY two earlier
indices j1<j2 with h_j1,h_j2>1. Set M0=1+sum_(t<j1)h_t,
M1=1+sum_(j1<t<j2)h_t, M2=1+sum_(j2<t<n-1)h_t,
A=(h_j1-1)/M0, D=h_j2-1, E=h_(n-1)-1. This is the exact physical
partition(1), with all unselected steps retained. Use the stated slope
rule, check cap(5), and use value(6). Let D3_sr(h) be the maximum over
these admissible pairs, or0 if there are none, n=0, or final step<1. The joint
candidate gives W_n(h)>=max{K_n(h),D3_sr(h)}. Establishing that this or
some enlarged policy uniformly matches1/B_n remains completely unpaid.

For the hierarchy diagnostic fix ONE enclosing m>=7, lambda=1+sqrt2,
beta_h=1-1/(4m), c_h=lambda*(1+1/(4m)), a_h=beta_h/c_h,
H_0=(), H_(l+1)=(H_l,1+a_h*c_h^l,H_l), S_l=sum H_l,
b_l=1+a_h*c_h^l. The mass recursion1+S_(l+1)=2(1+S_l)+a_h*c_h^l
gives1+S_l=v_h*c_h^l+(1-v_h)*2^l, v_h=a_h/(c_h-2) in(0,1).
Here c_h-2>1/lambda>a_h and c_h<3, so the first bridge ratio
a_h*c_h^j/(1+S_j)<c_h-2<1, including j0.
On this enclosing-m hierarchy, one may actually initialize this law on
the DECLARED truncated physical prefix

    (H_j,b_j,H_j,b_(j+1),H_(j+1),b_(j+2)),                (7)

with the same enclosing m throughout. Here M0=M1=1+S_j,
M2=1+S_(j+1), A=(b_j-1)/M0<1 and D=b_(j+1)-1. The static guard
proof is independent of the old A>=4B+4 contract. If its final cap(5)
is not paid, this is only a legal prefix and no formula(6) is claimed
at that terminal. This prefix is not the full H_m; its omitted suffix

## 5. Law2: a reached p-q-r-q return with A=1/2

Let M>=6. Each of FOUR nonnegative physical lists L0,L1,L2,L3 has mass

Thus the first bridge ratio is1/2, rather than the old sufficient
contract A>=12 when its old second ratio is2. Freeze the slopes

    p=e1/M,
    q=(-2,sqrt(10),0)/(14M),       Q=||q||^2=1/(14M^2),
    r=-q+sqrt(Q)*e3,              R=||r||^2=2Q.

The old function is the explicitly specified F_old above, with its

First block and bridge: gradient p, because q_1<0 and r_1=1/(7M)<p_1.
The first bridge reaches X=-e1/2. Second block and bridge: gradient q.
At t in[1,M], q.(X-tq)=(M-t)Q>=0, r support is its negative, and

    y=(-5/14,-sqrt(10)/14,0),       q.y=r.y=0.

After the second bridge, X_R=y-2M q. Third block and bridge: gradient r.

and its first coordinate is at most -1/2+3/7=-1/14<0. Its endpoint is

The third bridge reaches X_D=z-2M r. Fourth block and final bridge query:

r support(-4M+t)Q<0, and first coordinate at most-5/14<0.
Thus this is an ACTUAL earlier-face return, not a reset. Its endpoint is

    w=z-2M r-Mq=y-3M sqrt(Q)e3,
    w_1=-5/14,        q.w=0,          r.w=-3M Q.

Every physical preterminal query is p,q,r or RETURNED q as proved.
The last physical update reaches x_n=w-E q. All old values remain the

## 6. Law2's eight anchors, full minima and actual terminal value

Put a=1-1/M. Retain the EIGHT distinct affine anchors

    (0,0), (a e1,a/M),
    (y,0), (y+(M-1)q,(M-1)Q),
    (z,0), (tR,(M-1)R),      tR=z+(M-1)r,
    (w,0), (w+(M-1)q,(M-1)Q).

Each stage's entire physical segment is retained as convex combinations
of its endpoints. In particular the FIRST r query tR is retained. Define

    H4(v)=||v||^2/2+max_(all EIGHT anchors (t,delta))(t.v-delta),
    F4=H4^*                                                   (9)

BEFORE running(8). Accepted1252 and the checked original jets guarantee
that this one function preserves every preterminal update and queried value.

For a closed terminal calculation set

    L=3M-1,       T=2M-1,       N4=T^2+L^2=13M^2-10M+2,
    ER=tR-w=Mq+Lr=-Tq+Lsqrt(Q)e3,
    theta=[E*T-2(M-1)]/N4,
    v=-E q-theta*ER.                                     (10)

Since6<=E<=M and M>=6, E*T-2(M-1)>=10M-4>0, whereas
E*T-2(M-1)<M*T<N4, the latter difference being11M^2-9M+2>0.
Thus0<theta<1. The w and tR affine terms tie.
Their common w-value is3theta*M*Q*L>0. All OTHER anchors are checked:

* q.v=Q[-E+theta*T]<0, since theta*T<E. Thus both q-start anchors
  are below their own endpoint anchors; y.v=0 and the optimum value0
  are below w.
* r.v/Q=[E*M*L+2(M-1)(5M-2)]/N4>=2. Indeed this is equivalent to
  E>=2(8M-3)/(3M-1), which is strictly below6. Therefore
  (tR-z).v-(M-1)R=(M-1)(r.v-R)>=0. Since tR ties w, z is below w.
* For the INITIAL anchor tP=a e1 set EP=tP-w. Direct products give
  EP.(-Eq)=E*a*d/M, d=1/7, and
  EP.ER=(d/M)[a*T+(3/2)L]. Consequently

    EP.v = [E*d*L/(M*N4)]*[a*L-(3/2)T]
           +[2(M-1)*d/(M*N4)]*[a*T+(3/2)L].

  The first bracket is -5/2+1/M<0. The positive second term is at
  most13*a*d*M/N4<=13*a*d/(5M)<a/M, because N4>=5M^2 for M>=2.
  Thus EP.v<=deltaP and tP is below w. This pays the initial-anchor
  price at A=1/2; a large-A bound has not been substituted.

All eight anchors, including z and the FIRST r query, have now been checked.
The convex combination w+theta*ER of w,tR is active and
x_n=v+w+theta*ER is a subgradient of H4 at v. Thus v is the UNIQUE
ACTUAL terminal gradient, with exact normalized value

    V4=2F4(x_n)
      =Q*[E^2-(E*T-2(M-1))^2/N4],
    V4>=(1/2)E^2 Q=E^2/(28M^2).                         (11)

For the lower bound,0<E*T-2(M-1)<E*T and T^2/N4<1/2.
In particular the exact formula has no omitted return-memory anchor.
The old function's unqueried value need not be0 throughout the stated
E interval; it is irrelevant to the fixed completion and is not asserted.

The complete minimum set is conv{0,y,z,w}, minimum0. These are exactly
the active affine slopes at v=0, all other offsets being positive.
All nonzero vertices have negative first coordinate. Origin is the unique
closest initial minimizer and the TRUE radius is1. A small positive-e1
open set makes H4 purely quadratic; hence the global smoothness1 is sharp.
Convexity/C1/global1-smoothness, original prefix and optimum rows, both
terminal orientations and every minimizer row all hold for this single
actual F4 by accepted1252's necessity proof. Its Gram data are actual
Euclidean vectors. Zero steps add their actual repeated jets and do not
alter the envelope, minimum, radius or terminal value.

For general L>0 use f=L*F4 and physical steps h_i/L. The actual positions
are unchanged and normalized error remains V4. The same scaling applies
to Law1 and the small initialized example.

## 7. Scalable gain on an explicit SMALL-first-ratio physical family

For each integer N>=16 put M=2N(N+1)+1 and E=M^(3/4). These give
6<=E<=M. Let L_up be three increasing copies of Chi_N; let L_down be
three decreasing copies. Use L0=L_up, L1=L2=L3=L_down in(8).
Their actual length is n=12N+4, each ordinary mass is M-1, and
epsilon=E/M=M^(-1/4). The literal bridge ratios stay1/2,2,2 as N grows.

The exact attained value(11) gives

    V4/epsilon^2 -> (1/14)(1-4/13)=9/182.               (12)

This is an analytical limit of the printed formula, not a numerical fit.
All guards were order-independent and hold at every physical query.

### Same-word K_n screen

We first prove a uniform small ratio for the ordinary Chebyshev cuts.
In decreasing order write h_k=csc^2(k*theta), theta=pi/(2N+1).
Concavity of sin gives h_l>=h_k*(k/l)^2 for l>=k. For2<=k<=N-2,
the next two entries are at least[(2/3)^2+(2/4)^2]h_k>h_k/2.
For the last two entries h_k<=2, and1 plus following mass is>=1.
For k=1, theta<1/10, sin theta>(599/600)theta,
(600/599)^2<101/100, (2N+1)^2<(401/100)N(N+1), and pi^2>19/2
give h_1<(81002/190000)N(N+1)<(4/9)N(N+1).
Since sum Chi_N=2N(N+1)/3, its following sum is at least h_1/2.
Thus h<=2(1+following mass within its decreasing copy). Reversing gives
h<=2(1+preceding mass within its increasing copy). Three copies retain
both bounds, and max Chi_N<M follows from sin theta>=2theta/pi.

Every ordinary K cut in L0 has preceding ratio at most2 and following
mass at least M/2; hence its term<=4/M. For every later ordinary cut,
preceding mass>=3M/2, h<=M and h<=2(1+T_j). Therefore
h^2/(1+2T_j)<=2h<=2M and its cut term is at most8/(9M).
Each of the first THREE selected bridges has preceding mass bounded
below by M-1 and following mass at least M-1, hence is O(1/M), with
absolute constants. The last bridge cut is EXACTLY

    E^2/(1+P_last)^2=4epsilon^2/289,

because P_last=(17/2)M-1. The global Huber term is O(1/M).
It follows uniformly that K_n(h)/epsilon^2 ->4/289. The strict gap
9/182>4/289 is exact, so this new return value is not pointwise below K.

### Every convex quadratic on these SAME words

The accepted polynomial identity gives |P_N(t)|^2<=min(1,1/(T0*t)),
T0=(2N+1)^2=2M-1,0<t<=1. To see the first inequality directly,
put sqrt(t)=sin u and use |sin((2N+1)u)|<=(2N+1)|sin u|; the second
uses |Cheb_(2N+1)(sqrt(t))|<=1. Every quadratic in every dimension has
normalized value bounded by max_(0<=t<=1)t product(1-h_i t)^2.
Writing u=T0*t, each bridge is at most3M and each bridge factor at
most1+3u. All FOUR ordinary lists give P_N^12, so the full value is
at most T0^(-1)u(1+3u)^8 min(1,u^(-12))<=4^8/T0=O(1/M).
Thus every quadratic is o(V4). No full nonlinear scalar upper is claimed.

The named UNIVERSAL legal complex lens can also be screened. For z=alpha+i*beta
define Lens_l by alpha-|z|^2>=|beta|*cot(pi/(l+2)); its prescribed legal
polynomial-transcript payoff Gap_h(z) is the one in factdfde2fa326c908bf,
with initial point1, specified minimum0 and normalized radius1. That fact
gives Gap_h(z)<=2|z|*|Poly_n(z)|^2 and
|z|*|P_N(z)|^2<=cosh^2(2)/T0 on Lens_N. Because n>=N,
Lens_n is contained in Lens_N. If u=T0*|z|<=1, the direct product bound
|P_N(z)|<=exp(s(Chi_N)*|z|)<=exp(1/6)<cosh(2) pays the missing small-u
bound. Thus for Kc=cosh(2),
|P_N(z)|^2<=Kc^2*min(1,u^(-1)). Repeating the preceding full-word
factor calculation gives

    sup_(z in Lens_n)Gap_h(z)<=2*Kc^24*4^8/T0=O(1/M)=o(V4).

This does not upper-bound the FULL schedule-dependent complex legal region.

### A complete comparison with the homogeneous two-block/last-cut policy

This comparison includes EVERY original choice j before the LAST step,
including ordinary entries, not only the three named bridge choices.
The precisely scoped reference is: fix p=e1/M0', use ONE selected
bridge with excess A'*M0', run all remaining preterminal steps as one
constant-gradient block of mass M1'-1, and require its last proximal
height0 with homogeneous second slope. Allow every dimension and every
such second direction, and its exact maximal all-prefix completion.
This is the entire two-block class described below. Other checkpoint
policies, scalar functions, spiral witnesses or general nonlinear
multistage portfolios are not upper-bounded by this statement.

For a chosen index j, M0'=1+P_j, M1'=1+sum_(j<t<n-1)h_t,
A'=(h_j-1)/M0' if h_j>1. Write c=1/M0',a=1-c and
epsilon'=E/M1'. The zero-endpoint equation forces every homogeneous
second slope, up to rotation, to be

    q'=(-kappa,sqrt(kappa(A'-kappa)))/M1', 0<=kappa<=A'.

Its last minimum is y'=-A'e1-M1'q'. Keeping ALL its four conjugate
anchors, choose in the variational upper bound the convex combination
(1-t)y'+t*a e1. The quadratic conjugate bound gives

    V_ref(kappa)<=A'kappa*epsilon'^2
            -2t*a*(kappa*epsilon'-c)
            +t^2[(A'-kappa)(A'+2a)+a^2],    0<=t<=1.     (13)

This follows simply from max of the endpoint affine terms>=their
convex combination; the supremum over the terminal gradient is a
completed square. No external/minimax/unverified optimization result
is required. The last query has value||q'||^2/2 and gradient q'; a unit
descent step to y' gives F(y')<=0, while the retained optimum gives
F>=0, so EVERY such completion has this actual minimum. Its fixed
initial p jet forces every minimizer's first coordinate<=0 and hence
TRUE initial radius1, with origin attaining it. The smoothness upper
V_ref<=x^2 used below therefore uses the same true-distance normalization.
Put x=A'epsilon'. If a>0 and x<=A'+2a, choose
t=x/(A'+2a). The kappa coefficient cancels and EVERY direction obeys

    V_ref<=[(A'+a)/(A'+2a)]^2*x^2+2ac*x/(A'+2a).        (14)

For M0'=1 or outside this restricted test use the unconditional
forced-minimum smoothness upper V_ref<=x^2. These are upper bounds on
the declared reference class, derived here and not adopted from recall.

For the first three selected bridges, (A',M1'/M) are respectively
(1/2,7),(4/5,4),(4/11,1), with a->1. Their x=O(epsilon) and
c=O(1/M), so(14) gives their limiting upper coefficients

    9/4900,       81/4900,       900/20449.

All original ORDINARY choices are paid next. In L1 and L2, M0' is at
least3M/2 and9M/2, whereas M1' is at least6M and3M. Since h<=M,
x<=epsilon/9 and2epsilon/27 respectively; forced-minimum smoothness
gives coefficients at most1/81 and4/729. In L3, M0'>=15M/2,
A'<=2/15, and h<=2M1' by decreasing order. Thus x<=4epsilon/15;
(14) gives coefficient at most289/14400+o(1), its linear term being
O(epsilon/M). These uniform o(1) errors do not depend on which index
within those three lists was chosen.

For L0 the increasing order gives A'<=2 and M1'>=15M/2,
x<=4epsilon/15. Split its choices using the ACTUAL M0'. If
M0'>=M^(1/3), then a>=1-M^(-1/3), so(14) gives uniformly
V_ref<=epsilon^2[1/25+O(M^(-1/12))]. Indeed its quadratic coefficient
tends to(3/4)^2*(4/15)^2=1/25, and the linear term divided by
epsilon^2 is O(c/epsilon)=O(M^(-1/12)). For sufficiently large M,
x<=A'+2a, as required for this test.

If M0'<M^(1/3), the cut is in the FIRST increasing copy, with position
i<=M0' since each preceding Chebyshev step is>=1. Its angle is at
most2i/N and tends uniformly to0. For sufficiently large M, tan angle
<=2 angle, hence h_i-1<=16i^2/N^2<=80(M0')^2/M, using M<=5N^2.
It follows that x<=11epsilon*M0'/M<=11epsilon*M^(-2/3).
The unconditional V_ref<=x^2 gives o(epsilon^2), even if M0'=1.
This also covers h_j<=1 by zero payoff/no selected overstep.

Taking the maximum over ALL physical j and ALL homogeneous directions,

    limsup V_two(h)/epsilon^2 <=900/20449 <9/182.

The final strict inequality is the rational comparison
900*182=163800<184041=9*20449. Thus the small-first-ratio return
adds a uniform positive terminal coefficient beyond this ENTIRE
specified two-block policy on the same unbounded family. The ratio
margin is finite, rather than a claimed divergence. There is no claim
that it dominates the entire existing adversarial portfolio.

### Original benchmark and actual horizons

Let lambda=1+sqrt2 and r_N=floor(log_2 N). The original recurrences imply
C_(2^j-1)>=lambda^j by balanced splits, C_n nondecreasing, and
B_n>=G(C_(n-1),1)>=2C_(n-1). Since n-1>=2^(r_N+3)-1,
B_n>=lambda^(r_N+3). Meanwhile M<20*4^r_N and(11) gives

    B_n V4 >=lambda^3/[28sqrt(20)]*(lambda/2)^r_N ->infinity.

Therefore at every sufficiently large N this ACTUAL lower value exceeds
the exact U_n^ZJ; no guessed benchmark constant is used. To obtain a
family at EVERY sufficiently large integer n, set N=floor((n-4)/12)
and add the remaining0 through11 zeros at the VERY beginning. They
repeat the initialized jet, preserve the same fixed H4/F4 and value,
retain true radius1 and sharp smoothness1, and are evaluated against
the benchmark B_n at the ACTUAL padded horizon. All comparisons above
remain true at those padded physical horizons. This remains only a
prescribed-family theorem candidate, not the original all-word theorem.

## 8. Independent affine heights do NOT remove the return debt

This is an exact scoped failure of a proposed THIRD repair, not a third
attained construction and not a general impossibility theorem.

Suppose ANY fixed max-affine function has two DISTINCT slopes q,r with
independently fixed offsets o_q,o_r. Other planes may be present.
Assume actual queries realize: a q-stage ending at proximal point y;
a bridge with excess D>=0; an r-stage of ordinary mass M2-1, ending
at z=y-Dq-M2r; a bridge with excess C>=0; and a RETURNED q-stage
of ordinary mass M3-1, ending at w=z-Cr-M3q. Here M2,M3>=1.
These are actual physical updates, not reset states. Heights at y,z,w
may be arbitrary positive values, and no endpoint is assumed minimal.
The named q-plane and r-plane must actually be active at their respective
queries; representing q as a mixture of unrelated active slopes is outside
this specific fixed-two-plane face policy.

Put s=<q,r>, alpha=||q||^2-s, beta=||r||^2-s, and
ell(u)=<q-r,u>+o_q-o_r. The q/r active-plane conditions demand

    ell(y)>=0,      ell(z)<=0,       ell(w)>=0,
    ell(z)=ell(y)-D*alpha+M2*beta,
    ell(w)=ell(z)+C*beta-M3*alpha.                       (15)

In any nontrivial feasible q-r-q path alpha,beta MUST be positive.
If alpha<=0, then beta>0 because alpha+beta=||q-r||^2>0;
ell(z)>=ell(y)+M2*beta>0 contradicts its r guard. If beta<=0,
alpha>0 and ell(w)<=ell(z)-M3*alpha<0 contradicts its q guard.
Thus the first two guards in(15) give beta/alpha<=D/M2, and the last
two give beta/alpha>=M3/C. If C=0 they directly contradict one another.
Consequently every such fixed-two-plane return NECESSARILY obeys

    D*C >= M2*M3.                                      (16)

Offsets, positive affine heights, minimum-level translations or subsequent
terminal completions cannot repair a violation: they cancel from these
ORIGINAL preterminal active-plane comparisons. Other planes only add
guards. The failure already precedes any smoothness or radius issue.

Apply this to the declared first-occurrence physical prefix

    (H_j,b_j,H_j,b_(j+1),H_(j+1),b_(j+2),
        H_(j+2),b_(j+3)),                               (17)

using ONE enclosing m>=7, j+3<m, and tau=1/4 throughout. Define
lambda=1+sqrt2, beta_h=1-1/(4m), c_h=lambda*(1+1/(4m)),
a_h=beta_h/c_h, H_0=(), H_(l+1)=(H_l,1+a_h*c_h^l,H_l),
S_l=sum H_l, b_l=1+a_h*c_h^l. Induction on the mass identity gives

    1+S_l=v_h*c_h^l+(1-v_h)*2^l,  v_h=a_h/(c_h-2) in(0,1).

Indeed c_h-2>1/lambda>a_h, c_h<3. For the q-r-q assignment in(17),
D=a_h*c_h^(j+1), C=a_h*c_h^(j+2),
M2=1+S_(j+1)>v_h*c_h^(j+1),
M3=1+S_(j+2)>v_h*c_h^(j+2). Therefore

    D*C/(M2*M3)<(a_h/v_h)^2=(c_h-2)^2<1.

This contradicts(16), even with independent affine offsets and POSITIVE
endpoint heights. Thus this specific first-occurrence two-slope-return
repair cannot be instantiated on the actual inherited hierarchy. The
explicit return Law2 is legal because its declared physical data instead
give D*C/(M2*M3)=4. These two statements concern different physical
words; no ratio is silently substituted or reset.

This does not exclude interleaved visits with changing gradients within
a face/edge, more than two returning slopes, nonlinear curved regions,
different physical checkpoint selections, or other full smooth functions.
It does not give an upper bound on W_n(H_m). It is useful because it
identifies a debt unaffected by the newly proposed affine-height freedom.

## 9. What remains at theorem level

The first law replaces the old fixed -2 longitudinal slope by an explicit
ratio-dependent direction and permits unequal masses; its retained anchor
weight cap(5) is explicit. The second law gives an actual initialized
p-q-r-q return at first ratio1/2, with an exact eight-anchor terminal value,
radius1 and a genuine scalable same-word two-block-policy gain. Both are
one static function, not changed after any algorithm query.

The full target still requires a decomposition valid for EVERY physical
word and horizon, including small/zero final steps and all continuation
after early first-occurrence visits. It requires a word-independent
(1-o(1))/B_n margin, not a new growing family. No all-word comparison of
D3_sr, the return policy, K_n, spiral/circular/ray or other channels is
proved. No full-class upper improvement or unrestricted lower theorem
is claimed. The inherited-m obstacle(16) is scoped, not a reason to stop
the project and not permission to claim target completion.

## 10. Retrieval and allocation record

Skills: query-memory; construct-toy-examples; direct-proving;
construct-counterexamples; search-math-results. The active program was
smaller-ratio static multistage geometry, stage active_program, search
mode mutation/repair, missing mechanism legal actual face changes with
terminal retention. Fact searches preceded proving. Targeted gm searches
recovered old failed faces/offsets and the same-word two-block class.

Three Matlas searches were made. Their returned Moreau-composition,
unrelated interpolation and convexity hits did not supply a small-ratio
bridge or independent-offset return theorem.
Primary-source web retrieval found Taylor--Hendrickx--Glineur,
Smooth Strongly Convex Interpolation and Exact Worst-case Performance of
First-order Methods, arXiv1502.05666v6. The own exact previously downloaded
HTML/text was used, with its Lemma2 conjugacy proof and Remark2 explicit
maximum-quadratic construction read again. It provides conceptual context;
no external theorem is a black-box proof premise. Definitions are the
finite-dimensional smooth convex specialization mu0,L1. Accepted1252
supplies the actual interpolation result used here.
Source: https://arxiv.org/html/1502.05666v6.

Initial lookup of downloads/1502.05666v6.txt failed with 'No such file or
directory'. The exact own file was located at
downloads/multistage_envelope_completion_20261002/1502.05666v6.txt.
No installation or mathematical executable was used. Text/JSON metadata,
literal copying and SHA256 readback are administrative operations only.

All new mathematics remains UNVERIFIED, no fact_submit was made, and no
fact_id is claimed. This report records the explicit terminal cap caught
during direct screening instead of concealing its large-E gap. No frozen

# max65: completed full-objective discovery for the exact small-tau hierarchy

**Status: numerical discovery complete; no new fact; original minimax goal OPEN.**
All six authorized jobs completed, with five solver invocations and one
independent solver-free readback. The canonical ledger is `closed_exhausted`:
180 accounted seconds, **49.26249355098116 measured external child seconds**,
zero remaining jobs. No correction, timeout, failed mathematical job,
installation, external service, subagent, new benchmark DP, or formal
submission was used. The reservation guard1131->1311 is not measured runtime.

The finite full-PEP results are numerically Huber-like on all four specified
words. None of these solves supplies a stronger numerical objective than
the same-word Huber witness. This batch did not find the extra hard geometry
sought by the assignment. This is a scoped discovery outcome, not proof of
Huber optimality, a worst-case upper bound, or a conclusion about large m.

## 1. Exact object and prior-work distinction

For each enclosing m in {3,4,5,6}, the word is exactly

    tau=1/4, lambda=1+sqrt(2), beta_word=1-tau/m,
    c=lambda(1+tau/m), a=beta_word/c,
    H0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,
    h=H_m, n=2^m-1.

All children retain their enclosing m and tau. The numerical records save
the binary64 evaluation of these formulas and every physical step.

The historical full-PEP finding df56220f87e79c22 uses tau=1 at each of
m=3,4,5,6. This was checked from every public raw schedule formula, not
from an H label or a search match. Accepted e9a55f57461f253c uses exactly
the old m=6,tau=1 word, with c=(7/6)lambda and a=5(sqrt(2)-1)/7.
The new word differs already at its first leaf: the factor
(1-tau/m)/(1+tau/m) decreases strictly with tau, by cross multiplication.
Thus the previous experiments were not a replay of the new cases.
`historical_identity_inputs.json` stores the source identities and hashes.

Current steering: assignment/master_guidance6a33fa593da4ce7d; the later
7a4e289802c5f694 expressly leaves this max65 allocation unchanged.
Preregistration finding:79fdc29308070926. The three code files, exact saved
benchmark rows, historical identity record and preregistration were hashed
before the first mathematical job in `freeze_manifest.json`.

## 2. Full original mathematical model

Normalize L=1 and the SPECIFIED minimizer x_star=g_star=f_star=0. The
full symmetric Gram matrix G is on all formal vectors(x0,g0,...,gn).
The physical positions are x_i=x0-sum_(t<i)h_t*g_t. The program maximizes
2f_n under

    G PSD; G00=1;
    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2 <= 0
    for all distinct ordered i,j in {star,0,...,n}.

Diagonal rows are identities0=0. Every original nontrivial row is retained,
including both terminal directions and both minimizer directions. The
terminal gradient g_n is an interpolation variable, not an extra update.
There is no rank, support, scalar, quadratic, or primitive-certificate
restriction. Accepted1252b8823bb5a290 proof Sections1--3 supplies the full
interpolation equivalence and attained normalization at G00=1.

The only conditioning is the invertible transformation
M=1+sum h, D=diag(1,1/M,...,1/M), G=D Ghat D, f=fhat/M. The solver maximizes
2fhat_n and imposes M Q<=0. If A,F are the original Gram/value row maps,
the raw pair multipliers lambda are already in original units, while
q=raw_norm_multiplier/M. The full original dual quadratic matrix is
C=q E00+A^T lambda. Its original value flow must be F^T lambda=2e_n,
and scaled stationarity compares M D C D with the raw PSD multiplier.
The norm multiplier is unrestricted because normalization is equality.

| Case | n | Gram order | Original directed rows | Scalar variables | Solver iterations |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| h=(3/2) | 1 | 3 | 6 | 8 | 17 |
| H_(3,1/4) | 7 | 9 | 72 | 53 | 13 |
| H_(4,1/4) | 15 | 17 | 272 | 169 | 14 |
| H_(5,1/4) | 31 | 33 | 1056 | 593 | 20 |
| H_(6,1/4) | 63 | 65 | 4160 | 2209 | 29 |

All five solver statuses were `optimal`, using the fixed settings
CLARABEL(max_iter120,max_threads1,time_limit20,tol_gap_abs1e-9,
tol_gap_rel1e-9,tol_feas1e-9). An independent external30-second timeout
covered each entire child, including numerical imports, build, solve,
all diagnostics and raw export. No retry or fallback occurred.

Runtime: Python3.12.14, CVXPY1.9.3, CLARABEL0.11.1, NumPy2.5.3,
SciPy1.18.1; arithmetic IEEE754 binary64. Solver tolerance is not arithmetic
precision. BLAS and solver thread counts were restricted to1.

## 3. Calibration with exact symbolic reference

For h=(3/2), expand the original Q rows to obtain

 (1/4)||x0||^2-2f1+Q_(0,star)+Q_(1,star)+Q_(1,0)
        =||x0/2-g0-g1||^2.

Thus2f1<=1/4 for G00=1. The scalar function f(x)=x^2/2 with x0=1 gives
x1=-1/2 and2f1=1/4. The Huber function with threshold1/4 also attains
this value: g0=g1=1/4, x1=5/8, f1=1/8. This derivation was in the
preregistration before execution, not a post-fit interpretation.

The solver returned primal0.24999999998853667 and dual0.24999999993770056,
gap-5.0836113096863755e-11. Original G and C minimum eigenvalues were
-2.2403978076548187e-10 and-1.0956870692392237e-09; the maximum original Q
was-5.248615431163728e-11. All frozen calibration checks passed.
The numerical matrices are still not exact primal/dual certificates.

The exact original benchmark endpoints are the saved certified integers

The bound orientations are B_lo<=B_n<=B_hi,
1/B_hi<=U_n^ZJ<=1/B_lo, and B_lo*v<=B_n*v<=B_hi*v for v>=0.
Thus W/U_n^ZJ=B_n*W. Binary64 endpoint products in the saved results are

## 9. Scope and remaining interface

These are four depths m<=6. They do not enter the m>=1536 domain of the
small-tau geometric-family obstruction, so their benchmark ratios imply
neither a conflict with that result nor eventual behavior. Old accepted
tau=1 finite non-Huber examples cannot be transferred by keeping the

## DOC-003291 — project/research/small_tau_full_pep_20261001/preregistration.md

来源：D:\Danus\runtime\projects\gd-exact-profile-20260926\research\small_tau_full_pep_20261001\preregistration.md
